# Wonder Boy in Monsterland (Activision/Sega, 1989) -- SWB.PRG name map
#
# ###########################################################################
# ## LOAD BASE IS 0x3F8, *NOT* THE WORKSPACE DEFAULT 0x10000.              ##
# ###########################################################################
# SWB.PRG's entry stub copies the image from file offset 8 down to absolute
# $400 and jumps there, so the program is position-DEPENDENT at run time and
# its own absolute operands are "image offset + $3F8". Loading at 0x10000
# leaves every absolute reference dangling, which is why Ghidra recovered only
# 57 functions; at 0x3F8 it recovers 186+.
#
#   runtime = image_offset + 0x3F8          (image_offset = file_offset - 28)
#   runtime = old_ghidra_addr - 0xFC08      (converting a 0x10000-based note)
#
# Bootstrap with:
#   bash tools/headless.sh projects/wonderboy/ghidra_proj wonderboy \
#        projects/wonderboy/bin/disk1/AUTO/SWB.PRG 0x3f8 projects/wonderboy/decomp.c
# run.sh has been corrected to pass 0x3f8, so it is safe again -- but it still
# re-imports and wipes the DB, so iterate with reapply.sh.
#
# See notes/architecture.md for the evidence, the region table and the memory map.
#
# The linear listing out/wonderboy_dis.txt is regenerated at the same base with
#   python3 tools/prg_dis.py projects/wonderboy/bin/disk1/AUTO/SWB.PRG --base 0x3f8
# (its banner states the base). Any listing produced WITHOUT --base is at base 0,
# i.e. every address in it is runtime-0x3F8 and will not match this file.
#
# Untagged names were confirmed by reading the disassembled body.
# "# ctx" marks a name inferred from call context only.
#
# Every address a cmt cites is the INSTRUCTION's address, never the offset of the
# operand inside it -- so a cite can be looked up in out/wonderboy_dis.txt as it
# stands. (`move.l #imm,$a098.l` at $ff14 carries its $a098 at $ff1a; the cite is
# $ff14.)
#
# NEVER `proto` a register-returning routine: ApplyNames' `proto` forces a VOID
# return, so a directive on a routine whose result is a register would record
# something false. Put the register map in that routine's `cmt` instead.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Bootstrap / startup  (see architecture.md "Startup sequence")
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
cmt 0x3f8 GEMDOS entry. Only these first 8 bytes stay here; 0x3fa is `jmp startup_relocate_and_run` (one of the image's 3 relocations).
fn 0x217d8 startup_relocate_and_run
cmt 0x217d8 The only OS call in the whole program: GEMDOS Super($214d8+loadbase) to enter supervisor mode with a fresh SSP, then `move.w #$2700,sr` (IPL7), then copy $84f6 longwords (=$213d8 bytes) from image+8 down to absolute $400 and `jmp $400`. NOTE: this stub runs at the GEMDOS load address, so its address here ($217d8) is an artefact of the $3f8 base -- at run time it lives at basepage_text+$213e0.
fn 0x400 cold_start
cmt 0x400 First instruction executed at the final runtime address. move.w #$2700,sr; palette entry 15 := $077; jmp sys_save_tos_stack.
fn 0xe482 sys_save_tos_stack
cmt 0xe482 Stashes the TOS-supplied A7 at $f8b8 -- the only recorded way back to TOS -- then falls through to hw_init_vectors.
fn 0xf8bc hw_init_vectors
cmt 0xf8bc A7 := $80000 (top of a 512 KB ST); $70 (autovector 4 = VBL) := vbl_handler; $118 (MFP channel 6 = ACIA) := ikbd_acia_handler; MFP IERB/IMRB bit 6 set; sr := $2300 (IPL3, so VBL and ACIA get through); jmp init_ikbd.
fn 0xe48c init_ikbd
cmt 0xe48c 8-byte link in the boot chain: `bsr ikbd_disable_mouse` then `bra.w $e4e6` (boot_continue). That is its whole body -- the video/palette/screen-clear/MFP/VBL work the boot performs lives at 0xe4e6, which Ghidra folds into show_data_disk_prompt (0xe494), not into this function.
fn 0xf8f0 ikbd_disable_mouse
cmt 0xf8f0 Polls the ACIA status at $fffffc00 for TX-empty (bit 1) and writes IKBD command $12 ("disable mouse") to $fffffc02. Uses abs.w addressing ($fc00.w), which is why a longword scan for $fffffc00 does not see it.
cmt 0xe4e6 Boot continuation (Ghidra folds this into the 632-byte function at 0xe494 because show_data_disk_prompt falls through into it at $e4e4; it is a separate entry point, reached by `bra.w` from init_ikbd). video_set_lowres_50hz, clear_palette, clear_both_screens, mask MFP timer A/B (IERA/IMRA := 0), install vbl_handler, sr := $2300, arm copylock_arm_flag ($e51e), then load_resource_by_index(0 = TITLESCR.RAD -> $49800).
fn 0x4a0 game_main_loop
cmt 0x4a0 The main game loop: an unconditional `do { ... jsr flip_screen } while(1)` -- $508 is `bra.s $4a0` and there is no exit. Entered by `jmp $4a0.w` from $e708 (after the boot's first stage load) and from $f8b4. Body: two leading bsr's, then a block that runs only when $66e == 0 (jsr $e032 / $b346 / $dbc0 / $882, and $882 is `joy1_latch_edge` + the object-list pass at $8d0), then $8dfe, $7522, $8e66, $82f8, $50a, $8f02, a block gated on $a30 ($ff42), $bd8a, and finally flip_screen.
fn 0x638 game_unpause_on_key_release
cmt 0x638 54 bytes, RE-CHECKED AND STILL REJECTED (recreate/STATUS.md batch 12) -- it SPINS on memory no instruction in its own body writes. `tst.w $66e.l / beq.w $66c` returns at once unless the pause word is set, and `cmpi.b #$19,$879.l / bne.w $66c` returns unless key_last_scancode still holds the press; only then does `cmpi.b #$99,$879.l / bne.s $64e` wait for the RELEASE code ($19 | $80) that ikbd_acia_handler will store, and the payload behind it is `clr.b $879`, `clr.w $66e` and `move.b #$ff,text_request` -- i.e. unpause and dismiss the box. The pause is SET by the mirror-image arm at $60e inside $53e, which spins on the same byte and posts message $34 instead. THE SPIN IS WHY IT CANNOT BE PORTED, not the payload: nothing changes memory while an oracle run is in flight, so the checkpoint is unreachable and a wider register window or a `stop_pc` does not help. REGISTERED with terms -- trigger = a kit capability to schedule a memory poke at an instruction count or a PC; home = tools/recreate_kit, in emu.run. ONE `bsr` caller, game_main_loop's at $4a0.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Interrupt handlers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x716 vbl_handler
cmt 0x716 Vertical-blank handler (autovector 4, installed at $70 by hw_init_vectors and again at $e506). movem d0-a6; addq.w #1,vbl_counter; `lea snd_stub_00,a0 / jsr 14(a0)` = the music tick; then if floppy_idle_timer is non-zero, decrement it and call floppy_deselect_drives ($6268 -- NOT $6242) when it reaches 0. This is the only periodic tick in the program: MFP timers A and B are masked off at boot. (Earlier note said `fn 0x716` could not apply because Ghidra folded the bytes into flip_screen. That was wrong: flip_screen is [$694..$6f7]+[$702..$709] and getFunctionContaining($716) was empty. The real blocker was a bogus 4-byte `pointer` auto-defined at $714..$717 -- Ghidra read `0000 48e7` = $48e7, an in-image address -- which made disassemble() a no-op. ApplyNames now clears defined data under an `fn` address first.)
fn 0x754 ikbd_acia_handler
cmt 0x754 MFP channel 6 (ACIA) handler, installed at $118. Reads the IKBD byte from $fffffc02. $FE/$FF are the IKBD joystick-report headers: it re-vectors $118 to ikbd_joy0_byte_handler / ikbd_joy1_byte_handler so the NEXT interrupt captures the data byte, then returns. Anything else is a key scancode, decoded into the key bitmap at key_bits.
fn 0x83a ikbd_joy0_byte_handler
cmt 0x83a One-shot: stores the IKBD data byte in joy0_state, clears MFP ISRB bit 6, restores ikbd_acia_handler at $118, rte.
fn 0x858 ikbd_joy1_byte_handler
cmt 0x858 One-shot: stores the IKBD data byte in joy1_state, clears MFP ISRB bit 6, restores ikbd_acia_handler at $118, rte.
var 0x876 joy0_state
var 0x877 joy1_state
cmt 0x877 IKBD joystick-1 report byte. Bit 7 = fire. The idiom `clr.b $877 / tst.b $877 / bpl.s * / tst.b $877 / bmi.s *` that appears all over the boot path is "wait for fire pressed, then released" -- it is the "press a key" wait, NOT a keyboard flag. (Naming it from position alone would have got this wrong.)
var 0x604 key_sequence_matched_604
cmt 0x604 Word, and the game's own CHEAT ENABLE. It is raised $ffff at $5fa, on the arm the walk at $5a8 reaches when the byte cursor $606 has stepped the sequence at $608 all the way to its $ff terminator against key_last_scancode -- so a key sequence unlocks it. FIVE readers, every one a bare `tst.w`: $556 and $59e and $5d0 (which gate three more scancode actions, one of them a `bchg #3,$bd6b`) and TWO inside player_meter_empty_check, where a raised word takes the revival arm with an EMPTY medicine slot and then skips the rearm -- the meter refills on every death for ever. What the sequence SPELLS is not decoded here; the bytes at $608 are 61 30 13 1e ff.
var 0x878 key_bits
cmt 0x878 One byte, 8 key flags. ikbd_acia_handler stores the raw scancode at key_last_scancode ($879), strips bit 7 (the key-release flag) and then compares the code against the 8 bytes of key_watch_scancodes ($87a..$881), bset'ing (press) or bclr'ing (release) the matching bit of THIS byte via `lea $879,a0 / lea -1(a0),a1`. Nothing in the image ever reads $878, and key_watch_scancodes is all zeroes in the image with no writer, so no scancode can ever match: the whole keyboard path is vestigial and the game is joystick-only. (Scanned for abs.l, abs.w and pc-relative d16 references to $878/$87a -- zero of each.)
var 0x879 key_last_scancode
cmt 0x879 The last raw IKBD scancode byte, stored by ikbd_acia_handler before the release bit is stripped. Unlike key_bits this IS read: `cmpi.b #imm,$879.l` at $560, $574, $5da, $5e6, $60e, $642, $64e...
var 0x87a key_watch_scancodes
cmt 0x87a 8 bytes, the scancodes ikbd_acia_handler folds into key_bits (index = bit number). All zero in the image and never written -- see key_bits.
fn 0x682 joy1_newly_pressed
cmt 0x682 `move.b joy1_prev,d0 / move.b joy1_current,d1 / eor.b d1,d0 / and.b d1,d0 / rts` -- returns in d0 the bits that are set in joy1_current and were NOT set last frame, i.e. a rising-edge mask over the joystick byte. Note it reads $8b3/$8cf, not key_bits. REGISTER INTERFACE: no arguments, and the result is d0's LOW BYTE ONLY -- every instruction is a `.b` op, so the caller's own top 24 bits of d0 pass through untouched. Eight call sites reach it (bsr.w from $e6c/$ef0/$1220/$526e, jsr from $6fde/$dc00/$dc34/$dc6e); only the three inside FUN_0000dbc0 ($dc00/$dc34/$dc6e) are in recovered code, and those three take the result as a BYTE -- the other five are unexamined. The reconstruction reproduces the whole longword anyway (recreate/src/input.c, g_joy1_newly_pressed) because that is what the differential compares. d1 is clobbered.
fn 0x88c joy1_latch_edge
cmt 0x88c `move.b joy1_current,joy1_prev / move.b joy1_state,joy1_current` -- shifts the joystick byte one frame down the pipeline so joy1_newly_pressed can diff them. Called from $882 once per frame.
var 0x8b3 joy1_prev
cmt 0x8b3 The joystick-1 report byte as of the PREVIOUS frame. It physically occupies the NUL terminator of the "Wheres Saigon??????" filler string -- the filler is inert but this byte inside it is live.
var 0x8cf joy1_current
cmt 0x8cf The joystick-1 report byte latched for THIS frame (copied from joy1_state by joy1_latch_edge). Occupies the NUL terminator of the "I Dunno Im not Psychic!!!!!" filler string.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Video / palette  (see architecture.md "Memory map")
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x694 flip_screen
cmt 0x694 Swaps screen_front/screen_back, then writes the new front buffer's bits 16-23 and 8-15 to $ffff8201/$ffff8203. Waits on vbl_counter and clears it. Then `not.w frame_toggle` -- a 0 <-> $ffff TOGGLE, not a decrement -- and, if flash_timer is non-zero, decrements it and writes $777 (white) to $ffff8240, or 0 (black) on the frame it reaches 0. So flip_screen paints colour 0 for the duration of the flash: a visible full-screen colour-0 flash the differential oracle cannot see (palette writes are off-image). READ THE LISTING, NOT decomp.c, FOR THIS ONE: Ghidra reports `pcode error at $6f8: Unable to resolve constructor` and emits only `do {} while (vbl_counter < 1); do {} while(true);` -- the whole toggle/flash tail is dropped. Pre-existing, not caused by the $714 retype.
var 0x712 frame_toggle
cmt 0x712 Word, inverted by flip_screen every frame ($0000 <-> $ffff). Read as a boolean at $4f08, $8e40, $8ed4.
var 0x714 flash_timer
cmt 0x714 Word countdown decremented by flip_screen; while non-zero colour 0 is forced to $777, and it is cleared to black on expiry. Read at $23b8. NOT a pointer -- auto-analysis typed $714..$717 as one, which is what used to block `fn 0x716`.
fn 0x70a return_to_tos
cmt 0x70a `movea.l tos_stack_save,a7 / rts` -- restores the A7 sys_save_tos_stack captured at boot and returns to GEMDOS. No jsr/jmp/bsr/bra anywhere in the image targets it and no longword or abs.w operand holds $70a, so as shipped it is unreachable: the game never exits.
var 0x74a vbl_counter
var 0x74c screen_front
cmt 0x74c Longword = address of the buffer currently being displayed; $74d/$74e are its high/mid bytes, written straight to $ffff8201/$ffff8203. Initialised to $078000.
var 0x750 screen_back
cmt 0x750 Longword = address of the buffer being drawn into. Initialised to $070000.
fn 0xf906 video_set_lowres_50hz
cmt 0xf906 $ffff8260 := 0 (low res, 320x200x16); screen base := $070000; $ffff820a := 2 (50 Hz PAL).
fn 0xf926 clear_both_screens
cmt 0xf926 clr.l over $3f40 longwords from $70000 -- i.e. $70000..$7FD00, exactly the two 32000-byte screen buffers back to back.
fn 0xf938 copy_screen
cmt 0xf938 d0 := $1f3f then falls through to copy_longs: copies $1f40 longwords = 32000 bytes = one screen, a0 -> a1.
fn 0xf93c copy_longs
proto 0xf93c copy_longs src@A0 dst@A1 count_minus_1@D0
cmt 0xf93c move.l (a0)+,(a1)+ / dbf d0. Copies d0+1 longwords. Entered directly by callers that preset d0.
fn 0xf944 set_palette
proto 0xf944 set_palette src@A0
cmt 0xf944 24 bytes, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/stage.c, differential in recreate/test/test_stage.py) -- but its OUTPUT is not pinned and cannot be. `lea $ff8240.l,a1` then eight `move.l (a0)+,(a1)+`: 16 ST palette words from a0 into the shifter's colour registers. $ff8240 is off the loaded image, so the oracle DROPS all sixteen writes exactly as it drops every hardware write (the kit models hardware READS only -- tools/recreate_kit/include/hw.h has no hw_write8), and the routine touches no image byte at all. What a case CAN show is that both sides write nothing and that a0 comes back at src+32; WHICH words reached which colour register is untested, and recreate/STATUS.md registers the kit-side remedy (a dropped-hardware-write ledger). FOUR call sites: $f9f2 (stage_load_window, palette_table + 32 * 9(stage_start_ptr)) and $e4d0/$e540/$e588 in the boot prompt.
fn 0xe7f4 clear_palette
cmt 0xe7f4 clr.l over all 16 palette registers at $ffff8240.


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Masked planar sprite blitters, $8fce..$989c -- 12 routines behind 3 jump tables.
# The earlier region table called $8fce..$a271 DATA on "0 bsr, 0 movem". That test
# is the wrong one here: this block has 16 `rts` in 1127 words (14.2/kW, squarely in
# the CODE band) and 8 `dbf`, but it is leaf code entered only through a pointer
# table, so it contains no bsr and no movem at all. Correct split:
#   CODE $8fce..$989c (2254 B)   DATA $989c..$a271 (2517 B, 88.3% zero, 0 idioms)
# Dispatch tail of sprite_draw_pass ($8f02), at $8f9a: `lea blit_table_mid,a2 / tst.w d4 /
# bpl (skips) -> blit_table_left / cmp.w #$b0,d4 / blt (skips) -> blit_table_right /
# lsl.w #2,d2 / adda.w d2,a2 / movea.l (a2),a2 / jsr (a2)`. d4 = X, d2 = width class 0..3.
# A body draws N 16-pixel columns from N-1 SOURCE CELLS. A cell is 5 words -- an AND mask then the
# 4 bitplanes, 10 bytes -- so a row is (widthcode+1)*10 bytes. Every word is `ror.l d6,dn` as a
# LONGWORD, which pushes the pixels past the 16-pixel boundary into the high half; the low half
# draws its own column and the high half (exposed by `swap`) is `and`/`or`ed into the next one.
# So column 0 is cell 0's low half alone, column N-1 is cell N-2's high half alone, and every
# column between is a SEAM of two cells. The register window walks DOWN one register per cell:
# the masks land in d1, d0, d5, d4 for cells 0..3, the four planes following each mask upward.
# Per column: `and.w mask,(a1) / or.w plane,(a1)+` x4, the row's LAST `or.w` with no
# post-increment -- then `lea <162 - 8*columns>(a1),a1` steps one 320px 4-plane scanline.
# The lea constant gives the width: 146 -> 2 columns (32 px), 138 -> 3, 130 -> 4, 122 -> 5.
# d2 (the width code) and d5 (y) are NOT read by any body -- the width is the entry point and the
# y is already folded into a1. Reads a0/a1/d6/d7 (+d4/a5 in a prelude); clobbers d0-d5, a0, a1, d7.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x8f02 sprite_draw_pass
cmt 0x8f02 204 bytes, reconstructed in recreate/src/blit.c. THE SPRITE PASS: game_main_loop's one `jsr $8f02.l`, and the only entry to the twelve blitters at $8fce..$989c. Walks the 19 actor_screen_records; a record whose sprite word is 0 is skipped. Otherwise the sprite word indexes resource_table -- `mulu.w #$14,d0 / adda.w d0,a4`, so only the SIGN-EXTENDED LOW WORD of the 32-bit product moves the cursor: an index past 3276 wraps, and one whose low word has bit 15 set moves the cursor BACKWARDS ($248d8 +-32 KB is the reachable band). From the descriptor: source pointer -> a0, height byte `ext.w`ed -> d7 (a NEGATIVE height is representable), width-code byte, x/y offsets. y = 2(a6) + 10(a4). Below 0: the clipped rows come off d7 (`add.w d1,d7`, `bmi` skips) and the source steps past them by (widthcode+1)*10*(-y) (`muls.w` gives a NEGATIVE product and `suba.l` therefore ADDS), then y := 0. Above $9f: skipped. Otherwise d7 = min(d7, $9f - y) by `cmp.w d7,d2 / bge` -- SIGNED against a count that is never negative, so a NEGATIVE d7 is KEPT and reaches a blitter: the two-column bodies refuse it, the three wider ones run 65,536 rows. x = (a6) + 8(a4): d6 = x & $f, a1 = screen_back + $1420 + ((x & $fff0) >> 1, SIGNED) + y*160, the offset built entirely in SIXTEEN BITS (y<<5 then <<2) and only then sign-extended by `adda.w`. Table by x: negative -> blit_table_left, $b0 and up -> blit_table_right, else blit_table_mid, both tests SIGNED. `move.w d1,d5` at $8f68 is DEAD -- nothing here or in the twelve reads d5 before writing it; it survives only through the two wholly-off-screen prelude arms. a5 passes straight through and each wholly-off-LEFT sprite takes 6 off it. `lea $248d8.l,a4` is INSIDE the loop, so no record's wrapped cursor carries into the next; the walk ends at $995e.
var 0x989c blit_table_mid
cmt 0x989c 4 longwords: $9594 $95f6 $9692 $9774 -- the unclipped blitters, widths 2/3/4/5 words. Selected when 0 <= d4 < $b0. Indexed by the descriptor's width-code byte times four ($8fb6); a code outside 0..3 jsr's through whatever longword the arithmetic lands on -- the recreation refuses that input rather than reproducing it.
var 0x98ac blit_table_left
cmt 0x98ac 4 longwords: $8fce $908c $91be $936c -- the left-edge clip preludes, widths 2/3/4/5. Selected when d4 < 0. Indexed by the descriptor's width-code byte times four ($8fb6); a code outside 0..3 jsr's through whatever longword the arithmetic lands on -- the recreation refuses that input rather than reproducing it.
var 0x98bc blit_table_right
cmt 0x98bc 4 longwords: $8fe4 $90b4 $91f8 $93b6 -- the right-edge clip preludes, widths 2/3/4/5. Selected when d4 >= $b0. Indexed by the descriptor's width-code byte times four ($8fb6); a code outside 0..3 jsr's through whatever longword the arithmetic lands on -- the recreation refuses that input rather than reproducing it.
var 0x9966 blit_clip_mask
cmt 0x9966 Byte written by the eight clip preludes and tested with `btst #n,$9966` inside the clipped bodies to skip individual 16-pixel columns. The LEFTMOST column is the HIGHEST bit, so 1 draws the rightmost column alone. Complete value set: left ladders 1/3/7/$f, right ladders 3,2 (w2) 7,6,4 (w3) $f,$e,$c,8 (w4) $1f,$1e,$1c,$18,$10 (w5). 38 references (24 `move.b`, 14 `btst`), all inside $8fce..$989c.
fn 0x8fce blit_clip_left_w2
cmt 0x8fce Left-edge prelude for the 32-pixel blitter: if d4 < -16 the sprite is fully off-screen -- `subq.w #6,a5` (a 32-BIT subtract: a `.w` mnemonic on an address register is a full-width operation) and rts; otherwise blit_clip_mask := 1 and `bra.w $900a`, the shared clipped 2-word body. blit_clip_right_w2 branches to the same body. WHOSE pointer a5 is is NOT established: the sprite pass at $8f02 walks actor_screen_records in a6 and never touches a5. The four `blit_clip_*_w2..w5` pairs are all built this way, and only the LEFT ones unwind -- the right ladders' off-screen arm is a bare `rts`.
fn 0x8fe4 blit_clip_right_w2
cmt 0x8fe4 Right-edge prelude for the 32-pixel blitter: d4 >= $f0 -> fully off-screen, rts; $e0 <= d4 < $f0 -> blit_clip_mask := 2; d4 < $e0 -> blit_clip_mask := 3. Then `bra.w $900a` into the shared clipped 2-word body ($900a..$908a).
fn 0x908c blit_clip_left_w3
cmt 0x908c Left-edge prelude, 48 pixels: d4 >= -16 -> blit_clip_mask := 3; d4 >= -32 -> 1; else `subq.w #6,a5` and rts. Then `bra.w $90ec`, the shared clipped 3-word body.
fn 0x90b4 blit_clip_right_w3
cmt 0x90b4 Right-edge prelude, 48 pixels: d4 < $d0 -> blit_clip_mask := 7; < $e0 -> 6; < $f0 -> 4; else rts (no a5 unwind). Shared clipped 3-word body at $90ec..$91bc.
fn 0x91be blit_clip_left_w4
cmt 0x91be Left-edge prelude, 64 pixels: d4 >= -16 -> 7; >= -32 -> 3; >= -48 -> 1; else unwind and rts. Then `bra.w $9242`.
fn 0x91f8 blit_clip_right_w4
cmt 0x91f8 Right-edge prelude, 64 pixels: d4 < $c0 -> $f; < $d0 -> $e; < $e0 -> $c; < $f0 -> 8; else rts. Shared clipped 4-word body $9242..$936a. ODDITY: its third column's merge, `or.w d4,d3`, sits at $9324 INSIDE the arm the `btst #1` at $9312 branches to, where every other body merges before the test. A skipped column 2 therefore leaves d3 unmerged -- invisible in the pixels (that low word is only ever drawn by the arm that merges it) but visible in the d3 returned. Reproduced, not tidied.
fn 0x936c blit_clip_left_w5
cmt 0x936c Left-edge prelude, 80 pixels: d4 >= -16 -> $f; >= -32 -> 7; >= -48 -> 3; >= -64 -> 1; else unwind and rts. Then $9412 -- the -48 arm reaches it with a `bra.s` ($939e, 6072) where its three neighbours use `bra.w`, an assembler inconsistency with no behavioural effect (the -64 arm's target is nearer still and is long anyway).
fn 0x93b6 blit_clip_right_w5
cmt 0x93b6 Right-edge prelude, 80 pixels: d4 < $b0 -> $1f; < $c0 -> $1e; < $d0 -> $1c; < $e0 -> $18; < $f0 -> $10; else rts. Shared clipped 5-word body $9412..$9592.
fn 0x9594 blit_sprite_w2
cmt 0x9594 Unclipped masked blit, 2 columns = 32 pixels wide, d7+1 rows; `lea 146(a1),a1` per row. THE ONLY WIDTH THAT COUNTS ITS ROWS UP FRONT: `addq.w #1,d7 / tst.w d7 / beq / bmi` and a `dbf` back to the `tst`, so a count that is zero or negative once bumped draws nothing and it exits with d7 = 0. The clipped 2-column body at $900a has the same guard (exiting through blit_clip_right_w2's own `rts` at $9008). $95f6/$9692/$9774 and their clipped bodies have NO such guard -- a bare `dbf`, exiting with d7 = $ffff, and an entry d7 of $ffff runs 65,536 rows (measured: a0 advances 65,536 rows' worth; the dbf exits when the counter is back at $ffff, one full 16-bit cycle). Also the only width that never touches d0: five source words fit in d1-d5.
fn 0x95f6 blit_sprite_w3
cmt 0x95f6 Unclipped masked blit, 3 words = 48 pixels; `lea 138(a1),a1` per row.
fn 0x9692 blit_sprite_w4
cmt 0x9692 Unclipped masked blit, 4 words = 64 pixels; `lea 130(a1),a1` per row.
fn 0x9774 blit_sprite_w5
cmt 0x9774 Unclipped masked blit, 5 words = 80 pixels; `lea 122(a1),a1` per row. Ends at $989a, immediately before blit_table_mid. KNOWN ISSUE: this is the only function in the program that ExportDecompC cannot emit -- Ghidra's decompiler process dies on it every run (not a timeout; the 60 s limit is never reached), so decomp.c carries `// <decompile failed: ... 00009774 ...>` here. The function itself is defined and named correctly in the DB; read it in the listing instead. The other 11 blitters decompile fine, so the trigger is probably its length (296 bytes of fully unrolled straight-line and/or-blitting).

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Raw floppy: WD1772 FDC + DMA driver.  NO GEMDOS/BIOS/XBIOS IS USED.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
cmt 0x6118 --- Raw FDC/DMA driver. Real extent $5e3e..$64f0 (code) + $64f0..$6528 (state) -- NOT $6118..$64f0: disk_check_signature ($5e3e), disk_load_file ($5e7c) and its FAT helpers ($5f06, $5f76, $5fc4, $604a, $6068, $6092, $60da) all sit below $6118, and fdc_restore ($6408) calls $645e/$646c/$64ea above it. rad_depack ends at $5e3a, immediately below. Registers: $ffff8604 = FDC data/access, $ffff8606 = DMA mode/status, $ffff8609/860b/860d = DMA base address high/mid/low, $ffff8800/8802 = PSG (drive + side select via port A), $fffffa01 bit 5 = MFP GPIP FDC-done. Driving the controller directly is what lets the game read the protection sectors (IDs 11/12 on cylinders 0-4) that no GEMDOS/BIOS/XBIOS call can address. THIS function is the track-read core: $6120 computes the DMA end bound as `(11 - sector) * 512 + <DMA address counter read back>` into fdc_dma_end_track; $6164 writes a sector count of 13 to the DMA sector-count register ($ffff8606 := $90) and then FDC command $90 = Read Sector with m=1 (multiple record), so one command streams the track and fdc_wait_irq_bounded aborts it on the address bound -- i.e. 10 data sectors per track, which is exactly a disk whose sector IDs 11 and 12 are the extra protection sectors. Up to fdc_retry_count attempts; failure returns -3. (Ghidra also invented a misaligned FUN_0000615a two bytes inside the `move.w #$190,$ffff8606` at $6154 -- an artefact, ignore it.)
fn 0x624c psg_set_drive_select
proto 0x624c psg_set_drive_select bits@D0
cmt 0x624c PSG register 14 (port A) read-modify-write: keeps bits 3-7, replaces bits 0-2 with d0. Bit 0 = side select, bit 1 = drive A select*, bit 2 = drive B select* (both active LOW).
fn 0x6242 floppy_select_drive_a
cmt 0x6242 d0 := 5 = %101 -> side 1, drive A SELECTED (bit 1 low), drive B deselected; clears floppy_idle_timer; falls through to psg_set_drive_select. This SELECTS a drive at the start of a disk operation -- it is not the idle routine. Two callers, both `bsr.w`: disk_check_signature ($5e48) and disk_load_file ($5e8e). vbl_handler calls floppy_deselect_drives instead.
fn 0x6268 floppy_deselect_drives
cmt 0x6268 Saves d0/d1, d0 := 7 = %111 -> both drive-select lines high = ALL DRIVES DESELECTED (motor off), calls psg_set_drive_select, restores. This is the real idle routine: its only caller is vbl_handler ($73e) once floppy_idle_timer expires.
var 0x64f2 floppy_idle_timer
cmt 0x64f2 Frame countdown, decremented by vbl_handler. floppy_unwind_return arms it with $96 (150 frames ~ 3 s at 50 Hz); on expiry vbl_handler calls floppy_deselect_drives.
fn 0x6278 fdc_dma_read_addr
cmt 0x6278 Reads the DMA address counter back out of $ffff8609/860b/860d into fdc_dma_addr_readback.
var 0x64fc fdc_dma_addr_readback
cmt 0x64fc Where fdc_dma_read_addr parks the hardware DMA address counter, so the transfer can be resumed from exactly where it stopped. Read at $6038 and by fdc_dma_restore_readback.
fn 0x62c0 fdc_dma_restore_readback
cmt 0x62c0 `move.l fdc_dma_addr_readback,d0 / bra fdc_dma_set_addr+2` -- reprograms the DMA address registers from the READ-BACK counter, as opposed to fdc_dma_restore_addr ($62c8) which uses the last address the driver PROGRAMMED (fdc_dma_addr).
fn 0x6298 fdc_dma_set_addr
proto 0x6298 fdc_dma_set_addr addr@A0
cmt 0x6298 Stores a0 in fdc_dma_addr and writes it to $ffff8609 (bits 16-23), $ffff860b (8-15), $ffff860d (0-7).
fn 0x62b8 fdc_dma_set_addr_to_dirbuf
cmt 0x62b8 Same as fdc_dma_set_addr but with the address taken from fat_dir_buffer.
fn 0x62c8 fdc_dma_restore_addr
cmt 0x62c8 Re-writes the DMA address registers from the saved fdc_dma_addr.
fn 0x62d0 fdc_wait_irq
cmt 0x62d0 Spins up to 600000 iterations on $fffffa01 bit 5 (MFP GPIP, FDC/HDC done, active low). On timeout: fdc_deselect_regs then floppy_unwind_return.
fn 0x6308 fdc_wait_irq_bounded
cmt 0x6308 fdc_wait_irq plus a per-iteration read-back of the DMA address counter, aborting early once it reaches fdc_dma_end_transfer ($6504) or fdc_dma_end_track ($6508). This is how a transfer of unknown length is bounded. On the 600000-iteration timeout it issues fdc_force_interrupt and unwinds with -1.
var 0x6504 fdc_dma_end_transfer
cmt 0x6504 Longword: the DMA address at which the whole requested transfer is complete (programmed address + byte count). Written at $5fe4, $6016, $60ba, $60f8; tested by fdc_wait_irq_bounded.
var 0x6508 fdc_dma_end_track
cmt 0x6508 Longword: the DMA address at which the current TRACK's data run ends -- `(11 - sector) * 512` added to the live DMA counter at $6120. Tested by fdc_wait_irq_bounded so the multiple-record read stops before sector IDs 11/12.
var 0x6526 fdc_retry_count
cmt 0x6526 Word retry counter. Reloaded with 2 at $6118, $637e, $63c0 and $6488; decremented with `subq.w #1` at $61a8, $63a6, $63f8 and $64da, and the operation is abandoned when it hits 0.
fn 0x63b6 fdc_delay
cmt 0x63b6 `move.w #$2a,d7 / dbf d7,*` = 43 iterations (dbf runs until d7 = -1), not 42 -- the settle delay the WD1772 needs between selecting a register on $ffff8606 and accessing it on $ffff8604.
fn 0x645e fdc_write_data_reg
proto 0x645e fdc_write_data_reg value@D1
cmt 0x645e fdc_delay; move.w d1,$ffff8604; fdc_delay.
fn 0x646c fdc_read_data_reg
cmt 0x646c fdc_delay; move.w $ffff8604,d1; fdc_delay. The read counterpart of fdc_write_data_reg; used to fetch the WD1772 status byte after a command.
fn 0x647a fdc_force_interrupt
cmt 0x647a $ffff8606 := $80 (select the FDC command register) then writes $D0 through fdc_write_data_reg. $D0 is WD1772 Type IV **Force Interrupt** with no interrupt condition, i.e. "abort whatever command is running" -- this terminates a transfer, it does not deselect anything.
fn 0x6408 fdc_restore
cmt 0x6408 d1 := $05 = WD1772 Type I **Restore with verify** (seek to track 0, V=1, 6 ms step): $ffff8606 := $80 then fdc_write_data_reg, then spin on $fffffa01 bit 5 with a $493e0 = 300000 timeout, read the status with fdc_read_data_reg, `bclr #6,d1`, and return it in d7. On timeout: d0 := -5 and floppy_unwind_return. Called by disk_check_signature ($5e4c), disk_load_file ($5e92, $5efe) and -- crucially -- by the Copylock failure path at $f56a. Ghidra's `/* WARNING: Instruction at 0x6436 overlaps instruction at 0x6434 */` here is a decode artefact of the `bsr.w` displacement word at $6436; there is no self-modifying code in this routine.
fn 0x644e floppy_unwind_return
cmt 0x644e Common exit for the whole floppy stack. Arms floppy_idle_timer with $96, restores the stack pointer disk_load_file saved in $64f8, and returns with d0 as the status -- effectively a longjmp, so an error deep in the driver returns straight to disk_load_file's caller. Error codes seen: -2, -3, -4, -5; d0 >= 0 is success (load_resource_by_index tests with `tst.w d0 / bpl`).

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# FAT12 filesystem, implemented on top of the raw FDC driver
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x5e7c disk_load_file
proto 0x5e7c disk_load_file name@A0 dest@A1
cmt 0x5e7c Open-and-read-whole-file. a0 = 12-byte "NNNNNNNN.EEE" name, a1 = destination. Saves A7 at $64f8 for floppy_unwind_return, reads the boot sector, computes the FAT/root/data geometry, finds the directory entry, then caches dir[26] (first cluster) at fat_file_first_cluster and dir[28] (file size) at fat_file_size -- the standard FAT12 directory-entry offsets, which is what pins this as a real FAT12 reader rather than a bespoke on-disk layout.
fn 0x61b8 fat_find_dir_entry
proto 0x61b8 fat_find_dir_entry name@A0
cmt 0x61b8 Linear scan of the loaded root directory at fat_dir_buffer, fat_dir_entry_count entries, stride 32. Compares name[0..7] against entry[0..7] and name[9..11] against entry[8..10] -- it skips the '.' at index 8 of the search key, matching the space-padded 8.3 names in resource_file_table. Returns error -4 if not found.
fn 0x5f06 fat_calc_data_start
cmt 0x5f06 Decodes four BPB fields out of the boot sector in fat_dir_buffer: offset 0x10 (number of FATs) x offsets 0x16/0x17 (sectors per FAT) -> fat_area_sectors ($650c) and, x512, fat_area_bytes ($650e); offsets 0x18/0x19 (sectors per track) -> fat_sectors_per_track ($651a); offsets 0x11/0x12 (root directory entries) -> fat_dir_entry_count ($6518) and, >>4, fat_root_dir_sectors ($6512, = entries/16 since 512/32 = 16 entries per sector).
var 0x650c fat_area_sectors
var 0x650e fat_area_bytes
var 0x6512 fat_root_dir_sectors
var 0x651a fat_sectors_per_track
fn 0x5e3e disk_check_signature
cmt 0x5e3e Saves A7 for floppy_unwind_return, pushes the literal $face, floppy_select_drive_a, fdc_restore, requires the status byte to be $a6 or $a4, reads the boot sector ($6488), and compares the WORD at boot-sector offset 2 against $face; mismatch returns -2, match returns 0 via $644c. NOTE: no `jsr`/`jmp`/`bsr`/`bra` in the plaintext image targets $5e3e and no operand holds its address, so its only possible caller is the encrypted Copylock body -- see the copylock section. Neither shipped disk image carries $face at boot+2 (disk 1 has 'Lo' = $4c6f, the repaired disk 2 has $0000), so on the cracked disks this check would fail; that is consistent with it belonging to the protection rather than to the loader. # ctx
var 0x64f4 fat_dir_buffer
cmt 0x64f4 Pointer to the sector staging buffer. The boot sector, the FAT and the root directory are all read here; the cluster extent list is built at fat_dir_buffer+5120.
var 0x64f0 floppy_preamble_flag
cmt 0x64f0 Byte, $01 in the image. disk_load_file tests it at $5e86: non-zero means "do the full preamble" (select drive, restore, read boot sector, decode the BPB). Nothing in the image writes it, so the preamble always runs.
var 0x64f1 floppy_restore_after_load_flag
cmt 0x64f1 Byte, $01 in the image. disk_load_file tests it at $5ef4 and, when non-zero, issues a final fdc_restore before unwinding. Never written either.
var 0x64f8 disk_load_saved_sp
var 0x6500 fdc_dma_addr
var 0x6518 fat_dir_entry_count
var 0x651e fat_file_first_cluster
var 0x6520 fat_file_size

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# .RAD / .CRU resource cruncher (see notes/rad_depacker.asm, tools/depack_rad.py)
# Contributed by the format agent; addresses rebased from 0x10000 to 0x3F8.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x5d62 rad_depack
proto 0x5d62 rad_depack src@A0 dst@A1
cmt 0x5d62 Depacks one .RAD container. a0 = file as read off disk (12-byte header included), a1 = destination. Backwards LZ77, longword-buffered bitstream, XOR checksum. On a checksum failure it spins a $ffff-iteration delay then `moveq #$ff,d0` -- which SIGN-EXTENDS, so d0 = $ffffffff, not $000000ff. d0 is meaningless on success. The header is `packed_size.l, unpacked_size.l, seed.l`; every OVALAY*.RAD unpacks to $3ce8 = 15592 bytes.
fn 0x5e14 rad_refill_bit_buffer
cmt 0x5e14 Reads the next stream longword into d0 backwards, XORs it into the checksum d5, and rotates a fresh marker into bit 31; returns the longword's bit 0 in C.
fn 0x5e20 rad_get_bits
cmt 0x5e20 Reads d1 stream bits into d2, MSB first. `clr.w d2` only clears the low word, which is why d1 must stay <= 16.
var 0x5e3a rad_saved_a7
cmt 0x5e3a rad_depack parks the entry a7 here and restores it on the success path only.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Resource table, level sequence, and the loader that ties them together
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0xe782 load_resource_by_index
proto 0xe782 load_resource_by_index index@D0 dest@A1
cmt 0xe782 The single entry point for all disk loading. Saves d0/a1 at $e7ec/$e7f0 (for the retry path at $e7d8), computes resource_file_table + index*16, and calls disk_load_file. TWO things the earlier note omitted. (1) On success it tests copylock_arm_flag ($e7cc) and, if armed, does `jsr copylock_entry` ($e7bc) and clears the flag -- so the FIRST resource load of the boot runs the copy protection. (2) On d0 < 0 it sets colour 0 to $700 (red) and falls into $e7ce, which is `clr.b joy1_state / tst.b joy1_state / bpl.s *` = wait for FIRE, then restores d0/a1, clears colour 0 and retries the load. The error path is interactive ("disk error, press fire"), not a silent retry. Seven call sites -- see architecture.md for the full index/destination table.
var 0x2143e resource_file_table
cmt 0x2143e 40 entries x 16 bytes: a 12-byte space-padded 8.3 filename plus 4 bytes that are zero in the image AND are never written at run time (load_resource_by_index only reads the name field), so the 4 bytes are stride padding, not a runtime address slot. Index order -- this IS the ID space the level sequence uses: 0 TITLESCR.RAD, 1 CREDITS.RAD, 2..36 the 35 OVALAY*.RAD, 37 TILEDATA.RAD, 38 SPRITES.CRU, 39 DATADISK.RAD. OVALAY10.RAD and OVALAY11.RAD exist on disk 2 but are NOT in this table.
var 0x216be level_seq_index
cmt 0x216be Current index into level_seq_table; post-incremented on every stage load, cleared by the game-restart path at 0xfe4a. Its value in the shipped image is 1, not 0 -- the binary was saved after the sequence had already advanced once.
var 0x216c0 level_seq_table
cmt 0x216c0 35 entries x 8 bytes -- exactly the number of OVALAY* files in resource_file_table. Read by the dispatcher at 0xe5be. [0] = overlay ordinal; the loader does `addq.b #2,d0` on it to skip TITLESCR/CREDITS and index resource_file_table. [1] -> $e70c. [2] -> sets $e70e to $ffff/0. [3] -> $bd88, the stage/round number: sprites_cru_install subtracts 6 when it exceeds 9 and uses it to index a $40-byte-per-stage table at $e978. [4..7] are zero in every entry and have no reader I found -- UNVERIFIED.
var 0xbd88 stage_number
cmt 0xbd88 Set from level_seq_table[3] at 0xe61a. NINE operand references in the image ($1a02, $1c0e, $1c36, $5448, $bd3c, $e1d0, $e1f8, $e620, $e8a2), all abs.l and none PC-relative; $bd3c is hud_draw_stage_number's read, which draws the LOW BYTE of this word as two digits at screen_back + $7311.
fn 0xe494 show_data_disk_prompt
cmt 0xe494 clear_palette, screen base := $078000, load_resource_by_index($27 = DATADISK.RAD -> $49800), rad_depack to $77f80, set_palette from $77f84, then `clr.b joy1_state / wait for bit 7 set / wait for it clear` = press and release fire. The prompt is the DATADISK.RAD BITMAP -- str_insert_data_disk is NOT used here (nothing anywhere references it). Ghidra's function at this address is 632 bytes because the code falls through at $e4e4 into the boot continuation at $e4e6; only $e494..$e4e4 is the prompt. Entered by `jmp $e494.l` from $700e.
fn 0xe87c sprites_cru_install
cmt 0xe87c Consumes the raw SPRITES.CRU image loaded at $25298. First it copies $97a longwords from $25298 down to $24898 ($e88c) -- forward copy, dst < src, but afterwards $25298..$26e80 has been overwritten. Then it walks a bit-mask table at $e978 (stride $40 per stage, selected by stage_number with `if (n > 9) n -= 6`) and rebases the image's internal pointers with `adda.l #$252d8` / `suba.l #$248d8` -- the +64 in both constants is the .CRU header size, and the file confirms it: SPRITES.CRU is 279034 bytes with a header length field of $441ba = 278970, and 279034 - 278970 = 64. Never calls rad_depack (packed and unpacked sizes in the header are equal).
var 0xe7ec load_resource_last_index
var 0xe7f0 load_resource_last_dest
var 0xf8b8 tos_stack_save
cmt 0xf8b8 The TOS-supplied A7 stashed by sys_save_tos_stack at $e484 -- the only recorded route back to TOS. Read at $678 and by return_to_tos ($70c).

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# THE COPY PROTECTION: a Rob Northen-style trace-decrypting Copylock, $ecba..$f574
#
# This is what the earlier pass filed as "UNKNOWN, entropy 7.73, probably packed".
# It is not packed data. It is a self-decrypting protection blob that single-steps
# itself: an `illegal` instruction vectors into a handler that decrypts exactly the
# next longword before letting the CPU execute it, and re-encrypts it afterwards.
# Consequences: (a) $ed8e..$f540 CANNOT be disassembled statically at all -- no
# scan of this program for addresses, traps, hardware registers or OS calls can see
# inside it; (b) a differential harness hits `jsr copylock_entry` on the FIRST
# resource load of the boot and must either model the decryptor or stub $e7cc/$ecca.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
var 0xe7cc copylock_arm_flag
cmt 0xe7cc Word. `move.w #$ffff,$e7cc` arms it at $e51e (immediately before the TITLESCR.RAD load) and at $e6dc (immediately before the SPRITES.CRU load); load_resource_by_index tests it at $e7b2, calls copylock_entry and clears it at $e7c2. Zero in the image. Stubbing this to 0 is the cheap way to keep a harness out of the Copylock.
var 0xecba copylock_table_ptrs
cmt 0xecba Four longwords -- $f576, $f63f, $f708, $f7d1, the four screen_wipe_order tables. No plaintext instruction references this table or any of its four targets (checked abs.l, abs.w and pc-relative d16), so its reader is inside the encrypted body.
fn 0xecca copylock_entry
cmt 0xecca `moveq #0,d0 / move.l #$ffffffff,d1 / bra.s $ed46`. Called only from load_resource_by_index ($e7bc). Ghidra DID create a function here (FUN_0000ecca, ~96 bytes); the earlier pass concluded "Ghidra created no function in the region" because it filtered functions by START address and this one starts at $ecca, just below the region boundary at $ed2a, while its body reaches to about $ed8e inside it. At $ed46: push a6, `lea $ecd4(pc),a6`, `movem.l d0-a7,(a6)` (the 64-byte register save area), a6 += 64, patch the saved a6, then read the illegal-instruction vector $10, `pea $ed6a(pc)`, install that as $10, and execute `illegal` at $ed68 -- which "returns" to the very next instruction. Pure anti-trace. It then restores $10, saves vectors $8..$27 with `movem.l $8.l,d0-d7 / movem.l d0-d7,(a6)`, installs copylock_trace_decryptor at $10, primes the decrypt cursor, and executes a second `illegal` at $ed8c. Everything after that is ciphertext.
var 0xecd4 copylock_reg_save
cmt 0xecd4 96 bytes of zero-filled scratch: $ecd4..$ed13 = d0-a7 saved by `movem.l d0-a7,(a6)`, $ed14..$ed33 = exception vectors $8..$27 (bus error, address error, illegal, zero divide, CHK, TRAPV, privilege, trace) saved by `movem.l d0-d7,(a6)`. copylock_restore_state puts both back.
var 0xed3e copylock_decrypt_cursor
cmt 0xed3e Two longwords: [0] = the address of the longword currently decrypted in place, [4] = its original ciphertext. The trace handler re-encrypts [0] from [4] before decrypting the next one, so at most one instruction is ever plaintext in memory.
fn 0xee02 copylock_illegal_handler
cmt 0xee02 The smoking gun, and the whole mechanism in twenty instructions. Saves d0/a0/a1; installs $ee3c as the TRACE vector ($24) and copylock_key_check as the PRIVILEGE-VIOLATION vector ($20); then edits the exception frame ON THE STACK -- `addi.l #2,14(sp)` steps the return PC past the `illegal`, `ori.b #7,12(sp)` forces IPL7 in the saved SR so nothing can interrupt, `bchg #7,12(sp)` flips the saved T bit so the CPU returns in single-step mode. Then it takes the frame's PC into a0 and does `move.l -4(a0),d0 / not.l d0 / swap d0 / eor.l d0,(a0)` -- the next longword of the instruction stream is XORed with a key derived from the PREVIOUS (already executed) longword. rte. From here every instruction is decrypted one step ahead of the PC and re-encrypted one step behind it, by the trace handler at $ee3c which does the mirror-image `movea.l (a1),a0 / move.l 4(a1),(a0)` restore first.
fn 0xf542 copylock_restore_state
cmt 0xf542 The plaintext tail. `movem.l d0-d7,$8.l` puts exception vectors $8..$27 back, `movem.l $ecd4(pc),d0-a6` puts the entry registers back, rte.
fn 0xf552 copylock_key_check
cmt 0xf552 Installed at vector $20 (privilege violation) by copylock_illegal_handler. `cmp.l #$b472043f,d0 / beq.s $f562 / cmp.l #$8f25c241,d0 / bne.s $f564 / rts`: two accepted 32-bit key values, either of which returns. Anything else falls into the failure path at $f564 -- `jsr load_resource_by_index / jsr fdc_restore / jsr $6f9e.w / jmp $6bb8` -- and $6f9e is actor_behavior_type61 (batch 31), the four-message sequence that ends by resetting a7 and jumping to show_data_disk_prompt, so the failure path posts messages $72..$75 and restarts the machine. d0 is produced inside the encrypted body from the fuzzy protection sectors, so it can never be read statically, and it is not reproducible even dynamically (the fuzzy bytes read back differently on each revolution). This branch is STRUCTURALLY unpinnable: not merely non-deterministic, but written in code that cannot be disassembled at all.
fn 0x6bb8 actor_defeat_and_score
cmt 0x6bb8 WHAT A DEFEATED ACTOR COSTS, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/actor.c, differential in recreate/test/test_actor.py). a0 = the record that just died. Pay the packed-BCD score spawn_score_table ($6c5c) holds for its TEMPLATE's type through bcd_add_score_bd70, count the kill against that template (+1 at 6(a1)), lower the template table's live count (-1 at -6(a6)), stamp actor_free_marker $ffbe into the record's x, and -- if the spawn cursor has already been round once (-2(a6) nonzero, ANY nonzero, where $ff42's own test is `cmpi.w #$ffff`) -- re-arm the template with 30(a1) = 31(a1) = $ff. Above all of that one gated block: while state_flag_a32 is up AND a0 is EXACTLY boss_fragment_origin ($9e94, slot 3 of actor_table_a32), snd_stub+28 (snd_stop) silences the chip, boss_defeat_flag := $ffff for scene_run_frame's fragment arm to find next frame, snd_stub+56 fires SFX $19 on channel A, and hud_meter_add_clamped adds 4 -- its ONE caller in the image. Actor type $26 skips the score, the kill count and the respawn entirely. NOT copylock-specific despite being the Copylock failure path's final `jmp` target, AND NOT RARE: the image holds TWENTY-NINE control-flow sites aimed here -- that one `jmp` ($f572), four unconditional `bra.w` ($35c2, $3ff2, $5472, $54a6) and TWENTY-FOUR conditional `bne.w` ($2556, $272c, $28e2, $2ac2, $2dac, $2ea4, $3192, $3356, $3464, $370e, $3842, $39da, $3b62, $3e26, $428c, $4456, $45be, $47c2, $48dc, $4ab8, $4bf0, $4dd2, $54e8, $70aa). Every one of the twenty-four is the same shape: a per-monster state routine ends `bclr`/`btst #3,9(a0)` and branches here when the bit was set, and bit 3 of the actor's second flag byte is the DEFEATED bit $6b46 raises -- so this routine runs on EVERY monster death, which also makes the $6cdc respawn continuation it branches to hot code rather than a corner. (An earlier revision of this plate counted only the four `bra.w`.) BODY = 164 bytes ($6bb8..$6c5b), not the 290 Ghidra reports -- that figure folds in the 128-byte score table at $6c5c, and stops 2 bytes short of its end. The `ble.w $6cdc` at $6c34 LEAVES the routine for actor_respawn_as_new_kind ($6cdc..$6d59), which batch 22 reconstructed -- so there is no longer a checkpoint here and both exits run to an `rts`; the port still reports WHICH exit it took. NOTE that $6c38, the retire tail, has THREE entrances, not two: the unscored `beq` at $6c10, this `ble` falling through, and the continuation's `bmi.w $6c38` at $6d0a on a negative forced kind -- so a defeat under the kill limit can still free the slot. THE EXTEND BIT THIS ROUTINE HANDS THE SCORE, and it is PINNED rather than an edge: `lsl.w #2,d2` at $6c20 pushes bits 15 and 14 of the spawn type out of the word and the LAST one out -- bit 14 -- stays in X, which bcd_add_score_bd70's first `abcd` folds into the lowest digit pair, so a type with bit 14 set pays ONE EXTRA UNIT. The bit is produced INSIDE this routine, so it is nothing to do with the harness's entry CCR: recreate/src/actor.c THREADS it (batch 33 phase B) and recreate/test/test_actor.py drives both answers as ordinary differential rows (SCORE_EXTEND_TYPES = $4000, $ffff). An earlier revision of this plate called it an unpinned edge and said every shipped type has bit 14 clear; the battery was REFUSING such a seed, the refusal was hiding a live divergence (seeded, the port that passed a zero is red at bcd_score_bd70+3), and the shipped-types reading was uncheckable anyway -- the template table has NO shipped bytes, it is loaded from disk.
fn 0x6cdc actor_respawn_as_new_kind
cmt 0x6cdc 126 bytes ($6cdc..$6d59), RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/actor.c, differential in recreate/test/test_actor.py). WHAT A RESPAWNED SLOT COMES BACK AS. Reached from ONE place -- actor_defeat_and_score's `ble.w` at $6c34 -- and ending in the `rts` that returns to THAT routine's caller, so it is a continuation rather than a subroutine; a0 is still the record that died and a1 its 32-byte spawn template. `cmpi.w #$2,6(a1) / beq` splits on the kill count the `addq` just raised: EXACTLY spawn_kill_respawn_limit takes 10(a1) and, if that word is zero, stage_random_kind8 ($e1f0, the 8-wide table); anything else takes 8(a1) and stage_random_kind32 ($e1c8, the 32-wide one). So the two forced-kind fields are per-template overrides and a zero means "draw one". `tst.w d0 / bmi.w $6c38` then sends a NEGATIVE forced kind into the retire tail instead -- a drawn one can never be negative, because both draws close with `andi.l #$1f`. It rewrites NINE fields of the record, not the six an earlier revision of this plate (written before the port) counted: 20(a0) = the kind's low byte; 8(a0) `bset #0 / bset #1 / bclr #2`, which is actor_start_motion_at_speed's three writes in another order; 10(a0) = $a; 11(a0) = $8; 12(a0) = $c8; 30(a0) = $8; then `lea $1044c.l,a2 / lsl.w #4,d0 / lea 0(a2,d0.w),a2` and `move.w (a2)+,4(a0)` / `move.w (a2)+,6(a0)` for the record's new type and sprite out of the 16-byte rows at actor_kind_table, and finally `move.l #$40006,14(a0)` -- half width 4, size second word 6, spelt inline where $ffe4 reads them out of actor_size_table. THE TABLE INDEX IS BOUNDED BY ITS OWN ARITHMETIC AND NOTHING ELSE: the `bmi` has refused a negative kind, `lsl.w #4` scales inside the WORD (a kind at or above $1000 wraps) and the `lea` SIGN-EXTENDS that word, so the read lands in [$1044c-$8000, $1044c+$7ff0] -- inside the image at both ends, which is why the reconstruction needs no bus mask here where recreate/src/rng.c does. ALL TWENTY-ONE of the table's first rows carry type $26 = actor_type_unscored, so a slot that has respawned once pays no score, counts no kill and never respawns again when it next dies.
cmt 0xf564 The Copylock failure path. It is also the mystery "seventh call site" of load_resource_by_index that the earlier pass could not resolve: d0/a1 are set up inside the encrypted body, so the file index and destination are unreadable.
var 0xf576 screen_wipe_order_even_odd
cmt 0xf576 201 bytes: a permutation of 0..199 terminated by $ff -- all 100 even lines top-down, then all 100 odd lines bottom-up. One of four scanline orders reached through copylock_table_ptrs. **This block is why the region measured entropy 7.73 and got filed as "packed".** A permutation of 0..199 is maximally entropic BY CONSTRUCTION -- every byte value distinct -- so "high entropy therefore compressed" was never a valid inference here. Measured alone, $f576..$f89e is 7.65 bits/byte and it is a plaintext lookup table.
var 0xf63f screen_wipe_order_converge
cmt 0xf63f 201 bytes, permutation of 0..199 + $ff: 0,199,2,197,4,195... -- top and bottom converging on the middle.
var 0xf708 screen_wipe_order_quarters
cmt 0xf708 201 bytes, permutation of 0..199 + $ff: every 4th line in four descending/ascending passes (0,4,8..196, 197,193..1, 2,6..198, 199,195..3).
var 0xf7d1 screen_wipe_order_bands
cmt 0xf7d1 201 bytes, permutation of 0..199 + $ff: four interleaved 50-line bands (0,50,100,150, 2,52,102,152, ...).
var 0xf89a copylock_flag_a
cmt 0xf89a Word, `clr.w $f89a` at $fb8a. Written by plaintext code and read by nothing in the plaintext image -- like copylock_flag_b, its reader must be in the encrypted body.
var 0xf89c copylock_flag_b
cmt 0xf89c A BYTE, not a word: `st $f89c` at $fb90 is a Scc, which is byte-sized at every addressing mode, so $f89d is NOT written -- corrected when stage_publish_scroll_state was reconstructed, and pinned there by a case that requires $f89d to be absent from the write set. Same story as copylock_flag_a otherwise: written by plaintext code, read by nothing in the plaintext image. Both writes are inside stage_publish_scroll_state and are each address's ONLY operand site, which is the other side of recreate/PORTABILITY.md 6.1's "knowingly unpinned" list.

# Strings

# A scan of every abs.l, abs.w and pc-relative-d16 operand in the image found ZERO
# references to $412, $46e, $46f, $472, $8a0 or $8b4. All of these are dead text.
var 0x412 str_psygnosis_taunt
cmt 0x412 "If you keep running out, you soon run out of places to run!  Psygnosis!!   And so do I!!!!!!" -- inert filler in the hole after cold_start; nothing references it.
var 0x46e str_insert_data_disk
cmt 0x46e The run starts HERE, not at $46f: $46e/$46f are `1b 59` = VT52 ESC 'Y' (position cursor) and $470/$471 are the row/column bytes '+' ' '; the text "Please insert data disk and press a key." proper begins at $472 and is NUL-terminated at $49a. The VT52 prefix means it was written for GEMDOS Cconws -- and SWB.PRG makes no GEMDOS call but Super(). It is a leftover from a pre-raw-FDC version, exactly like the Psygnosis taunt. show_data_disk_prompt displays the DATADISK.RAD bitmap instead and never touches this string.
var 0x8a0 str_wheres_saigon
cmt 0x8a0 "Wheres Saigon??????", NUL at $8b3.
var 0x8b4 str_not_psychic
cmt 0x8b4 "I Dunno Im not Psychic!!!!!", NUL at $8cf. Developer filler in a hole between routines -- but see joy1_prev/joy1_current: the two NUL terminators at $8b3 and $8cf are live input variables, so this hole is not entirely dead.
var 0xa271 dialogue_text_block
cmt 0xa271 The in-game dialogue/shop/hint strings, each prefixed by a one-byte layout code ('<', '2', ...). Runs to about $b346. No direct reference either, but unlike the filler strings above that proves nothing: this block must be indexed through a table, and I did not trace the indexer. Several of the raw `4e4x` trap-opcode byte hits in this program are ASCII pairs inside this block ("NI", "NG") -- none of them are instructions, and the block also scores 78 false `bsr` hits ($61xx = 'a' + a byte), which is why idiom density alone cannot classify a region.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Sound module (self-contained, PC-relative, runtime $17adc..$1abc8; full map with
# evidence in notes/sound_module_recon.md -- every body below was read via objdump)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
var 0x1738c snd_module_base
cmt 0x1738c The address every routine in the module reaches its state through: `lea $1738c(pc),a3` opens snd_trigger_effect and its siblings, and every state/table offset in this block is a3-relative off it. WB_SND_MODULE_BASE in recreate/include/wonderboy.h.
fn 0x17adc snd_stub_00
cmt 0x17adc The sound module's entry vector: a run of movem-save/restore stubs at $17adc..$17b39, called as `lea snd_stub_00,a0 / jsr N(a0)` from 25 sites ($58e .. $f9fc) -- and stub +0 is also called directly, which is why this address must be an `fn`, not a `var`. A `var` here silently RENAMED Ghidra's real 14-byte function and made decomp.c print `snd_stub_table();` in three places as though a table were being called. Map, all read from the bodies (notes/sound_module_recon.md): +0 -> snd_play_song ($17b3a, d0.b = song id 0..16); +14 -> snd_music_tick (the one vbl_handler invokes with `jsr 14(a0)`); +28 -> snd_stop ($17f24, resumable); +42 -> snd_resume ($17f82); +56 -> snd_call_trigger_effect ($17b14) -> snd_trigger_effect ($1a48a, d0.b = SFX id 0..25, d1.b = channel); +70 -> snd_stop_all_sfx ($1aaea); +84 -> snd_start_fadeout ($17f92). Not "seven 14-byte stubs": the +84 stub is 10 bytes, and the register sets differ -- +42 saves only a3, +70 only d1/d2, the rest d0-a6. Everything below is reached only through this table, which is why Ghidra's flow analysis never entered most of the module. The game polls `378(stub)` = snd_engine_enabled ($17c56) at $192c to detect "song finished".
fn 0x17b14 snd_call_trigger_effect
cmt 0x17b14 Stub +56, 14 bytes, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py): `movem.l d0-a6,-(a7) / bsr.w snd_trigger_effect / movem.l (a7)+,d0-a6 / rts`. The two masks are the same fifteen registers written in the two orders the 68000 uses ($fffe pre-decrement, $7fff post-increment), so EVERY register but a7 is preserved across the call -- which is what $17fd4 (pattern opcode $97) depends on and what the game's own `lea $17adc.l,a1 / jsr 56(a1)` sites get for free. Memory cannot show that, so it is pinned by entering the oracle with a distinct value in all fifteen and requiring all fifteen back.
fn 0x17b3a snd_play_song
cmt 0x17b3a Stub +0, 140 bytes, d0.b = song id 0..16. RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py). Calls snd_stop first -- through the STUB at $17af8 (+28), whose `movem` pair is what carries the song id in d0 across it, so the four PSG accesses a start makes are snd_stop's. Then: global transpose ($17c64) := 0; reads the 8-byte record at snd_song_directory + 8*id (byte +1 = song speed -> $17c58, copied on to $17c59 FROM $17c58 by a pc-relative read, not from the directory again; words +2/+4/+6 = per-channel sequence-table offsets, a3-relative); resets the three 48-byte music channel states at $17bc6/$17bf6/$17c26 (duration 1, flags/porta-control/yield/detune cleared, arpeggio base AND cursor := $1844e, sequence offset := the directory word, sequence index := 2, pattern cursor := a3 + the word at a3 + that offset, i.e. the sequence's entry 0); `clr.l` over the four channel-lock bytes ($17c5e); master volume := 15; fade rate ($17c65) := 0; speed accumulator ($17c6a) := $ff via `st`, so the first tick's speed add CARRIES and the song's first row steps at once; and `st` over song-loaded ($17c63) and ENGINE ENABLED ($17c56). PLATE CORRECTIONS (bytes): the earlier text said "clears the channel HW-lock flags" for `clr.l 2258(a3)` (right address, and it is four bytes) but omitted the `st 2270(a3)` at $17bb8 entirely. NO BOUNDS CHECK: `ext.w d0 / mulu.w #$8,d0` makes the id a SIGNED byte scaled into a WORD index that the indexed operands sign-extend, so an id of $80..$ff reads the directory BACKWARDS. Must run before ticking -- snd_music_tick bails at $17ca0/$17ca6 unless $17c56 or an SFX flag is set. It is also what first DEFINES the mutable band at $17bc6..$17c71, which the .PRG ships dirty.
fn 0x17c74 snd_music_tick
cmt 0x17c74 Per-VBL replay tick, stub +14: SFX ENGINE FIRST, then the music channels, then the PSG output (the order is verified below and an earlier revision of this plate had it backwards). Opens with the tempo selector: btst #7 of $00fffa01 (MFP GPIP mono-monitor detect) and btst #1 of $00ff820a (shifter sync) pick the tick-drop value at $17c6e -- 0 (colour 50 Hz), $2b (60 Hz: drop 43/256 ticks), $48 (mono: drop 72/256); the drop accumulator at $17c6f skips the WHOLE tick on carry, so it is a fractional tick-dropper, not a tempo scaler. An emulator whose hardware reads return 0 lands on the MONO branch and plays ~28% slow (TRUE AS HISTORY, and RESOLVED in batch 25: the kit's Phase 7 seeded hardware read model makes both bytes DECLARED case inputs, so a differential whose case names no machine is refused rather than served that 0 -- see the batch-25 note at the end of this plate). All PSG output is $17e34..$17f20: writes regs 0-6 and 8-10 from the shadow file at $18352, then a read-modify-write of mixer reg 7 -- the READ of $ff8800 at $17f08 (and $17f3e in snd_psg_silence) must be modelled as reg-7 read-back; a zero read clears bits 6/7, the port A/B direction bits the FDC's psg_set_drive_select depends on. Requires supervisor mode (`move.w #$2700,sr` around the PSG windows). Song speed is a fractional row-rate byte added to the accumulator at $17c6a each tick; channels step on carry. CALLEE ORDER, verified against the bytes in batch 23 (an earlier revision of this plate had it backwards): the SFX engine runs FIRST -- `bsr.w snd_sfx_tick` at $17cb6, before a single music byte is read -- then the fade countdown, then snd_channel_step ($18106) three times on carry of the speed accumulator, then snd_channel_period_and_volume ($18208) three times, and only then the SFX mix override ($17d90..$17e2e) and the PSG output block. Callees: $1a5da, $18106, $18208 and, through the two `bra.w`s below, snd_stop. NON-LOCAL EXIT: the fade path `beq.w $18016` at $17ccc/$17cda leaves the tick's body for good -- $18016 clears "song loaded" ($17c63) and `bra.w`s to stub +28, so the stop's `rts` returns to the TICK's caller and the rest of the tick never runs. Pattern opcode $8e enters the same tail two bytes earlier at $18014, whose `addq.l #4,sp` UNWINDS snd_channel_step's frame first -- so a song that ends mid-row abandons both the channel step and the tick. Whatever ports the tick body ($17c74) or the channel step ($18106) has to model that exit. BATCH 24: the body below the head is RECONSTRUCTED as fn 0x17ca0 snd_music_tick_body -- see its plate. BATCH 25: the 44-byte HEAD ($17c74..$17c9f) is RECONSTRUCTED too (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py), so this routine is WHOLE and head+body tile $17c74..$17f23 with nothing between them -- the sound module's last unported bytes. Read off the bytes, and the POLARITY is the opposite of both register names: `move.b #0,2274(a3)` stores the 50 Hz value FIRST, then `btst #7,$fffa01 / bne.s $17c90` skips the mono store because GPIP bit 7 is the mono-detect line ACTIVE LOW (SET = COLOUR), and `btst #1,$ff820a / bne.s $17ca0` skips the 60 Hz store because sync bit 1 SET = 50 Hz. The mono arm `bra.s`es over the sync test, so a mono machine NEVER READS $ff820a -- an ordering no image byte can show and which the differential's read stream is the only witness to. This is also the routine that ESTABLISHES a3 (`lea $1738c(pc),a3`, its first instruction): $17ca0, $18106, $18208 and $1aaca all inherit the base from here, so a differential entering $17c74 seeds no a3. The capture profile $fffa01=$b0 / $ff820a=$02 is a 50 Hz COLOUR ST (bit 7 set = colour; bits 5/4 = the FDC and ACIA lines, active low and so set because idle), which is why recreate/out/audio played at the right speed. STILL NOT PINNED, and it is the model's limit rather than this port's: that a real ST serves those bytes.
fn 0x17ca0 snd_music_tick_body
cmt 0x17ca0 644 bytes ($17ca0..$17f23), RECONSTRUCTED in batch 24 (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py): snd_music_tick BELOW its 44-byte tempo selector. The head ($17c74..$17c9f) was NOT ported when this was written; BATCH 25 ported it (see cmt 0x17c74) and the two now tile $17c74..$17f23. This entry stays a function of its own because the split is what lets a case reach all three drop values with no machine to declare -- it pokes $17c6e (0 / $2b / $48) and enters here, while a case entering $17c74 declares the machine with `hw_seed=` and runs the whole tick. Body in order: the GATE (engine enabled, or any of the FOUR bytes the `tst.l 2254(a3)` covers -- the pad byte at $17c5d keeps the tick alive too); the DROP accumulator ($17c6e added into $17c6f, whose carry abandons the WHOLE tick, SFX engine and chip write included); `bsr.w snd_sfx_tick`; a RE-READ of the engine flag (snd_sfx_tick's end-of-effect arm can have cleared it) which on zero jumps straight to the SFX mixdown; the FADE ($17cc2 -- rate 0 disables it, a master volume already 0 ends the song, else the countdown spends a tick and the volume a countdown); $17ce4 noise period out := its base; the ROW step ($17cea, song speed accumulated in $17c6a and all three channels stepped on its carry, each `bsr.w $18106`); $17d0c master volume &= 15 and three `bsr.w $18208`, each publishing a 12-bit period through the scratch word $17c68 into PSG shadow regs 0/1, 2/3, 4/5 and a volume into 8/9/10 reduced by the ATTENUATION (master volume EOR 15), the borrow clamping at silence; $17d8a shadow reg 6 := $17c6c; the SFX MIXDOWN ($17d90..$17e32); and the PSG OUTPUT ($17e34..$17f20). TWO NON-LOCAL EXITS end the song: the fade's two `beq.w $18016` and opcode $8e's tail through $18014 -- both clear $17c63 and run the stop chain, whose `rts` returns to THIS routine's caller, so the rest of the tick never runs. THE MIXDOWN'S ARMS ARE NOT THREE OF A KIND: channel A's is 54 bytes and B's and C's 52, because A alone carries a `bmi.w $17c72` (a NEGATIVE SFX-active flag abandons the tick before the mixer mask and before the chip is written -- exactly as snd_sfx_tick's own double test of the same byte does) and B and C alone carry the `rol.b #1`/`#2` that rotates the descriptor's mixer byte into their bit positions. The three `ori.b` immediates $09/$12/$24 are one constant rotated by the channel number, and are also the three records' constant field +47. The PSG block writes regs 0-6 and 8-10 from $18352, SKIPPING any channel whose $17c5e+n lock byte is set (and reg 6 unless all FOUR lock bytes are clear -- a `tst.l`, because one noise generator is shared), then read-modify-writes mixer reg 7: the READ at $17f08 must be modelled (a case declares it with `psg_seed`), and only the bits of the UNLOCKED channels are taken from the shadow, so a locked channel's enables survive as the chip held them.
fn 0x18106 snd_channel_step
cmt 0x18106 258 bytes ($18106..$18207), RECONSTRUCTED in batch 24 (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py) together with the 306 bytes of opcode handlers BELOW it ($17fd4..$18105) -- the two are one flow graph, since this routine's last instruction is the `jmp` that enters a handler and every handler but one `bra`s back INTO this body. a0 = one 48-byte music channel record, d1 = the channel opcode $97 hands the SFX trigger (and which this routine never sets -- see cmt 0x17fd4). INHERITS a3: its first instruction is `subq.b #1,27(a0)`, not a `lea`, so it is the third internal `bsr` target after $1aaca and $18208 and a differential entering it must seed the module base. Counts the note-duration byte (+27) down and, while it is still turning, applies the pitch slide (+/-1 on the note, bit 3 of the flags enabling it and bit 7 choosing the direction) and returns. On expiry it clears the flags, picks the pattern cursor up out of +2 and reads bytes until one ends the row. RANGES, corrected from the bytes: the `cmp.b #$b8,d0 / bcs` at $181a6 sends $00..$7f (a note, via the `bmi` above it) and $80..$b7 -- NOT $80..$97 -- to the jump table at $17fa4, which has only 24 entries, so $98..$b7 index PAST it into the handlers' own code and `jmp` through a word of instruction stream; $b8..$bf and $c0..$cf both reach the ARPEGGIO arm ($b8..$bf with a decoded index of $f8..$ff, i.e. entry 248..255 of a table that holds SIXTEEN); $d0..$df an instrument; $e0..$ff a duration 1..32. Its LAST instruction is the `jmp`, and opcode $8e ($18014) `addq.l #4,sp`s this routine's own return address away -- see cmt 0x17c74. Nothing in the shipped data produces an $98..$b7 byte (all 106 patterns decode with zero out-of-range opcodes), and the reconstruction reproduces the 24 the table holds and nothing else.
fn 0x18208 snd_channel_period_and_volume
cmt 0x18208 330 bytes ($18208..$18351), RECONSTRUCTED in batch 23 (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py). a0 = a 48-byte music channel record ($17bc6/$17bf6/$17c26); out d0 = the note period in its LOW WORD (every write to d0 is a `.w`/`.b`, so the caller's high word survives) and d1 = the channel's volume (+30) in its low byte over the portamento's leftover scratch in its second, the rest zero (`moveq #0,d1`). Called three times by snd_music_tick at $17d16/$17d3e/$17d66 and by nothing else. Six arms in body order: ENVELOPE (+31 countdown, +26 reload, +32 cursor -- the NEXT byte is peeked through `move.b 1(a2),d0` and a negative one holds +40 with the cursor unmoved); note = +29 + snd_global_transpose + detune +46; ARPEGGIO byte through +20 with `bclr #7` as the terminator, which restores +16 as the cursor rather than +16 plus one, so the terminating entry is itself played; the period-table lookup, whose index is `add.b d0,d0` -- (note*2) & $ff, so a note from 96 up ALIASES past snd_note_period_table's 96 entries into snd_arpeggio_ptr_table and a note from 128 up wraps to the table's start; PORTAMENTO (+41 limit, doubled for the compare and halved again for the subtraction; +42 step; +43 current; +44 control, bit 6 enable, bit 5 which end it runs to, bit 7 = advance only on the ticks whose flag bit 0 is clear), whose offset is DOUBLED once per octave the note sits below BYTE index 96 of snd_note_period_table -- i.e. below note 48, entry 48 -- because the period table halves once per octave (`addi.b #-96,d5` on the doubled note; WB_SND_PORTA_OCTAVE_BIAS in recreate/include/wonderboy.h); and VIBRATO (+25 speed, +24 signed depth, +14 accumulator) -- whose speed byte is a DELAY, not a divider, because the tick that decrements it to zero does not store the zero back, so it stays at its last value and the accumulator steps every tick thereafter. It is not a pure function of its record: it also publishes snd_noise_period_out ($17c6c) and merges this channel's constant mixer mask (+47) into snd_psg_shadow's mixer byte ($18359) with `eor/and/eor`, and a channel whose +45 has bit 7 set (yielded to the SFX engine) gives up its three noise enables for that tick and has the bit cleared.
fn 0x17f24 snd_stop
cmt 0x17f24 Stub +28, 12 bytes, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py). Clears ENGINE ENABLED ($17c56) with an `sf` and TAIL-JUMPS (`bra.w`, not a call) to snd_stop_all_sfx -> snd_psg_silence. Does NOT clear "song loaded" ($17c63), so snd_resume can restart the same song -- this is pause, not unload.
fn 0x17f30 snd_psg_silence
cmt 0x17f30 Tail of snd_stop/snd_stop_all_sfx (entered by bra.w, not a stub), 82 bytes, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py) -- THE FIRST PORTED CODE IN THIS PROJECT THAT DRIVES THE CHIP. `move sr,d2 / move #$2700,sr` saves the SR and masks IRQs so the tick cannot write the chip mid-sequence, `move d2,sr` restores it; the reconstruction reproduces neither (C has no SR, and the oracle enters every run at $2700 already), so the save is observable only as the outgoing d2, which the battery asserts. Then a read-modify-write of PSG mixer reg 7 with `ori.b #$3f` (all six tone/noise enables off -- the READ at $17f3e preserves bits 6/7, the port A/B I/O DIRECTION lines the floppy drive-select depends on), then zeroes volume regs 8/9/10. It writes NO image byte at all: its whole output is five PSG accesses, which is why the port takes no image argument and why it became portable only with the kit's seeded PSG read model (TRAP_MODEL.md Phase 6) -- a case declares reg 7 with `psg_seed` and both cores are served the same byte. `move.b $ff8800,d1` and `ori.b` are BYTE ops, so d1's upper three bytes are the caller's.
fn 0x17f82 snd_resume
cmt 0x17f82 Stub +42: re-sets ENGINE ENABLED only if "song loaded and not ended" ($17c63) is still set.
fn 0x17f92 snd_start_fadeout
cmt 0x17f92 Stub +84: fade countdown := 10, fade rate := 10 (hardcoded). The tick then decrements master volume every 10 sub-ticks ($17cc2..$17ce2) and self-stops via $18016 at volume 0.
cmt 0x17fa4 Pattern-opcode jump table: 24 a3-relative WORDS, opcodes $80..$97, reached by `andi.w #$7f,d0 / add.w d0,d0 / movea.w 0(a2,d0.w),a2 / jmp (a3,a2.w)` at $181f6 -- and NOT bounds-checked, so a pattern byte of $98..$b7 indexes past it (cmt 0x18106). 24 entries, 23 distinct targets: $83 and $8d share one handler. THE TABLE, opcode -> handler -> role -> reached by the shipped data (a walk of all 106 patterns of all 17 songs, every channel and every sequence entry; the counts are that walk's): $80 $180ec rest, volume:=0 and close the row -- YES x658. $81 $180de portamento off (+44:=0) -- YES x4. $82 $180d6 portamento on (+44:=$40) -- YES x2. $83 $180e4 set flag bit 1 -- no. $84 $1809c vibrato on, 2 operands: depth +24, speed +25, accumulator +14 cleared, flag bit 2 set -- no. $85 $180c2 pitch slide on (flag bit 3) -- no. $86 $180bc pitch slide UP: sets flag bit 7 and FALLS INTO $85's `bset` -- no. $87 $1801e advance to the next pattern in the sequence; a 0000 terminator restarts at entry 0 with the index left at 2. THE ENTRY IS RE-READ AT $18036 AND THE NEW INDEX STORED AT $1803c, in that order, so a sequence table that names the record's own index word resolves the OLD index -- reachable only from a seeded table: the 51 shipped sequence tables span $18508..$1a42a in seventeen disjoint runs, INTERLEAVED with the pattern data rather than gathered in one band -- only song 0's three sit in the 28 bytes at $18508 -- and the lowest of them is $18508, so none of them can name a byte of the music channel records at $17bc6..$17c55. Pinned by a case that solves for the aliasing offset. YES x95. $88 $180ca portamento step +42, then ONE byte into BOTH the limit +41 and the current +43, then FALLS INTO $82 -- YES x48. $89 $180b0 global transpose ($17c64), 1 operand -- YES x5. $8a $18064 merge this channel's NOISE enables ($38) into the routing mask and clear +1 -- YES x51. $8b $18044 merge its TONE enables ($07) into the routing mask and set +1 to $ff -- no. $8c $18084 drop this channel out of the routing mask (`and` with its complement) and set +1 to $ff -- no. $8d $180e4 the SAME handler as $83, through a second table entry -- no. $8e $18014 END OF SONG: `addq.l #4,sp` unwinds snd_channel_step's frame, then $18016 `sf`s $17c63 and `bra.w`s stub +28 -- YES x11. $8f $180f2 run the envelope (flag bit 5) and close the row -- YES x88. $90 $180fa offer the channel to the SFX engine (+45:=$ff) -- no. $91 $18100 take it back (+45:=0) -- no. $92 $180b6 detune +46, 1 operand -- YES x16. $93 $18002 new sequence-table offset as TWO BYTES over +6/+7, index +10 := 0, 2 operands -- YES x3. $94 $17ff4 song speed $17c58 and its copy $17c59, 1 operand -- no. $95 $17fe6 fade rate $17c65 and countdown $17c66, 1 operand -- no. $96 $17fde master volume $17c57, 1 operand -- no. $97 $17fd4 trigger an SFX, 1 operand -- no, and it CANNOT be: see cmt 0x17fd4. $87 and $8e between them account for all 106 patterns exactly (95 + 11), since every pattern ends in one or the other -- which is the census's own self-proof. Eleven of the 24 are reached and thirteen are not; the thirteen are pinned from seeded streams and recreate/test/test_sound.py says so per opcode. Arpeggio bytes: only $cf, twice. Instruments: $d0, $d1 and $d4..$de -- THIRTEEN distinct values, not the fifteen recreate/notes/sound_module_recon.md said ($d2, $d3 and $df never occur; the note stated the RANGE).
