cmt 0x17fd4 Opcode $97, and the module's ONE LATENT DEFECT, reproduced in batch 24 and not fixed: `move.b (a1)+,d0 / bsr.w $17b14 / bra.w $18116` sets the SFX id in d0 and NEVER SETS d1, which is the channel snd_trigger_effect dispatches on -- so the effect would land on whatever channel the SFX tick left in d1 earlier in the same snd_music_tick. It is latent only: $97 occurs ZERO times in all 106 patterns, so nothing has ever heard it. That is also the reason snd_channel_step takes d1 as an argument in the reconstruction and the TICK hands it a value of its own: snd_sfx_tick's outgoing d1 is not modelled, so the defect is pinned from snd_channel_step's own entry (where d1 is the case's) and no tick-entered case may put a $97 in a pattern.
cmt 0x17c72 snd_music_tick's shared `rts`, the twin of $1a5d8. FOUR paths reach it: the gate's `beq.s` at $17caa, the drop accumulator's `bcs.s` at $17cb4, the mixdown's `bmi.w` at $17d96 (channel A's SFX-active flag negative) and the `bra.w` at $17f20 that closes the PSG block. It sits BELOW the tempo head, so it belongs to neither the head's 44 bytes nor the body's 644.
cmt 0x1a5d8 snd_sfx_tick's SHARED `rts`, and the two bytes that separate the trigger's body from the tick's. Reached ONLY from inside $1a5da -- the `bmi.s` at $1a5e6 (channel A's flag negative) and the `bra.s` at $1a600 (the ordinary end) -- exactly as $17c72 serves snd_music_tick, and by nothing else in the image (verified by a whole-image branch/jump scan). ../recreate/STATUS.md batch 23 records the correction; the earlier plate on $1a48a called it an orphan.
fn 0x1a5da snd_sfx_tick
cmt 0x1a5da 600 bytes with its shared `rts` ($1a5d8..$1a82f), RECONSTRUCTED in batch 23 (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py). snd_music_tick's FIRST callee, `bsr.w` from $17cb6, and its only caller. Steps snd_prng_step unconditionally, then runs one arm per channel whose snd_sfx_active_flags byte is non-zero. CHANNEL A'S FLAG IS TESTED TWICE: `tst.b 2254(a3) / bmi.s $1a5d8` ends the WHOLE routine when it is negative, so B and C do not run either, and only then does the `beq.s` skip A alone; B and C get a `beq` and no sign test. Nothing in the shipped code writes a negative flag (the trigger `sf`s it to 0 and stores 1), so that arm is reachable only from seeded state. The three arms at $1a602/$1a6bc/$1a776 are 186 bytes each and identical bar SIX base-plus-stride blocks: the 26-byte state (a3+14064/14090/14116), the active flag (2254/2255/2256), the mix period word (4052/4054/4056), the mix volume byte (4060/4061/4062), the PSG VOLUME SHADOW the end-of-effect arm clears (4046/4047/4048 = $1835a/$1835b/$1835c, snd_psg_shadow + PSG register 8/9/10 -- NOT the SFX mix volumes) and, unexpectedly, the PRNG BYTE: channel A reads $1aae6, B $1aae7 and C $1aae8, three different bytes of one 32-bit state. The shared noise byte $18366 is written by whichever arm ran last, from the LOW byte of that channel's own mix period.
fn 0x1a602 snd_sfx_tick_channel_a
fn 0x1a6bc snd_sfx_tick_channel_b
fn 0x1a776 snd_sfx_tick_channel_c
cmt 0x1a602 One arm, 186 bytes, `bsr`d from $1a5da and from nowhere else (the B and C arms are the same body at $1a6bc and $1a776). Sequence: descriptor +0 (duration) and +12 (sustain) both zero -> `sf` the active flag, zero this channel's PSG volume shadow, return. Otherwise decrement +0; if the period countdown state+14 has expired AND either +12 or the slide count +9 is non-zero, rewrite the mix period from descriptor +2..3 plus a delta byte -- 0, or the channel's PRNG byte when +7 is set (sfx 12, 20 and 21) -- added to BOTH halves via `add.b` then `addx.b`, so the 16-bit period moves by delta*257 and not by delta; if the countdown has expired with neither, jump straight to the volume stream, skipping the countdown decrement, the secondary counter, the slide and the noise byte. Then decrement state+14; the secondary counter state+16 (reload +13, and a reload of 0 disables it so the slide runs every tick) gates the constant pitch slide, whose direction byte +8 is 0/positive/NEGATIVE for none/subtract/ADD by the word at +4. Then, unless `btst #3` of +6 is set, the shared noise byte takes this channel's period low byte. Finally the volume countdown state+15 (reload +11) steps the volume stream at state+22: $00-$7f is the volume, $80 reloads the cursor from state+18 and takes the byte there, and ANY OTHER negative byte HOLDS -- neither the cursor nor the mix volume is written at all.
fn 0x1aaca snd_prng_step
cmt 0x1aaca 28 bytes ($1aaca..$1aae5), RECONSTRUCTED in batch 23 (recreate/src/sound.c, differential in recreate/test/test_sound.py). THE SOUND MODULE'S OWN PRNG, distinct from the game's rng_next ($68c6). One `bsr.w` caller, $1a5de in snd_sfx_tick, and nothing else. `andi.b #$48 / addi.b #$38 / lsl.b #2` on the state's top byte leaves bit 3 XOR bit 6 in X (the bias is what carries bit 3 into bit 6), and `roxl.w 14172(a3)` then `roxl.w 14170(a3)` chain that X through the LOW word and on into the HIGH one -- so it is a 32-bit shift left with the feedback bit entering at the bottom, spelt as two word RMWs on memory whose ORDER is the mechanism. Returns d0 with the state's new top byte in its LOW BYTE (a `move.b`, so the rest of d0 is the caller's); the one caller ignores it and each arm re-reads snd_prng_state + its own channel number instead. Stepped EVERY tick and not reset by snd_play_song, so it is history-dependent -- any test comparing two ticks must seed $1aae6.
fn 0x1a48a snd_trigger_effect
cmt 0x1a48a Stub +56, 334 bytes ($1a48a..$1a5d7; the `rts` at $1a5d8 is NOT an orphan -- corrected in batch 23: it is snd_sfx_tick's SHARED `rts`, the target of that routine's two backward branches at $1a5e6 and $1a600, exactly as $17c72 serves snd_music_tick. # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Effect dispatch table -- 23 handlers, none of which Ghidra reached.
# Dispatcher (0xddec and 0xde62): moveq #0,d0 / move.w 62(a1),d0 / lsl.w #2 /
# lea effect_handler_table,a0 / lea (a0,d0.w),a0 / movea.l (a0),a0.
# The names below state exactly what each body does. What the effects MEAN in
# the game (the "pick up an item" reading is the obvious one) is NOT verified.

var 0x1023a effect_handler_table
cmt 0x1023a 23 longword handler addresses, $10296..$103dc, indexed by a word field at offset 62 (and 64) of an object record. Both call sites also set $c030 (a message id) and $c034 := $32 (a 50-frame display timer) just before dispatching.
fn 0x10296 effect_add4_clamped_b6fa
cmt 0x10296 d0 := $b6fa + 4; if d0 > $b6f8 then $b6fa := $b6f8 else $b6fa := d0. hud_meter_value raised by 4 and clamped to the maximum in hud_meter_max. Two details a port has to keep, both read off the encoding: the `addq.w` wraps in 16 BITS, and the `bgt` is a SIGNED compare, so a value raised past $7fff comes out negative and is then stored rather than clamped. `bgt` is also strict, so a raise that lands EXACTLY on the maximum takes the store arm.
fn 0x102bc effect_add2_clamped_b6fa
cmt 0x102bc Same as effect_add4_clamped_b6fa but +2.

fn 0x103dc effect_restore_b6fa_to_max
cmt 0x103dc $b6fa := $b6f8 -- one memory-to-memory `move.w` with no test at all, so a maximum BELOW the counter LOWERS it. That is what separates it from the two clamped adds, which can only ever raise.

# --- the state all 29 of the routines above write --------------------------
# Read from the bodies of their WRITERS and of their readers, and mirrored as
# constants in recreate/include/wonderboy.h (which test/ scrapes). Every name
# here is at the mechanism and carries its address, because what the values
# MEAN is still open -- see the section header above.
var 0xbbbe hud_slot_helmet
var 0xbbbf hud_slot_bbbe_changed
cmt 0xbbbe $bbbe..$bbc9 is SIX 2-byte HUD slots, each {value, changed}. $b8f0 walks the six `changed` bytes every frame: a nonzero one is cleared, a per-slot flag in the $dbb6.. block is raised, the slot's cell is cleared ($bb8a) and -- only when `value` is nonzero -- an icon is OR-blitted over it ($bba0). So one `move.w #$Nff,slot.l` from a handler means "value := N, and redraw me", and the low byte is always $ff. The VALUE's meaning differs slot by slot; batch 17 identified the first two FROM THE MESSAGES THEIR OWN PATHS POST: $bbbe is the HELMET (actor_damage_followed spends one charge per hit INSTEAD of hud_meter_value; emptying it posts message $18, "   Helmet is Broken") and $bbc0 the GAUNTLET (actor_damage_template_hitpoints spends one charge per hit and DOUBLES the damage dealt while one remains; emptying posts $19, " Gauntlet is Broken") -- both rearmed $00ff as they empty, both string resolutions pinned by a case; that the slot whose break-message names the helmet IS the helmet's charge counter is the reading those pins support. $bbc8 is tested against 1..6 as an icon variant, still unidentified. All six values are cleared together by the new-game reset at $fe4a.
fn 0x69fe actor_damage_followed
cmt 0x69fe 266 bytes ($69fe..$6b07, ending where actor_damage_table begins -- the word table its own `lea $6b08.l,a2` at $6a62 names), RECONSTRUCTED in recreate/src/actor.c. What a hit COSTS the followed record. `tst.b $a32.w` -- a BYTE test of state_flag_a32, where all twelve other readers in the image are `tst.w`, so this one reads only the HIGH byte and a small positive word sends it to followed_actor_default where followed_actor_record's `bne` would pick followed_actor_a32 -- picks a1, and a record already carrying bit 4 of 9(a1) (the invulnerability the flicker countdown at $f14 clears) returns having written NOTHING AT ALL. Otherwise: bset #0 of 9(a1); 31(a1) := $c - 2 * effect_state_bd66; clr.b 22(a1); then `bset #6,8(a1)` -- the flicker -- and, ONLY IF IT WAS CLEAR, the cost. The cost is a word: 19(a0) with its SIGN BIT SET carries the damage in its own low seven bits (`bclr #7,d0`), otherwise 19(a0) indexes table_ptr_21e8c and that template's TYPE word at +12 indexes actor_damage_table -- and that second index is a LONGWORD one over an unsigned doubled word, so a type from $4000 up reads up to 64 KB ABOVE the table. It is spent on hud_slot_bbbe if that slot has a charge (rearming it $00ff and posting message $18, "   Helmet is Broken", which is what NAMES the slot) and otherwise on hud_meter_value with a `bpl` floor and 21(a1) := $64. Then the x compare against (a0) -- the same signed compare actor_set_side_flag makes, read onto the DAMAGED record and INCLUSIVE where that one is strict -- sets or clears bit 3 of 8(a1) and writes 30(a1) $ff or 0; then snd_trigger_effect(id $b, channel 0) through the stub, bits 0/1 of 8(a1) set, bit 2 cleared and 11(a1) := 5. THIRTY-EIGHT control-flow sites, not the ten this comment previously listed: ten `bsr.w` ($552a, $55dc, $5672, $56b4, $575e, $57f2, $5886, $5ae6, $5b70, $5c20) and TWENTY-EIGHT tail jumps -- 23 `bra.w`, four `bne.w` ($413e, $4650, $4c84, $7298) and one `beq.w` ($465a).
var 0x6b08 actor_damage_table
cmt 0x6b08 62 bytes ($6b08..$6b45), 31 WORDS: how much damage the attacker of each spawn type deals, read by actor_damage_followed alone. DATA between the two damage paths' bodies, and what fixes both extents -- its one reference in the image is the `lea $6b08.l,a2` at $6a62, and it ends where the 25 control-flow sites that enter $6b46 land. The shipped words are 0..6 (six distinct values). NOT self-bounding and NOT bounds-checked from either end: the index is the template's TYPE word doubled by an `add.w` (sixteen-bit wrap) and consumed by a LONGWORD `move.w 0(a2,d0.l)`, so a type from $4000 up reads up to 64 KB ABOVE the table and $8000 reads entry 0. Mirrored as WB_ACTOR_DAMAGE_TABLE in recreate/include/wonderboy.h.
fn 0x6b46 actor_damage_template_hitpoints
cmt 0x6b46 114 bytes ($6b46..$6bb7), RECONSTRUCTED in recreate/src/actor.c. What a hit COSTS the actor a0 names. Its FIRST FOUR INSTRUCTIONS are `move.w #$13,d0 / move.w #$1,d1 / lea $17adc.l,a5 / jsr 56(a5)` = snd_trigger_effect(id $13, CHANNEL B) -- the ONE call site in the image that does not pass channel A, which is why the sound module's B arm is live code and not the dead arm the $1a48a comment claimed. Then a1 := table_ptr_21e8c + (19(a0) << 5), the actor's own template; d0 := effect_record_list's first BYTE plus one (`addq.b`, so $ff wraps to 0); hud_slot_bbc0 is spent one charge (rearming it $00ff and posting message $19, " Gauntlet is Broken", which NAMES that slot) and d0 is DOUBLED on BOTH arms that spent one -- the `beq` at the top of the slot test is what skips the `add.w`, so the doubling is skipped only when the slot was ALREADY EMPTY on entry (the previous reading here had this inverted). Then `sub.w d0,4(a1)` off the template's hit-point pool and, on zero OR negative, `bset #3,9(a0)` -- the one writer in the image of a bit 35 `btst #3,9(a0)` sites read. TWENTY-FIVE control-flow sites, not the one this comment previously listed: `bsr.w` at $709a and TWENTY-FOUR `bra.w` tail jumps.

cmt 0xb6fa The value and the maximum of a meter DRAWN IN FOUR-UNIT CELLS by $b61e: `value/4` filled cells (each an 8-row blit through $b6c2 into screen_back), then the remainder, then `max/4 - value/4` empty ones -- so both words are consumed as counts, not as flags. $b74a sets the maximum to $18..$28 off thresholds on $bd70; $69fe subtracts damage from the value and floors it at 0; $e032 ticks it by one each way; $fe4a resets both to $14. The three handlers here raise it by 4 or 2 (clamped, SIGNED) or restore it to the maximum. var 0x21e4 effect_state_21e4
cmt 0x21e4 Word. FIVE READERS, and every one of them is inside player_stage_transition ($1f6e, $2018, $205e, $2072, $20d4) -- which is why cmt 0xbd66 and recreate/include/wonderboy.h both said "no reader at all" from batch 2 until batch 40 phase C ported that routine. SIX WRITERS, and ELEVEN operand sites in all -- recreate/test/test_player.py rebuilds the whole census from the bytes and asserts it, including the `bra.w` DISPLACEMENT at $4962 that a naive scan for the address counts as a twelfth. The writers: `move.w #$1,$21e4.l` at $c06 (player_pending_event_gate) -- the ONLY absolute-LONG site of the eleven, which is why an encoding-blind census misses it, and it is not incidental: it sits between `jsr $fe8c.l` (the life restart) and `lea 4(a7),a7 / jmp $e5ba.l`, so THE GATE FORCES THE PLAYER'S FORM BACK TO 1 ON A STACK-UNWINDING EXIT. Then `move.w #$1,$21e4.w` at $101c6 (scene_exit_action_select_a30_table), `move.w #$2,$21e4.w` at $10350/$10360/$10370 (the three $bd68 effect handlers) and the `clr.w $21e4.w` at $fe56 (the new-game reset). WHAT IT MEANS, read off the SHIPPED bytes rather than off its readers: it picks one of the three 88-byte posture records at $21e6 / $223e / $2296, and those hold three CONSECUTIVE SPRITE FAMILIES ($104.., $10e.., $11e.., each with an idle pair, a jump pair, a fall pair and a four-frame walk cycle) -- so the word is WHICH OF THREE PLAYER APPEARANCES IS DRAWN. It also selects which of the two 24-word transition tables at $2312/$2342 plays, whether the SWING animation runs at all, and -- by an EQUALITY test, `cmpi.w #$1` -- which of two hurt sprite pairs a hurt airborne player shows, so a hurt player in state 0 gets record 2's pair ($12c/$12d) because record 0 has none of its own. RECONSTRUCTED as WB_EFFECT_STATE_21E4.
cmt 0xbd66 Three state words the handlers set to a small ordinal -- $bd66 to 1..5, $bd68 to 1..3, $bd6a to 1..4 -- plus effect_state_21e4, which only the three $bd68 handlers touch and always with the same 2. All four are cleared by the new-game reset at $fe4a. TWO of them have a reader among the recovered functions, and both spell the SAME shape -- `n - 2*value` stamped into a record byte: $69fe turns $bd66 into `12 - 2*value` for 31(a1), and $6796 (actor_stun_followed, batch 30) turns $bd68 into `$a - 2*value` for 29(a1). CORRECTION (batch 30): this plate previously said only $bd66 had one. What the ordinals SELECT is still open -- only what they COST is known. CORRECTION (batch 40 phase C): "$21e4 has no reader at all" is RETRACTED. effect_state_21e4 has FIVE readers ($1f6e, $2018, $205e, $2072, $20d4) and every one of them is inside player_stage_transition, which is why they were invisible while that routine was unported: it picks which of two transition frame tables plays, which of three posture records the player's sprite comes out of, which of two hurt sprite pairs a hurt airborne player shows, and whether the swing runs at all. CORRECTION (batch 40): $bd6a HAS THREE, and all of them are the PLAYER's, which is why they were invisible while that tier was unported -- $e12 (player_jump_step) and $109a (player_reset_ground_state) add 8 to its LOW BYTE for the jump's height, and $fde/$1048 (player_step_and_arm, unported) add 4 to the WHOLE WORD for the walk's top speed. So the word these three handlers stamp is how high the player jumps and how fast he runs. AND THAT IS A RECURRENCE, NOT AN INCIDENT: this plate's original sentence covered BOTH $bd6a and $21e4, batch 40 phase A retracted the $bd6a half and left the $21e4 half standing verbatim, and batch 40 phase C then had to retract that too -- for the same reason (the readers were all in an unported tier) and, in $21e4's case, with a second miss on top: its sixth WRITER is the only absolute-LONG site of eleven, so the census that found the readers still undercounted. The class is "a plate that names two addresses gets corrected for one of them"; the cure used here is that both halves now carry their own census, and recreate/test/test_player.py asserts $21e4's from the image. See cmt 0x21e4.
var 0x6f9c state_word_6f9c

A ONE-SHOT FLAG with exactly one writer and one consumer: set_state_6f9c_ffff ($10232, recreate/src/effects.c) writes $ffff through an absolute-SHORT operand, and actor_behavior_type60 ($6f7e, batch 31) tests it, `clr.w`s it and stamps `move.w #$36,4(a0)`. var 0xb444 effect_record_list
cmt 0xb444 ADDENDUM (batch 17): the first BYTE is also a 0..4 attack level -- `clr.b $b444` at $101cc, `cmpi.b #$3 / bgt` + `addq.b #1` at $10746/$10752 -- and actor_damage_template_hitpoints reads it as `byte + 1`, the damage one hit deals before the gauntlet doubles it. It is therefore BOTH that counter and the emptiness sign $b39c's `tst.w / bpl` reads, which is why different writers store different widths at this address. How the two readings coexist is not established.
var 0xb546 effect_record_write_ptr
cmt 0xb546 A LONGWORD write pointer into effect_record_list. The four push handlers advance it by 2 BEFORE re-reading it, so the record lands at the NEW pointer, the list grows upward, and the pointer addresses the newest record rather than the next free slot. $fe4a resets it to $b444 and sets that word to $ffff, which $b39c reads as "empty" ($b444 < 0 -> return). Note the pointer sits at the far END of the list's own $102 bytes: the 129th push writes over the pointer's high word. A record is one word whose two byte fields are not identified.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The status panel: panel_refresh_frame's once-a-frame pass and its leaves,
# $b346..$bf5e. Twenty of them are reconstructed in recreate/src/hud.c -- eleven
# leaves plus the second tier above them (the digit plotter, its three field
# walks, and the four fields panel_refresh_frame draws, plus the meter's pass);
# the addresses and the blit geometry are mirrored as constants in
# recreate/include/wonderboy.h, which recreate/test/ scrapes. Every name here
# is at the MECHANISM and carries its address, the same rule as the effect
# block above -- see recreate/STATUS.md for what each one is pinned by.
#
# Reference counts below ("the only writer", "seven readers") are BYTE SCANS OF
# THE WHOLE IMAGE for the address as an abs.l operand and, where the address
# fits, as an abs.w one -- not a walk of the 252 recovered functions. Where the
# claim is only over the recovered set it says so.
#
# THE BLIND SPOT IN THAT METHOD: an abs.l/abs.w byte scan cannot see a
# PC-RELATIVE reference. `lea $b6e4(pc),a0` encodes as 41fa 0052 -- a
# displacement from the instruction, with the target address nowhere in the
# bytes -- so it is invisible to a scan for $0000b6e4. A d16(pc) sweep (every
# even offset whose opcode carries EA mode 7 / reg 2, resolved to pc+2+d16) was
# run over all eight addresses this block counts and found exactly ONE such
# reference in the image: the $b690 one now recorded under $b6e4. The claims
# that depend on completeness -- frame_tick_b39a's "only writer" / "only
# entropy" ($b39a) and bcd_sub_score_bd70's "no reference anywhere" ($b5c6,
# dead as shipped) -- survive it. Any count added here later needs the same
# sweep; the second tier's eight new addresses were put through it and it
# found nothing, so every count below rests on the abs.l/abs.w scan alone.
#
# A CALL-SITE count ("sixteen call sites", "one caller") is a separate scan and
# covers a separate set of encodings: every even offset in $3f8..$218d0 decoded
# as bsr.s/bsr.w/bsr.l, jsr/jmp abs.l, jsr/jmp abs.w and jsr d16(pc). It cannot
# see a call through a register or a jump table -- which is not hypothetical
# here, see $bbca.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0xb346 panel_refresh_frame
cmt 0xb346 44 bytes, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/hud.c) -- the LAST of the subsystem, thirteen batches after the leaves under it, because $bbca could not be entered until the sound module was ported. Ten calls and an rts, run once a frame from game_main_loop's $66e-gated block: `jsr $d93a` then bsr to $b39c, $b54c, $b74a, $b7c6, $b61e, $b8f0, $bbca, $bd32 and $b372. It sets NEITHER d0 NOR d7 before any of them, so each callee is entered with what the one before it left, and reading the ten bodies settles what that means: d7 CANNOT reach a pixel (both readers `move.w field,d7 / swap`, burying the caller's half below every drawn nibble); d0 reaches three, and two are forced by the chain itself ($b5ea's `moveq #0,d0` gives $b74a a zero font select however the pass was entered, and $b7ea re-forces it for $b7c6); the third is NOT -- $bd32's font select is whatever $bbca left, i.e. hud_blit_panel_frame's last `movem.l (a0)+,d0-d5`, the panel frame's LAST ROW, FIRST LONGWORD. All thirteen shipped frames hold $1000e000 there, so the alternate font is unreachable on the game's own data; a poked frame reaches it, which is how the flow is pinned rather than argued.

fn 0xb372 select_table_21e8c_and_tick_b39a
cmt 0xb372 Two unrelated halves, and the last thing panel_refresh_frame does. `tst.w $a32.w / bne` picks table_ptr_21e8c := $21e6a when state_flag_a32 is non-zero and $21c60 when it is zero (a `move.l #imm` either way, so the two addresses are immediates, not a table lookup); then `addq.w #1,$b39a` ticks frame_tick_b39a. No callee, no hardware, no register argument.
var 0xa32 state_flag_a32
cmt 0xa32 A word flag addressed as abs.w ($a32.w) at all 16 of its operand sites and never as abs.l. Three writers -- `move.w #$ffff` at $1f2a, `clr.w` at $e01a and at $ff10 -- and thirteen readers, every one of them a bare `tst.w`: the twelve the linear listing resolves are $a64, $10be, $118c, $11b6, $13d6, $54b4, $5c8c, $67e0, $6bb8, $8e7c, $b372 and $dbc8, and the thirteenth is at $69fe, inside a run the listing desyncs on. So it is consumed purely as a boolean; WHICH mode it selects is not established, which is why the name carries the address.
var 0x21e8c table_ptr_21e8c
cmt 0x21e8c A longword pointer with exactly nine operand references in the image: the two `move.l #imm` writes inside select_table_21e8c_and_tick_b39a, and seven `movea.l $21e8c.l,An` reads ($6828, $6a52, $6b58, $6bfa, $6c38, $ff4a, $1004c). Nothing else writes it, so whatever those seven walk is chosen once a frame by state_flag_a32. $21e6a is also loaded directly by `lea` at $704a; $21c60 has no other reference at all.
var 0xb39a frame_tick_b39a
cmt 0xb39a A word ticked once a frame by select_table_21e8c_and_tick_b39a -- its ONLY writer in the image. It has exactly two operand references (it is above $7fff, so an abs.w encoding is impossible): the `addq.w #1` at $b392 and `move.w $b39a.l,d1` at $6916, which is inside rng_next. So this counter is the PRNG's only entropy that is not the shifter's video address register -- and under the differential oracle, where $ff8209 reads 0, it is the ONLY entropy left (see the cmt at $68c6).

fn 0xb39c hud_draw_newest_record
cmt 0xb39c 62 bytes, reconstructed in recreate/src/hud.c: panel_refresh_frame's SECOND call, and the display of whichever record was pushed last. `tst.w $b444 / bpl` reads effect_record_list's first WORD as the empty test -- the new-game reset at $fe4a leaves $ffff there, so a negative first word returns at once -- and everything after it addresses the record effect_record_write_ptr points at, which is the newest (the push handlers advance the pointer before storing). Then two independent gates: `tst.b $b54a` (record_fresh_flag) raises panel_restore_flag_dbb5 and `bsr $b410` to draw the bitmap the record's HIGH byte selects, and `cmpi.b #$ff,1(a0)` skips $b3da unless the record's LOW byte is something other than $ff. The write pointer is RE-READ between the two, since $b410 clobbers a0. ONE caller, $b34c (the whole-image branch scan described on $b850; the `bvs.s $b39c` the same scan reports at $b32a is inside the $a271 string block and is text, not an instruction).
fn 0xb3da hud_draw_record_digits
proto 0xb3da hud_draw_record_digits record@A0
cmt 0xb3da 54 bytes, reconstructed in recreate/src/hud.c, and a FOURTH digit-field walk beside the three at $b5ea/$b7ea/$bd4a. `moveq #$0,d7 / move.b 1(a0),d7 / lsl.w #8,d7 / swap d7` puts the record's LOW byte in d7's top byte, so the two `bsr $b850` draw that byte's high and low nibbles; the `moveq` clears the whole register first, so no caller value reaches the digits. It differs from all three other walks: `move.w #$1,d0` before EACH plot (so both digits use the alternate glyphs, which no other walk forces), and the latch forced between the two plots rather than before a tail, so a leading zero blanks and the second digit always prints. The step between the digits is `lea -1279(a0),a0` -- the same -$4ff as the others' `suba.l`, in a different encoding. Draws at screen_back + $3490, which is ROW 84, BYTE 16 -- INSIDE the 64 x 32 px rectangle hud_blit_record_bitmap fills at $2800 (rows 64..95, bytes 0..31). So on a frame that takes both of $b39c's arms the bitmap is blitted first and these two digits are stamped into it; recreate/test/test_hud.py pins that geometry as its own assert and compares the bitmap's rows with the digit bytes left out. ONE caller, $b3d4.
var 0xb54a record_fresh_flag
cmt 0xb54a The byte hud_draw_newest_record tests before it redraws the newest record's bitmap. TWO operand references in the whole image and NO clear among them: `st $b54a.l` at $1262, inside the unrecovered routine at $1208 that walks the record list, and the `tst.b` at $b3ae. So once something raises it the bitmap is redrawn every frame from then on -- unless a writer reaches it through an address register, which an abs.l scan cannot see.

fn 0xb410 hud_blit_record_bitmap
proto 0xb410 hud_blit_record_bitmap record@A0
cmt 0xb410 `move.b (a0),d0 / mulu.w #$400,d0 / lea $1079c,a0 / adda.w d0,a0` then 32 rows of 8 x `move.l (a0)+,(a1)+` with `lea 128(a1),a1` -- a 32-byte-wide (64 px), 32-row copy into screen_back + $2800. Called only from $b3be, with a0 = effect_record_write_ptr's value, so the byte it indexes on is the HIGH byte of the newest record (the 5/6/7/8 the four effect_push_record_* handlers push). Two operand-size details a port must keep: `move.b` leaves d0's high byte alive into the `mulu`, and `adda.w` then keeps only the product's low word, sign-extended -- so only the selector's low 6 bits reach the address at all, and a selector with bit 5 set addresses BELOW the table.
var 0x1079c record_bitmap_table
cmt 0x1079c $400 bytes per entry, indexed by a record's high byte. One operand reference in the whole image, the `lea` at $b416 inside hud_blit_record_bitmap.

fn 0xb54c hud_draw_counter_bd6e
proto 0xb54c hud_draw_counter_bd6e digits@D7
cmt 0xb54c 22 bytes, the second thing panel_refresh_frame calls: `movea.l screen_back,a0 / adda.w #$1e08,a0 / move.w bcd_counter_bd6e,d7 / swap d7 / bsr $b5ea`. The `move.w` replaces only d7's LOW word and the `swap` then buries the caller's own high word beneath the counter, where no rotation of the four-digit walk can bring it back under a drawn nibble -- so entry d7 is live input that cannot change the display, which recreate/test/test_hud.py pins with a poisoned d7 instead of arguing. It is NOT lost, though: four `rol.l #4` come to a `swap`, so at the `rts` d7 holds the caller's high word with the counter below it, which is what tells WHICH half was buried and is pinned as test_a_word_field_buries_the_callers_high_word_and_hands_it_back_in_d7. d0 is unread here (the four-digit walk forces its own). ONE caller, $b350; the count is the whole-image branch scan described on $b850.

fn 0xb562 bcd_add_counter_bd6e
proto 0xb562 bcd_add_counter_bd6e addend@D0
fn 0xb582 bcd_sub_counter_bd6e
proto 0xb582 bcd_sub_counter_bd6e subtrahend@D0
fn 0xb5a2 bcd_add_score_bd70
proto 0xb5a2 bcd_add_score_bd70 addend@D0
fn 0xb5c6 bcd_sub_score_bd70
proto 0xb5c6 bcd_sub_score_bd70 subtrahend@D0
cmt 0xb562 Four packed-BCD accumulators of one shape: save a0/a1, stage d0 at bcd_addend_bd78, point a0 past the staged addend and a1 past the accumulator, then N x `abcd -(a0),-(a1)` (or `sbcd`) walking DOWN so the lowest digit pair goes first. $b562/$b582 stage a WORD and fold 2 bytes into bcd_counter_bd6e; $b5a2/$b5c6 stage a LONGWORD and fold 4 into bcd_score_bd70. NOTE the `c308`/`8308` opcodes here are `abcd -(a0),-(a1)` and `sbcd -(a0),-(a1)`. tools/prg_dis.py printed them as `and.b d1,a0` / `or.b d1,a0` until batch 33 fixed the whole two-register family (abcd/sbcd, addx/subx, cmpm), and tools/recreate_kit/test/test_prg_dis.py now pins those forms and their operand order; the CHECKED-OUT out/wonderboy_dis.txt is a generated file that predates the fix, so its lines in this band are still the wrong ones until it is regenerated. Ghidra always got them right (bcdAdjust + in_XF in decomp.c). THE FIRST DIGIT PAIR FOLDS IN THE CALLER'S X FLAG: `movem.l`, `move.w d0,$bd78` and `lea` leave X alone, so entry X is live input; and the LAST pair leaves the carry out of the accumulator's top byte in X, which `movem.l (a7)+` and `rts` do not disturb either, so it is live output. RECONSTRUCTED WITH BOTH SINCE BATCH 33 -- recreate/src/hud.c takes the entry bit as a parameter and returns the exit one, and THREE ported sites hand it a bit their own run produced: $4e5a -> $4e64 (slot 28's counter then score, only a `move.l #imm,d0` between), $5184 -> $5188 (the payout's own `abcd`, then the counter), and $6c26 (the spawn type's bit 14 out of $6c20's `lsl.w #2`; see the plate at $6bb8). Entry X is not always 0 in the game -- $e058's `subq.w #1,hud_meter_value` sets it when the meter was already zero, and $e064 calls $b5a2 two instructions later -- and the differential oracle still cannot produce X=1 AT A RUN'S ENTRY (its shim FORCES SR=$2700 after the reset -- a 68000 reset leaves the condition codes alone, so the force is what makes the entry CCR deterministic at all -- and emu.run has no entry-CCR parameter), so a case entering one of these four DIRECTLY with X set remains impossible; the chains above are reached from a run that entered with X=0 and are pinned in recreate/test/test_behavior.py. Call sites: $b562 from $4e5a/$5188/$5450; $b582 from $4678 and (as `jsr`) $ddae/$de24; $b5a2 from eight sites; $b5c6 from NONE -- it has no reference anywhere in the image and is dead as shipped.
var 0xbd6e bcd_counter_bd6e
cmt 0xbd6e Two bytes of packed BCD, i.e. four digits, drawn by $b54c into screen_back + $1e08. Eight operand references in the image, among them the draw's own read at $b554 and the `clr.w` in the new-game reset at $fea6. The two accumulators that move it never name this address at all -- they reach it by pre-decrementing from $bd70 -- which is why a scan for writers of $bd6e misses them.
var 0xbd70 bcd_score_bd70
cmt 0xbd70 Four bytes of packed BCD, i.e. eight digits. Six operand references. Read as a LONGWORD by $b74a, which draws the eight digits at screen_back + $a21 and then thresholds it to set hud_meter_max ($30000 -> $18, $100000 -> $1c, $200000 -> $20, $300000 -> $24, $400000 -> $28); read again by $b7c6, which draws max($bd70, bcd_hiscore_bd74). Cleared by the new-game reset at $fea0. The other two references are the `lea $bd70,a1` inside the two COUNTER accumulators, for which this address is only the end marker of bcd_counter_bd6e's two bytes. That is the whole evidence for the word "score" in the name -- no display was followed to a rendered digit.
var 0xbd74 bcd_hiscore_bd74
cmt 0xbd74 The four bytes immediately above bcd_score_bd70, and the value $b7c6 compares the score against before drawing the larger of the two at screen_back + $a49. Four operand references: the two `lea $bd74,a1` that make it the END marker of the score's four bytes (so the score accumulators stop just below it, never writing it), and the `cmp.l` at $b7cc and the `move.l` at $b7d6 inside $b7c6. Nothing in the recovered code writes it.
var 0xbd78 bcd_addend_bd78
cmt 0xbd78 Four bytes of scratch: where the four accumulators park d0 so `abcd`/`sbcd` can read it from memory. Exactly four operand references in the image -- the `move.w`/`move.l d0,$bd78` at $b566, $b586, $b5a6 and $b5ca -- so nothing else in the program reads or writes it, and its contents between calls are dead. The word form writes only $bd78..$bd79 and leaves $bd7a..$bd7b holding the previous longword's low half.

fn 0xb5ea hud_draw_four_digits
proto 0xb5ea hud_draw_four_digits cursor@A0 digits@D7
cmt 0xb5ea 52 bytes: `moveq #$0,d0`, then four `bsr $b850` separated by `suba.l #$4ff` (net +1 byte -- the right half of the same 16-pixel group) and `suba.l #$4f9` (net +7 -- the next group's left half), since $b850 leaves a0 1280 bytes lower and each subtraction is that minus a column step. `move.w #$ffff,digit_significant_seen_b84e` before the FOURTH digit, so an all-zero field still prints its last digit, and `clr.w` after it. The `moveq` means the caller's font never reaches any of the four, which is what separates this walk from $b7ea's and $bd4a's. ONE caller, $b55c.

fn 0xb61e hud_draw_meter
cmt 0xb61e 164 bytes and no register arguments -- every input is a word in memory. `st panel_restore_flag_dbb3`, then `moveq #$0,d6 / move.w hud_meter_value,d6 / divu.w #$4,d6` and three passes over meter_cell_offsets threaded on the cursor hud_blit_meter_cell returns: `value/4` full cells ($146fc) in a `dbf` loop, then ONE partial cell selected by the remainder the `swap d6` brings down ($1471c/$1473c/$1475c for 3/2/1 and none for 0), then `hud_meter_max/4` minus the cells already drawn -- recovered as `(a1 - $b6e4) asr 1`, which is the only reason $b6c2 returns its advanced a1 -- of the empty cell $1477c. EVERY step after the divide is 16-BIT (`asr.w`, `sub.w`, and a `dbf` whose counter is a word), so a maximum below the value drives the empty count NEGATIVE, the `bne` still takes the loop, and the `dbf` then runs it 65535 times, walking the cursor far past the table's ten entries and blitting off the end of the screen. No differential case reaches that and whether the game's own writers can produce it was NOT established -- registered unpinned in recreate/STATUS.md. ONE caller, $b35c.
var 0x146fc meter_cell_full
var 0x1471c meter_cell_partial_3
var 0x1473c meter_cell_partial_2
var 0x1475c meter_cell_partial_1
var 0x1477c meter_cell_empty
cmt 0x146fc The FULL cell, and the first of a block of five consecutive 32-byte cell bitmaps (8 rows x 4 planes, one 8-pixel column) that runs $146fc..$1479b. Each is reached by its OWN absolute `lea` inside hud_draw_meter and handed to hud_blit_meter_cell in a2 -- $146fc full, $1471c/$1473c/$1475c the partial cells for a remainder of 3/2/1, $1477c empty -- so the block is contiguous by measurement, not by indexing: nothing in the image steps through it. Each of the five is labelled here separately and mirrored as WB_METER_CELL_FULL/_PARTIAL_3/_2/_1/_EMPTY in recreate/include/wonderboy.h. Each has exactly ONE operand reference in the image -- $b644, $b65c, $b672, $b688, $b6b4, in that order -- and a d16(pc) sweep over the whole image finds no PC-relative reference to any of them. What a cell DEPICTS is not established; see $b6fa for why the meter's own reading is still open.

fn 0xb6c2 hud_blit_meter_cell
cmt 0xb6c2 REGISTER INTERFACE (no `proto` line: the result is in a1, which the proto directive cannot express -- it forces a void return). a1 = a cursor into meter_cell_offsets, a2 = the cell's 32 bytes; returns a1 ADVANCED by 2, a2 advanced by 32, a0 and d0 clobbered. Body: `movea.l screen_back,a0 / adda.w (a1)+,a0` then 8 rows of `move.b (a2)+,(a0)` at +0/+2/+4/+6 with `lea 160(a0),a0` -- the four plane bytes of one 8-pixel column, 8 rows tall. The advanced a1 IS consumed: $b61e subtracts $b6e4 from it and halves the difference to count the cells it drew. The advanced a2 is not: FIVE `bsr` sites, all of them inside $b61e -- $b648 and $b6b8 are the bodies of `dbf` loops (over the full cells and over the empty ones), $b660/$b676/$b68c are the three arms of the one-shot `cmpi.w`/`bne` chain that draws the single partial cell -- and every one of the five is preceded IMMEDIATELY by its own `lea $146fc/$1471c/$1473c/$1475c/$1477c,a2`, including the two in loops, which reload inside the loop body. So a2 is dead on return at all five.
var 0xb6e4 meter_cell_offsets
cmt 0xb6e4 Ten words, $0a01 $0a08 $0a09 $0a10 $0a11 $0f01 $0f08 $0f09 $0f10 $0f11 -- the screen_back byte offsets of the meter's ten cells, walked with `(a1)+` by hud_blit_meter_cell. Half of them are ODD, which is why the cell is plotted with `move.b` and not a longword: an odd offset is the right-hand 8 pixels of a 16-pixel group. TWO operand references in the image, both inside $b61e: the `lea $b6e4.l,a1` at $b61e that opens it (the cursor the walk starts from) and the `lea $b6e4(pc),a0` at $b690 (41fa 0052), which reloads the base so the walk's distance can be turned into a cell count. The second is PC-RELATIVE and so invisible to the abs.l/abs.w byte scan the rest of these counts use -- see the block header. The array ends where hud_meter_max begins, at $b6f8.

fn 0xb6fe hud_meter_add_clamped
proto 0xb6fe hud_meter_add_clamped amount@D0
cmt 0xb6fe `add.w d0,$b6fa` -- a read-modify-write ON MEMORY, so the raised value is STORED before anything is tested -- then `move.w $b6f8,d1 / cmp.w $b6fa,d1 / ble` overwrites it with hud_meter_max. The general form of effect_add4_clamped_b6fa/effect_add2_clamped_b6fa, and NOT the same comparison: those use `bgt` on the value in a register and store max only when the raise passes it, this one clamps when the raise REACHES it. Both are signed 16-bit and both wrap. One caller, $6bf0.

fn 0xb74a hud_draw_score_and_size_meter
proto 0xb74a hud_draw_score_and_size_meter font_select@D0
cmt 0xb74a 124 bytes: `movea.l screen_back,a0 / adda.w #$a21,a0 / move.l bcd_score_bd70,d7 / bsr $b7ea` draws the eight digits, and then the score is RE-READ from memory and thresholded to set hud_meter_max -- `cmp.l #$400000,d7 / blt` and so on down through $300000/$200000/$100000/$30000, storing $28/$24/$20/$1c/$18 and returning on the first match, with nothing stored below $30000. Those compares are SIGNED, so a score whose top nibble is 8 or 9 reads NEGATIVE, matches none of the five and leaves the maximum untouched. Entry d7 is dead (the `move.l` overwrites it); entry d0 is NOT -- it reaches $b7ea's first digit, and panel_refresh_frame's `bsr $b74a` never sets it. ONE caller, $b354.

fn 0xb7c6 hud_draw_larger_score
proto 0xb7c6 hud_draw_larger_score font_select@D0
cmt 0xb7c6 36 bytes: `move.l bcd_score_bd70,d7 / cmp.l bcd_hiscore_bd74,d7 / bgt` keeps the score and otherwise reloads d7 with the high score, then `movea.l screen_back,a0 / lea 2633(a0),a0 / bsr $b7ea` draws eight digits at screen_back + $a49 (a `lea d16(a0)`, not the `adda.w` the other three fields use). The compare is SIGNED and STRICT: an EQUAL pair takes the high-score arm, which is unobservable because both fields then hold the same bytes, while a score with bit 31 set loses to any small high score. Entry d7 is dead; entry d0 reaches the first digit. ONE caller, $b358.

fn 0xb7ea hud_draw_eight_digits
proto 0xb7ea hud_draw_eight_digits font_select@D0 cursor@A0 digits@D7
cmt 0xb7ea 100 bytes: eight `bsr $b850` with the cursor stepped between them, alternating `suba.l #$4f9` (net +7) and `#$4ff` (net +1) and STARTING with the +7 -- the field begins on an odd byte, the right half of a 16-pixel group. A `moveq #$0,d0` follows each of the first six steps, so ONLY THE FIRST DIGIT sees the caller's d0 and the other seven use the default glyphs. `move.w #$ffff,digit_significant_seen_b84e` before the SEVENTH digit, so the last two always print, and `clr.w` after the eighth. TWO callers, $b758 (hud_draw_score_and_size_meter) and $b7e4 (hud_draw_larger_score).

fn 0xb850 hud_plot_digit
cmt 0xb850 REGISTER INTERFACE, and no `proto` line for two reasons (hud_blit_meter_cell has the first): it returns the advanced cursor in a0, which the directive forces to void, and its d7 is IN AND OUT. d0 = the font select, a0 = the cursor, d7 = the digit register; on return a0 is 1280 bytes lower (8 rows x 160), d7 has been ROTATED LEFT BY ONE NIBBLE, and d1/d6/a1 are clobbered. The rotation is now pinned DIRECTLY: recreate/test/test_hud.py compares the oracle's outgoing d7, the reconstruction's out-parameter and a rotate model on every one of the 45 cases. Body: `rol.l #4,d7 / move.l d7,d6 / andi.l #$f,d6` comes FIRST, so the digit drawn is the nibble that was at the TOP of d7 on entry; then 8 rows of `move.b (a1)+,{0,2,4,6}(a0)` with `adda.l #$a0,a0` -- the four plane bytes of one 8-pixel column. digit_significant_seen_b84e gates it: while that word is zero a zero nibble is drawn BLANK and no glyph is fetched, and the first non-zero nibble raises it to $ffff. The blank is not the same under both fonts -- four cleared planes when d0 = 1, but `st 2(a0)` (a solid $ff in plane 1) otherwise -- and the glyph table differs too ($145bc vs $1447c), so d0 selects both. SIXTEEN call sites, every one inside a routine that walks a field of digits left to right: $b5ec/$b5f6/$b600/$b612 in $b5ea, $b7ea/$b7f6/$b802/$b80e/$b81a/$b826/$b838/$b842 in $b7ea, $bd4a/$bd54 in $bd4a, and $b3f0/$b404 in $b3da (hud_draw_record_digits, the fourth walk and the only one that forces the ALTERNATE font). THE COUNT IS A WHOLE-IMAGE SCAN of every even offset in $3f8..$218d0 for bsr.s/bsr.w/bsr.l, jsr/jmp abs.l, jsr/jmp abs.w and jsr d16(pc); it cannot see a call made through a register or a jump table.
var 0xb84e digit_significant_seen_b84e
cmt 0xb84e The leading-zero latch hud_plot_digit reads: while it is zero a zero nibble is drawn as a blank instead of a glyph, and the first non-zero nibble sets it to $ffff so every digit after that prints. NINE operand references in the image, all abs.l (the address is above $7fff, so an abs.w encoding is impossible) and none PC-relative (a d16(pc) sweep over the whole image finds none): $b874 and $b8ca inside hud_plot_digit itself, then one raise and one `clr.w` in each of three of the field walks -- $b400/$b40a in $b3da, $b60e/$b618 in $b5ea, $b834/$b848 in $b7ea -- and $bd60 in $bd4a, which only CLEARS it. So every walk leaves it zero on exit, and nothing outside this cluster reads or writes it.
var 0x1447c digit_glyphs_1447c
cmt 0x1447c 32 bytes per glyph (8 rows x 4 planes, one 8-pixel column), indexed by the nibble hud_plot_digit draws -- so sixteen glyphs are addressable, of which the game can only ever reach 0..9, both fields it draws being packed BCD. ONE operand reference in the image, the `lea $1447c.l,a1` at $b862, i.e. the arm taken when d0 is not 1.
var 0x145bc digit_glyphs_145bc
cmt 0x145bc The glyphs hud_plot_digit uses when d0 = 1, and NOT a separate table: $145bc is $1447c + $140, exactly ten glyphs in, so this is the second ten of one twenty-glyph block. TWO operand references: the `lea` at $b858 inside hud_plot_digit and a `lea $145bc.l,a1` at $1d0a inside unrecovered code -- the only user of either table outside this cluster, and the reason the glyphs are named for their address rather than as "the digits".

fn 0xb8f0 hud_refresh_dirty_slots
cmt 0xb8f0 666 bytes, reconstructed in recreate/src/hud.c. Six slots keyed on the two-byte records at $bbbe/$bbc0/$bbc2/$bbc4/$bbc6/$bbc8, each handled the same way -- `tst.b 1(a0)` on the request byte and skip the slot when it is zero, else `clr.b` it, `st` the slot's restore flag ($dbb6..$dbbb, one per slot, in address order, the same array panel_restore_flag_dbb3 sits in), then hud_blit_cell_copy from a fixed blank cell ($1479c for the first three slots, $14bfc for the last three) followed by hud_blit_cell_or laying an icon over it. Which icon is byte 0 of the record: a two-way `tst.b (a0)` in the first five slots, a six-armed `cmpi.b #1..#6` chain under the same test in the sixth ($bafe..$bb62, whose #5 and #6 arms both `lea $152fc`, and whose fall-through leaves the cell blanked with NO icon). Destinations are screen_back + $3ca0/$4600/$4f60/$3cb0/$4610/$4f70 -- two columns (byte 0 and byte 16) of three rows (97, 112, 127), which is why the blanks come in a left and a right form. EIGHTEEN call sites of the pair -- six copies and twelve ORs -- each with both a0 and a1 reloaded ahead of it, so the advanced registers are dead. ONE caller, $b360 in panel_refresh_frame; both counts are the whole-image branch scan described on $b850. What a slot DEPICTS is not established.
var 0x1479c hud_slot_cells
cmt 0x1479c Fourteen consecutive 224-byte cells (16 bytes x 14 rows, the hud_blit_cell_copy geometry), every one of them `lea`d individually by $b8f0 and referenced from nowhere else in the image -- a whole-image abs.l scan finds all 14 addresses only at $b8f0's own `lea` sites. fn 0xbba0 hud_blit_cell_or

cmt 0xbb8a The two HUD-slot cell blits, identical in geometry and different in combine: 14 rows of 16 bytes (32 x 14 px) with `lea 144(a1),a1` between rows, i.e. a 160-byte scanline. $bb8a is 4 x `move.l (a0)+,(a1)+`; $bba0 is 4 x `move.l (a0)+,d1 / or.l d1,(a1)+`, so it READS the destination and a port that copied would agree with it only over a blank cell. $b8f0 pairs them per slot -- the copy with a blank source to clear the cell, then the OR with the slot's icon -- across 18 call sites, every one of which reloads both a0 and a1 immediately before the `bsr`, so the advanced registers (a0 +224, a1 +2240) and the spent d0/d1 are dead.

fn 0xbbca panel_frame_timers
cmt 0xbbca 268 bytes, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/hud.c, differential in recreate/test/test_hud.py) now that $1a48a is -- the blocker batch 3 registered here is CLOSED, and the scan gap that hid the sound call is diagnosed and fixed (batch 16a; every call is in exactly one ledger now, this site as `E 0xbbca 0x17b14 CALL`). Five words drive panel_frame_index 0..$c. panel_frame_rewind ($bd30) set: `addi.w #$14,$bd28` and, on a SIGNED `cmpi.w #$500 / blt`, clamp to $500 and clear panel_frame_hold and panel_frame_rewind. Then one of three arms. panel_frame_phase ($bd2a) set: panel_frame_dwell ($bd2e) counts down (one blit and nothing else); at zero, `cmpi.w #$a,$bd2c / bgt` -- SIGNED and strict, so a negative index fires too -- triggers SFX $f on channel A through snd_call_trigger_effect ($17b14, the `lea $17adc.l,a1 / jsr 56(a1)` at $bc9c/$bca2), dwell reloads to 4, a dead `clr.l d0`, a blit, and the index either wraps ($bd2c == $c clears the index AND the phase) or steps. Phase clear and $bd28 == 0: the restart -- phase := $ffff, dwell := 4, delay := $500, index := $a, and hud_meter_value spends 4 with `subq.w #4,d0 / bpl / clr.w d0`, whose branch tests the RESULT, so a value already negative comes back $7ffc rather than floored. Otherwise: index := `($500 - $bd28) asr.w 7`, a blit, and the delay ticks down unless panel_frame_hold is up. EVERY arm ends with hud_blit_panel_frame, which is why the d0 it leaves is what $bd32 is entered with.

fn 0xbcd6 hud_blit_panel_frame
cmt 0xbcd6 No arguments: `move.w $bd2c,d0 / mulu.w #$300,d0 / lea $153dc,a0 / lea (0,a0,d0.w),a0` then 32 rows of 24 bytes into screen_back + $5b40 (48 x 32 px), moved four rows per iteration as `movem.l (a0)+,d0-d5 / movem.l d0-d5,{0,160,320,480}(a1)` with `lea 640(a1),a1`. Same operand-size trap as hud_blit_record_bitmap: the `mulu` is 32-bit and the indexed `lea` keeps only d0's low word, sign-extended. Called from four sites inside $bbca, its own driver. IT ALSO LEAVES A LIVE REGISTER: the last `movem.l (a0)+,d0-d5` loads the FINAL row, so d0 comes out holding that row's first longword -- $bbca hands it straight on and panel_refresh_frame spends it as hud_draw_stage_number's font select, which is why the reconstruction returns it.

fn 0xbd32 hud_draw_stage_number
proto 0xbd32 hud_draw_stage_number font_select@D0 digits@D7
cmt 0xbd32 24 bytes: `movea.l screen_back,a0 / adda.w #$7311,a0 / move.w stage_number,d7 / swap d7 / rol.l #8,d7 / bsr $bd4a`. The swap and the rotate together leave stage_number's LOW BYTE in d7's top two nibbles, so the two digits drawn are that byte and the high byte is never displayed; the caller's own d7 high word ends up below both and cannot reach either. fn 0xd93a panel_restore_dirty_regions
cmt 0xd93a 446 bytes, reconstructed in recreate/src/hud.c: panel_refresh_frame's FIRST call, and the other half of every `st $dbbN` in this subsystem. `lea $dbb0.l,a6` then FIFTEEN identical entries, each `tst.b (a6)+ / beq next / clr.b -1(a6) / movea.l $74c.w,a0 / movea.l $750.w,a1 / lea origin(a0),a0 / lea origin(a1),a1` and, for TWELVE of them, a `bsr` to one of six routines -- eleven to a blit that copies that region from SCREEN_FRONT to SCREEN_BACK, and one ($1e08's) to $db34, which is a bare `rts`.
