var 0xdbb0 panel_restore_flags
cmt 0xdbb0 The 15-byte flag array panel_restore_dirty_regions walks, $dbb0..$dbbe. ONE operand reference in the whole image, the `lea $dbb0.l,a6` at $d93c that opens the walk; every other access to the array is `tst.b (a6)+ / clr.b -1(a6)` through that register, or an `st $dbbN.l` on a single entry. A whole-image abs.l scan for each of the fifteen bytes finds a writer for only EIGHT of them: $dbb3 ($b626), $dbb5 ($b3ba) and $dbb6..$dbbb ($b904/$b962/$b9c0/$ba1e/$ba7c/$badc). The other SEVEN -- $dbb0/$dbb1/$dbb2/$dbb4/$dbbc/$dbbd/$dbbe -- have NO writer in the image at all, and THREE of those seven are live entries that do blit ($dbbc, the panel frame's, and $dbbd/$dbbe), so nothing a scan can see ever triggers them.
var 0xdbb3 panel_restore_flag_dbb3
cmt 0xdbb3 One byte of the flag array at $dbb0. ONE operand reference in the whole image, the `st $dbb3.l` at $b626 inside hud_draw_meter, so the meter raises it and only $d93a consumes it; this is entry 3, whose region is the `lea 2720(a0)` pair at $d9a0/$d9a4 ($aa0, row 17) and whose blit is $db12's 20 rows of 32 bytes. That region does NOT cover the meter's top row -- meter_cell_offsets starts at $a01, row 16 -- so the pairing is close but not exact, which is why the restores are named for their geometry and not for a region.
var 0xdbb5 panel_restore_flag_dbb5
cmt 0xdbb5 Entry 5 of the flag array, region $2800 -- the record bitmap's own origin -- blitted by $db36 as 29 rows of 32 bytes. ONE operand reference in the image, the `st $dbb5.l` at $b3ba inside hud_draw_newest_record, i.e. raised on the same branch that draws the bitmap.
var 0xdbb6 panel_restore_flag_dbb6
var 0xdbb7 panel_restore_flag_dbb7
var 0xdbb8 panel_restore_flag_dbb8
var 0xdbb9 panel_restore_flag_dbb9
var 0xdbba panel_restore_flag_dbba
var 0xdbbb panel_restore_flag_dbbb
cmt 0xdbb6 Entries 6..11 of the flag array, one per HUD slot in the same order $b8f0 walks them, each raised by that slot's own `st` and each restoring that slot's cell ($3ca0/$4600/$4f60/$3cb0/$4610/$4f70) through $db58, whose 14 rows of 16 bytes ARE the hud_blit_cell_copy geometry. One operand reference each, all inside $b8f0.

fn 0xdaf8 panel_restore_44x8
proto 0xdaf8 panel_restore_44x8 source@A0 destination@A1
cmt 0xdaf8 26 bytes, reconstructed. 8 rows of 44 bytes, both cursors stepping a 160-byte scanline: one `movem.l (a0)+,d1-d7/a2-a5` (mask $3cfe, ELEVEN registers = 44 bytes) into `movem.l ...,(a1)`, then `lea 116(a0),a0` (44 + 116 = 160) and `lea 160(a1),a1`. TWO callers, both in $d93a: $dad4 (region $520) and $daf2 (region $570). Neither region has a draw anywhere in the recovered code, and neither flag ($dbbd/$dbbe) has a writer in the image.
fn 0xdb12 panel_restore_32x20
proto 0xdb12 panel_restore_32x20 source@A0 destination@A1
cmt 0xdb12 34 bytes, reconstructed. 20 rows of 32 bytes (8 x `move.l (a0)+,(a1)+` then `lea 128` on BOTH cursors, i.e. a 160-byte scanline each). ONE caller, $d9a8, the entry panel_restore_flag_dbb3 drives.
fn 0xdb34 panel_restore_none
cmt 0xdb34 TWO BYTES: a bare `rts`, and a routine rather than an absent call -- $d93a's entry 4 (the counter's region, $1e08) `bsr`s it at $d9c6, its only caller. So that entry clears its flag and restores nothing. Whether it is a stub or the remains of a blit that was removed is not established; no `fn` boundary evidence either way beyond the `bsr`.
fn 0xdb36 panel_restore_32x29
proto 0xdb36 panel_restore_32x29 source@A0 destination@A1
cmt 0xdb36 34 bytes, reconstructed. Identical to $db12 bar the row count: 29 rows of 32 bytes. ONE caller, $d9e4 -- the $2800 entry, the record bitmap's origin, where hud_blit_record_bitmap draws THIRTY-TWO rows. The three-row shortfall is the original's, and is reproduced.
fn 0xdb58 panel_restore_16x14
proto 0xdb58 panel_restore_16x14 source@A0 destination@A1
cmt 0xdb58 26 bytes, reconstructed. 14 rows of 16 bytes (4 x `move.l (a0)+,(a1)+`, `lea 144` on both cursors) -- exactly hud_blit_cell_copy's geometry, with the source stepping a scanline instead of running contiguously. SIX callers, all in $d93a ($da02, $da20, $da3e, $da5c, $da7a, $da98), one per HUD slot.
fn 0xdb72 panel_restore_24x32
proto 0xdb72 panel_restore_24x32 source@A0 destination@A1
cmt 0xdb72 62 bytes, reconstructed. 32 rows of 24 bytes -- hud_blit_panel_frame's geometry -- moved four rows per iteration as `movem.l {0,160,320,480}(a0),d0-d5 / movem.l d0-d5,{...}(a1)` with `lea 640` on both cursors, so unlike $daf8's the source `movem` is NOT post-incrementing. ONE caller, $dab6, the $5b40 entry.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Pseudo-random number generator  (found by the portability measurement --
# see recreate/PORTABILITY.md; it is the game logic's ONLY hardware access)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x51ac bcd_add_random_1_to_4
cmt 0x51ac RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 33). 44 bytes, $51ac..$51d7. RENAMED FROM rng_1_to_4_masked, WHICH WAS WRONG, and the mistake is one instruction: tools/prg_dis.py printed the `c101` at $51d4 as `and.b d0,d1`, but opmode 100 with an ea mode of 000 is `abcd Dy,Dx` -- the SAME decoder gap this file documents for $b562's `c308`/`8308`, FIXED at batch 33 for the whole two-register family (a checked-out out/wonderboy_dis.txt predating that fix still shows the wrong line) -- so the routine does not MASK the caller's d0, it adds to it in packed BCD, and the result is in d0 rather than d1. Ghidra agrees (`bcdAdjust` in decomp.c). Body: `moveq #0,d1`, then four byte adds into d1 -- $ff8209, $ff8207, $9aec and $9aed, IN THAT ORDER -- `andi.b #$3,d1 / addq.b #1,d1` for a draw of 1..4, and `abcd d1,d0`. So d0 := d0 + draw, packed BCD, and only d0's LOW BYTE is written: both the low word's high byte and the register's high half come back as the caller left them. THE EXTEND BIT IT FOLDS IN IS ITS OWN -- `addq.b #1` on a byte masked to 0..3 always clears X -- but the one it LEAVES is the BCD carry out, which its caller's next `abcd` reads (see $517a); recreate/src/behavior.c hands that bit back through an out-parameter since batch 33. Its only machine entropy is the SHIFTER'S VIDEO ADDRESS COUNTER ($ff8207/$ff8209), which the kit's Phase 7 seeded-hardware model MODELS since batch 33: both bytes are declared per case with `hw_seed=`, served identically to the two cores and compared as an ordered read stream, so the result is a function of the declaration plus the two state bytes at $9aec/$9aed (the followed record's x, high byte then low) rather than of a fabricated 0. They are VOLATILE slots -- one declaration describes ONE read of each, and this routine reads each exactly once, so a per-run constant describes one CALL faithfully; a caller running it twice a frame would be refused (TRAP_MODEL.md, "Phase 7"). Named here because Ghidra never reached this routine on its own (nothing it disassembled calls $5184 either), and an `fn` line is what makes it disassemble -- see recreate/PORTABILITY.md, which was counting these two hardware sites by hand until it was added. TWO `bsr` callers, $5184 and $544c.
fn 0x68c6 rng_next
cmt 0x68c6 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/rng.c, differential in recreate/test/test_rng.py). The game's PRNG. Advances three free-running counters at $6932/$6934/$6936, then returns `($ff8209 ^ $b39a) + $6932 + $6934 + $6936` in d0. $ff8209 is the SHIFTER'S VIDEO ADDRESS COUNTER (low byte, the fastest-changing one -- $ff8205/8207/8209 are high/mid/low) -- the only genuine entropy in the mix, and the only hardware access anywhere in the game-logic subsystem. THAT FALSE GREEN IS RETIRED (batch 33): $ff8209 is now a MODELED hardware byte in the kit's Phase 7 table (OS_HW_SHIFTER_VCOUNT_LOW), so it is served from what the case DECLARES, it lands on the ordered read ledger both sides compare, and an undeclared read REFUSES the differential instead of being answered 0. Until then every off-image read returned 0, the term vanished on BOTH sides, the generator degenerated to a deterministic function of the three counters and the diff still came back clean -- a T3-DATA false green that silently removed the game's randomness. What a declared byte still does not buy is a counter that ADVANCES: this routine reads it once per call, so the constant is faithful per call and not across a frame. NOT MODULO $25/$17/$11, which an earlier revision of this plate said and batch 21b corrected from the bytes: each counter is `addq.w #1 / cmpi.w #N / bne / clr.w`, i.e. cleared when it REACHES N, so a counter seeded at or above its limit never meets it again and runs on to $ffff and round. The distinction is unreachable from the game's own state (all three start at 0) and is a case in recreate/test/test_rng.py. A second consequence of the OLD degeneracy, also retired: with the $ff8209 term at 0 the XOR was unobservable AS AN OPERATOR -- `0 ^ tick` is `0 + tick` -- so a port that added here was green too. The battery now declares five different counter bytes against one seeded state, which separates the two.
var 0x6932 rng_counter_a  # cleared when it REACHES WB_RNG_LIMIT_A ($25), not modulo it
var 0x6934 rng_counter_b  # ...and at WB_RNG_LIMIT_B ($17)
var 0x6936 rng_counter_c  # ...and at WB_RNG_LIMIT_C ($11)
fn 0xe1c8 stage_random_kind32
cmt 0xe1c8 RECONSTRUCTED in batch 22 (recreate/src/rng.c, differential in recreate/test/test_rng.py). 40 bytes ($e1c8..$e1ef). THE SAME ROUTINE AS stage_random_kind8 with three operands changed and no tail of its own: `lea $e222.l,a2` (its own candidate table, stage_kind32_table, which ends exactly where stage_kind_table begins and BEGINS exactly where stage_random_kind8's body ends -- 11 rows, half the 8-wide table's 22), `lsl.w #5` for a 32-byte row, `andi.l #$1f,d0` for a draw of 32 rather than 8 -- then `bra.w $e214` INTO stage_random_kind8's tail, so the fourteen bytes $e214..$e221 (`add.l d2,d0 / move.b 0(a2,d0.l),d0 / andi.l #$1f,d0 / rts`) belong to BOTH routines and recreate/src/rng.c spells them once, as a static body with three parameters. The BCD tens carry above it is stage_random_kind8's, instruction for instruction. ONE caller, `bsr.w` from $6cf2 inside actor_respawn_as_new_kind -- which calls $e1f0 at $6d04 as well, on the LAST respawn a template is allowed. Its `add.l d2,d0` has the same 24-BIT ADDRESS BUS exposure $e1f0's does, and recreate/test/test_rng.py pins the wrap AND the mask's width from this side too. (objdump misaligns here: the four bytes at $e1c6 swallow the `lea` unless disassembly starts at $e1c8.)
fn 0xe1f0 stage_random_kind8
cmt 0xe1f0 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/rng.c, differential in recreate/test/test_rng.py). 50 bytes ($e1f0..$e221). Draw one of the EIGHT candidates stage_kind_table ($e382) holds for the current stage_number, masked to 5 bits: `lea $e382.l,a2 / move.w $bd88,d2 / cmp.w #9 / ble / subq.w #6 / subq.w #1 / lsl.w #3 / bsr rng_next / andi.l #$7,d0 / add.l d2,d0 / move.b 0(a2,d0.l),d0 / andi.l #$1f,d0 / rts`. THE `subq.w #6` IS A BCD TENS CARRY -- stage_number is packed BCD, so $10..$19 become 10..19 (hud_draw_stage_number drawing that word's low byte as two digits is the other half of the reading). ONE caller, $6d04, inside actor_respawn_as_new_kind ($6cdc, reconstructed in batch 22): it stores the result at 20(a0) = actor_kind and indexes the 16-byte rows of actor_kind_table ($1044c) with it for the record's new type and sprite, so the value is which creature the slot comes back as -- and it is the LAST of a template's respawns that comes here, the earlier ones going to $e1c8. d2's HIGH half is load-bearing and never written -- every step on the row is a `.w` and then `add.l d2,d0` folds it into the table index -- though the one caller reaches here with d2's HIGH half zeroed by the `moveq #0,d2` at $6c14 and its low word holding the scaled spawn type the `lsl.w #2` left there, which this routine's `move.w $bd88.l,d2` overwrites -- so the effective entry d2 is 0, but not because a `moveq` immediately precedes the call, which an earlier revision of this plate implied. That index then goes onto the 68000's 24-BIT ADDRESS BUS: an entry d2 of $01000000 or more wraps back round into the image rather than off it, which recreate/src/rng.c masks for (WB_BUS_ADDR_MASK) and recreate/test/test_rng.py pins with a case that reads the same byte a d2 of 0 does.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# BACKGROUND SCROLL ENGINE ($7522..$8228) -- what produces the buffers the
# blitter below copies to the screen. Fifteen routines, no hardware access
# anywhere in the range (the hw scan reports none), and every callee inside it.
#
# The game keeps EIGHT pre-shifted copies of the level background, $5800 bytes
# each, tiling $44000..$70000. Copy N is the same map drawn two pixels further
# left than copy N-1, so a two-pixel horizontal scroll is a change of BUFFER and
# not a redraw, and only the one tile column a scroll uncovers has to be drawn.
# bg_scroll_preshift_rows is the other half of that scheme: it propagates a
# freshly drawn ROW through all eight copies, `rol.l #2` at a time.
#
# The shape is a request queue drained once a frame:
#   game_main_loop -> bg_scroll_run_queue ($7522, the image's only caller)
#     -> bg_scroll_raise_requests ($d28) raises up/down/left/right
#     -> N x bg_scroll_serve_requests ($759a), which dispatches each raised
#        request byte to a serve routine that CONSUMES it
#     -> serve = step (move the position words) + fill (draw the new edge)
# A step with nothing to do does `addq.l #4,(a7)` / `addq.l #8,(a7)` and `rts`,
# returning PAST its caller's `bsr`s to the fill -- so "no movement" and "skip
# the redraw" are one act. Ghidra models none of it (decomp.c shows a bare
# `return`), which is why the skip is stated on every step below.
#
# Verified byte for byte in recreate/: the six HORIZONTAL routines ($75fc,
# $760c, $795e, $79d2, $7c08, $7eb2). The vertical half and the queue above it
# are named here from the same reading and queued in recreate/STATUS.md.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0xd28 bg_scroll_raise_requests
cmt 0xd28 Compares the two words at $9934/$9936 against $5a and $30 and raises one horizontal and one vertical request byte accordingly ($7598 when $9934 is below $5a, $7599 when above; $7596 when $9936 is below $30, $7597 when above), returning the two distances from those centres in d1 and d0 -- negated so both come back positive. It carries all four of those `st` instructions in the image, which is what the name is read off. Its only caller is bg_scroll_run_queue, which halves both distances and drains that many two-pixel steps, so the effect is to chase whatever $9934/$9936 track towards the middle of the screen; that they are the player's position is a reading of that arithmetic and not something either routine proves. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
var 0x9934 scroll_follow_x
cmt 0x9934 Word pair with $9936: the followed object's position ON SCREEN, which is why bg_scroll_raise_requests compares them against $5a/$30 -- roughly the middle of a 320x200 display. $f9ae computes them from the object at $fe1a as `x - $20 - bg_scroll_pos_x` and `y - $40 - bg_scroll_pos_y`, i.e. map position minus the scroll, and skips that update while $d76 is set. Six abs.l references for $9934 and four for $9936; $512 and $538 read and write the two AS ONE LONGWORD, which is what fixes them as a pair.
var 0x9936 scroll_follow_y
var 0xd76 scroll_follow_frozen
cmt 0xd76 Word, NINE operand references in the image, listed here by the address of the INSTRUCTION: one abs.l ($af6) and eight abs.w -- it is below $8000, so both spellings reach it -- and no pc-relative one. Only TWO of them test it, the `tst.w $d76.w` at $7522 (bg_scroll_run_queue, which then serves whatever is already raised and raises nothing new) and the one at $f9a6 (which then leaves scroll_follow_x/_y stale), and the name is read off exactly those two. The other SEVEN are five `move.w #$ffff` ($af6, $18a0, $1ad0, $1e60, $1f0e) and two `clr.w` ($e002, $ff08); this batch did not read those routines, so what SETS the flag is unclaimed.
fn 0x7522 bg_scroll_run_queue
cmt 0x7522 Once a frame from game_main_loop ($4d0, the image's ONLY reference to this address). If the word at $d76 is nonzero it just runs bg_scroll_serve_requests once and returns. Otherwise it calls bg_scroll_raise_requests, halves both returned distances (`asr.w #1` on d0 and d1), and drains them: d1 two-pixel steps of the HORIZONTAL request word ($7598 -> $8232) and then d0 of the VERTICAL one ($7596 -> $8230), each followed by bg_scroll_serve_requests, then `clr.l` over both request words. Note both moves are WORD moves of a byte PAIR, which is how the down/right bytes ($7597/$7599 -> $8231/$8233) are raised at all -- they have no writer of their own anywhere in the image. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
var 0x7592 bg_scroll_queue_h_count
cmt 0x7592 Word: how many two-pixel HORIZONTAL steps bg_scroll_run_queue still owes. Four operand references, all inside $7522. $7594 (the vertical count) is the second half of the `clr.l $7592` that ends the drain.
var 0x7594 bg_scroll_queue_v_count
var 0x7596 bg_scroll_request_v
cmt 0x7596 Byte pair, set by bg_scroll_raise_requests and copied AS A WORD into bg_scroll_request_up/$8230: $7596 = up, $7597 = down. $7598/$7599 are the horizontal pair, copied into $8232/$8233 the same way. Each has exactly one `st` in the whole image and the `clr.l` at $757e/$7584 is the only other writer.
var 0x7598 bg_scroll_request_h
fn 0x759a bg_scroll_serve_requests
cmt 0x759a Tests the four request bytes in the order up ($8230), down ($8231), right ($8233), left ($8232) and calls the matching serve routine for each that is set. Not a dispatch table: four `tst.b`/`beq`/`bsr` in line. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x75d4 bg_scroll_serve_up
cmt 0x75d4 Clears bg_scroll_request_up, then `bsr` bg_scroll_step_up / bg_scroll_fill_top_row / bg_scroll_preshift_rows. The step's `addq.l #8,(a7)` skips BOTH of the others. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x75e8 bg_scroll_serve_down
cmt 0x75e8 The mirror: clears bg_scroll_request_down, then bg_scroll_step_down / bg_scroll_fill_bottom_row / bg_scroll_preshift_rows, and the step skips both. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x75fc bg_scroll_serve_right
cmt 0x75fc Clears bg_scroll_request_right, then bg_scroll_step_right / bg_scroll_fill_right_column. The step's `addq.l #4,(a7)` skips the fill. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x760c bg_scroll_serve_left
cmt 0x760c Clears bg_scroll_request_left, then bg_scroll_step_left / bg_scroll_fill_left_column, with the same one-call skip. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x761c bg_scroll_step_up
cmt 0x761c Skips its caller's next TWO calls (`addq.l #8,(a7)`) when bg_scroll_pos_y is already 0; otherwise steps it back by 2 and then runs the SAME block twice, once per ring cursor: bg_scroll_y with the eight even bg_scroll_buffer_rows pointers ($82a6 + 8k), then bg_scroll_y_bottom with the eight odd ones ($82aa + 8k). Each block reloads -- row = $ae, pointers = $44000 + k * $5800 + $5700 (i.e. $49700, $4ef00, ...) -- when its own row is ALREADY 0, and otherwise decrements the row by 2 and its eight pointers by $100. The invariant both keep is pointer = buffer + row * 128. Ends by republishing bg_scroll_y_coarse = bg_scroll_y >> 4, from the top cursor only. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c. NOTE, against an earlier reading of this routine: the two cursors reload to the SAME eight addresses (there is no $49700/$49800 split), and there is no half-rate latch on this axis.
fn 0x77ba bg_scroll_step_down
cmt 0x77ba The mirror of bg_scroll_step_up: skips two calls when bg_scroll_pos_y has reached bg_scroll_limit_y, else steps everything the other way, each cursor wrapping when its own row IS ALREADY $ae (`cmpi.w`, where the up step's test is a `tst.w` against 0) -- row = 0 and pointers = $44000 + k * $5800. Ends with the same bg_scroll_y_coarse republish. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x795e bg_scroll_step_left
cmt 0x795e Skips its caller's fill when bg_scroll_pos_x is already 0. Otherwise, if bg_scroll_step_pending is nonzero it CLEARS it and returns without moving (cancelling the half-step bg_scroll_step_right armed); else it steps bg_scroll_pos_x and bg_scroll_phase down by 2, and on the phase underflowing writes $e outright, decrements bg_scroll_map_cursor and bg_scroll_x, and moves bg_scroll_row_byte_offset back one 8-byte cell (masked to $7f, and zeroed outright when bg_scroll_x wraps to 0). Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x79d2 bg_scroll_step_right
cmt 0x79d2 The mirror: skips the fill when bg_scroll_pos_x equals bg_scroll_limit_x, arms bg_scroll_step_pending with $ffff on any NON-NEGATIVE latch (where $795e clears any NONZERO one -- the two tests are not complements), and otherwise steps everything up, letting the phase wrap through the nibble mask and leaving bg_scroll_row_byte_offset unmasked. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
var 0x7a3c bg_scroll_step_pending
cmt 0x7a3c Word: the half-rate latch bg_scroll_step_left and bg_scroll_step_right share, so the background advances on every SECOND request. Exactly four operand references in the image, all four inside those two routines -- nothing else arms, clears or reads it.
fn 0x7a3e bg_scroll_fill_top_row
cmt 0x7a3e Draws the tile row bg_scroll_step_up uncovered: steps bg_scroll_tile_row back 16 bytes (two scanlines of a 128-byte tile, wrapping at $7f and pulling bg_scroll_map_cursor back one map row when it does), then walks the map row at $22094 + cursor with `move.l (a0)+,(a1)+` pairs into the buffer row at bg_scroll_buffer_rows[0] + bg_scroll_row_byte_offset. Two halves, lengths out of bg_scroll_row_split_table[bg_scroll_x], for the buffer's horizontal wrap. Returns d0's low word = 0, which is what bg_scroll_preshift_rows tests. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x7b1a bg_scroll_fill_bottom_row
cmt 0x7b1a The mirror of bg_scroll_fill_top_row, reading the map row ten strides further down ($22094 + cursor + 10 * $22090) into bg_scroll_buffer_rows[1], and advancing bg_scroll_tile_row by 16 AFTER the draw rather than before it. Returns d0's low word = $ffff. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x7c08 bg_scroll_fill_right_column
cmt 0x7c08 Draws the tile column bg_scroll_step_right uncovered, into the pre-shifted buffer bg_scroll_phase names. Masks the 8-byte cell at bg_scroll_x + $e of every scanline with bg_scroll_edge_masks[phase], then walks the map column at $22092 + bg_scroll_map_cursor + $11 downwards, rotating each tile's four plane words LEFT as longwords by the phase (or by a whole 16 with the map cursor pulled back one, when the phase is 0): the high half is ORed into the cell that stays, the low half overwrites the cell 8 bytes on. Two halves, lengths out of bg_scroll_col_split_table[bg_scroll_y_coarse], for the buffer's vertical ring wrap. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
var 0x7eae bg_scroll_fill_counts
cmt 0x7eae Longword scratch in the code hole between $7c08's rts and $7eb2's entry: the pair of `dbf` counts both column fills copy WHOLE out of bg_scroll_col_split_table and then read back a word at a time. $7eb2 addresses it in absolute SHORT form and $7c08 in absolute long, which is the only difference between the two spellings. Six operand references, all inside those two routines.
fn 0x7eb2 bg_scroll_fill_left_column
cmt 0x7eb2 The mirror of bg_scroll_fill_right_column: no bias on bg_scroll_x, the map column at $22092 + cursor + 2 (fifteen cells to the left of $7c08's), the mask INVERTED while the phase is nonzero, the overwritten cell 8 bytes BEFORE the ORed one rather than after, and the two halves of the rotated longword swapped over. It has no phase-0 special case, so at phase 0 it clears the cell and ORs the unrotated tile straight back in. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
fn 0x8144 bg_scroll_preshift_rows
cmt 0x8144 Propagates a freshly drawn buffer row through the seven remaining pre-shifted copies. d0's sign picks the source row pointer (bg_scroll_buffer_rows[0] for a non-negative d0, [1] otherwise -- which is exactly what the two row fills' return values select); a1 is the same row $5800 on, i.e. the next copy. Three nested loops: 7 copies x 2 rows x 16 cells, each cell's four plane words `rol.l #2` with the high half ORed into the previous cell and the low half written to the next, and the row's LAST carry-out parked in $8228..$822f and ORed into its first cell. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
var 0x8228 bg_scroll_preshift_carry
cmt 0x8228 Four words, written and read only by bg_scroll_preshift_rows: the four plane words that rotate off the end of a row and back round to its start.
var 0x8230 bg_scroll_request_up
cmt 0x8230 Four request bytes, tested in bg_scroll_serve_requests and cleared by the serve routine each one selects: $8230 up, $8231 down, $8232 left, $8233 right. An abs.l scan of the image finds a writer for only $8230 and $8232 -- the two `move.w $759x.l,$823x.l` in bg_scroll_run_queue -- and those are WORD moves, so $8231 and $8233 are raised only as the low halves of them. Nothing else in the image writes any of the four.
var 0x8231 bg_scroll_request_down
var 0x8232 bg_scroll_request_left
var 0x8233 bg_scroll_request_right
var 0x8234 bg_scroll_tile_row
cmt 0x8234 Word: byte offset into a 128-byte tile bitmap, 0..$7f in steps of $10 -- the two scanlines a vertical step redraws. Seven operand references: the two row fills step it, the two column fills add it to their tile pointer, and $fb6c clears it with the rest of the scroll state.
var 0x8236 bg_scroll_row_split_table
cmt 0x8236 16 pairs of words, indexed by bg_scroll_x * 4 (`lea $8236.l,a0 / lsl.w #2,d7 / lea (0,a0,d7.w),a0`). Entry k is (15-k, k-1), so the two row-fill halves always sum to 14 `dbf` iterations plus 2, i.e. 16 tile cells, and entry 0's second word is -1 = no second half. Reached only by that `lea`; no abs.l reference names its interior.
var 0x8276 bg_scroll_y_coarse
cmt 0x8276 Word: bg_scroll_y >> 4, the tile row of the buffer the visible window starts at. Written by the two vertical steps, read by the two column fills, cleared at $fb72. bg_scroll_col_split_table begins in the very next word, and both fills reach that table by `lea $8276.l,a5 / move.w (a5)+,d0` -- so a var line on $8278 alone would have no operand reference to justify it.
var 0x8278 bg_scroll_col_split_table
cmt 0x8278 11 pairs of words indexed by bg_scroll_y_coarse * 4. Entry k is (10-k, k-1): the two halves of a column fill always sum to 9 `dbf` iterations plus 2, i.e. the buffer's 11 tile rows, and entry 0's second word is -1 = no wrap needed.
var 0x82a4 bg_scroll_row_byte_offset
cmt 0x82a4 Word: byte offset of the seam within a 128-byte buffer row, stepped by 8 with bg_scroll_x. bg_scroll_step_left masks it to $7f and bg_scroll_step_right does not; both zero it outright when bg_scroll_x wraps to 0. Eight operand references, the last being $fb78's clear.
var 0x82a6 bg_scroll_buffer_rows
cmt 0x82a6 Eight PAIRS of longwords, one pair per pre-shifted buffer, $8 apart: the two buffer rows the vertical half draws into and bg_scroll_preshift_rows propagates. bg_scroll_step_up/_down move all sixteen by $100 (two 128-byte scanlines) or reload them with the eight $5800-spaced buffer addresses; pair member 0 follows bg_scroll_y and member 1 bg_scroll_y_bottom, so the invariant is $44000 + k * $5800 + row * 128. $fb96..$fc3a starts them at rows 0 and $9e, i.e. 158 scanlines apart -- the visible 160-line window's two ends. ONLY PAIR 0 IS EVER READ: an abs.l scan gives $82a6 and $82aa seven references each (four in the steps, one in the matching row fill, one in bg_scroll_preshift_rows, one in the init) and $82ae..$82e2 only the steps' and the init's, because the pre-shift chains from one copy to the next by adding $5800 rather than by reading the next pointer. Pairs 1..7 are bookkeeping nothing consumes.
var 0x82e6 bg_scroll_map_cursor
cmt 0x82e6 Word: byte offset into the map of the visible window's top-left cell. A horizontal step moves it by 1 and a vertical one by a whole map row (the word at $22090). Nine operand references; $fb3e is the only writer outside this cluster.
var 0x82e8 bg_scroll_edge_masks
cmt 0x82e8 Eight words indexed by bg_scroll_phase used AS A BYTE OFFSET (0,2,..,14): $0000 $fffc $fff0 $ffc0 $ff00 $fc00 $f000 $c000. That is `$ffff << phase` for every entry but the first, which is $0000 rather than $ffff -- so at phase 0 a column fill clears the whole cell and redraws it. Both column fills duplicate the word into both halves of a longword with `swap` and mask two longwords per scanline with it.
var 0x83ae bg_scroll_pos_x
cmt 0x83ae Word: the absolute horizontal scroll, stepped +/-2 by bg_scroll_step_left/_right and bounded below by 0 and above by bg_scroll_limit_x. bg_scroll_phase is its low nibble and bg_scroll_x the next one up. Eight operand references; $fb48 sets it and $f9b6 subtracts it from something.
var 0x83b0 bg_scroll_pos_y
cmt 0x83b0 The vertical counterpart, stepped +/-2 by bg_scroll_step_up/_down against bg_scroll_limit_y. bg_scroll_y is the position within the 176-row ring buffer and this is the absolute one.
var 0x83b2 bg_scroll_limit_x
cmt 0x83b2 Word: where bg_scroll_pos_x stops, by `cmp.w` and not by `bge` -- so a limit the position steps PAST is never reached and the scroll runs on. Three operand references: bg_scroll_step_right's compare, a read at $11c4 and the write at $fb1c that sets it up with the rest of the scroll state.
var 0x83b4 bg_scroll_limit_y
var 0x1d43e bg_tile_bitmaps
cmt 0x1d43e Tile bitmaps, $80 bytes each (16 scanlines x 4 plane words), addressed as base + tile * $80 by all four fills and by bg_scroll_preshift_rows' three siblings at $dfe4/$e684/$f8a4. Twelve `lea` references and one `cmpa.l`; the last one, at $f95c, is what bounds the region.
var 0x21e90 bg_tile_index
cmt 0x21e90 256 words: map byte -> tile number, indexed by `add.w d0,d0` on the byte the map holds. It ends exactly where bg_map_row_stride begins, which is what fixes its length. Eleven `lea` references, eight of them the four fills'.
var 0x22090 bg_map_row_stride
cmt 0x22090 Word: bytes per map row, and the LEVEL MAP's own header -- scene_exit_and_reload's `lea $22090.l,a0` at $dfde hands this address to stage_load_window as the map, so for that caller the header width word and the global stride word are ONE word (every other call site of $f95c passes a map elsewhere). Bytes per map row: Added to a map pointer to walk DOWN a column (both column fills), multiplied by 10 to reach the window's bottom row (bg_scroll_fill_bottom_row), and added to or subtracted from bg_scroll_map_cursor by the two row fills.
var 0x22092 bg_map_data
cmt 0x22092 The map, one byte per cell, immediately after its own stride word. Both column fills reach it by `lea $22092.l,a3` and both row fills by `lea $22094.l,a6`, i.e. the same array indexed from its second byte -- the two spellings differ by the 2 that bg_scroll_fill_left_column adds to the cursor anyway.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Background scroll blitter: the SECOND unrolled blit family, behind a jump
# table -- exactly the failure docs/methodology.md records for $8fce..$989c.
# Found by re-reading the coverage gap the portability measurement admits to
# (recreate/PORTABILITY.md 8.1): $8366..$8dfe was in no Ghidra function at all,
# because the only way in is `movea.l (0,a2,d1.w),a2 / jmp (a2)`.
#
# game_main_loop ($4a0) calls bg_scroll_blit once a frame. It builds
#   a1 = screen_back + $1420                          (destination)
#   a0 = $44000 + bg_scroll_phase*$2c00 + bg_scroll_y*128 + bg_scroll_x*8
#   a2 = bg_scroll_blit_table[bg_scroll_x]            (0..15)
# then splits the 160 destination scanlines around the source buffer's
# vertical wrap: d7+1 rows before it and, if d6 >= 0, d6+1 rows after a
# `lea -$5800(a0),a0` rewind. BOTH HALVES RUN INSIDE THE VARIANT -- the two
# `jmp (a2)` at $8350/$8364 are the two arms of `subi.w #$10,d6 / bpl`, one
# loading d6 = $ffff outright (the window fits: no second half) and the other
# computing d7 and d6 from the ring row, and exactly ONE of them executes per
# call. An earlier reading of this block had them entering the variant twice;
# they do not, and the `tst.w d6 / bpl / rts` inside every variant is what makes
# the second half conditional.
#
# Each variant copies 30 longwords (120 bytes = 240 px of a 4-plane line) per
# scanline out of a 128-byte-wide circular row, then `lea 40(a1),a1` to finish
# a 160-byte screen line. Variant x is the one whose unrolled `move.l (a0)+,(a1)+`
# run is split where byte offset 8*x makes the source row wrap:
#   x=0,1  30 moves, no wrap (8*x + 120 <= 128), then a0 += 8
#   x>=2   (32-2x) moves, `lea -128(a0),a0`, (2x-2) moves, then a0 += 136
#          (the two runs always sum to 30: x=2 -> 28+2, x=8 -> 16+14, x=15 -> 2+28)
# so the 16 bodies tile $83b6..$8dfe exactly, 2 x 154 + 14 x 166 = 2632 bytes.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x82f8 bg_scroll_blit
cmt 0x82f8 Per-frame background scroll blit, called from game_main_loop -- an abs.l scan of the whole image gives $82f8 exactly ONE reference, the `jsr` at $4dc. Reads the three scroll variables that live in the code hole just above the first variant, picks one of the 16 unrolled copy routines out of bg_scroll_blit_table, and jumps into it. CORRECTED: it enters that routine ONCE, not once per half -- the two `jmp (a2)` at $8350/$8364 are the two arms of `subi.w #$10,d6 / bpl`, and the vertical split is done INSIDE the variant by its own `tst.w d6 / bpl / rts`. Note the `bpl` reads N alone, so that test really is the wrapped difference's sign, unlike the `bgt`/`blt` pair in bg_scroll_raise_requests. Ghidra cannot follow either jump, which is why $83b6..$8dfe was in no function until these names were added. Reconstructed in recreate/src/scroll.c.
var 0x8366 bg_scroll_blit_table
cmt 0x8366 16 longwords: $83b6 $8450 $84ea $8590 $8636 $86dc $8782 $8828 $88ce $8974 $8a1a $8ac0 $8b66 $8c0c $8cb2 $8d58 -- indexed by bg_scroll_x (0..15) via `movea.l (0,a2,d1.w),a2` at $8336 with d1 = bg_scroll_x * 4. An abs.l scan of the image finds ONE reference to the table (the `lea $8366.l,a2` at $832e) and exactly ONE to each of the sixteen targets -- its own entry here -- so this table is the only way into the family and $832e is its only reader. The indexing is unbounded, so a bg_scroll_x outside 0..15 would jump through the longword AFTER the table, which is bg_scroll_x itself; the three writers of that word all keep it in a nibble, which is what makes the domain 0..15.
var 0x83a6 bg_scroll_x
cmt 0x83a6 Horizontal scroll position in 16-pixel cells, 0..15 (`addq/subq #1` then `andi.w #$f` at $79b6..$7a26). Selects the bg_scroll_blit_table entry AND, times 8, the source byte offset within a 128-byte row.
var 0x83a8 bg_scroll_y
cmt 0x83a8 Vertical scroll position as a row index into the 176-row source buffer, stepped +/-2 and wrapped at $ae/0 ($763a..$783a). Times 128 it is the source row offset; $8340 subtracts $10 from it to find where the visible 160-line window crosses the buffer's end. Twelve operand references: eight in the two vertical steps, three in bg_scroll_blit, and the clear at $fb84.
var 0x83aa bg_scroll_y_bottom
cmt 0x83aa The SECOND ring cursor, for the visible window's bottom scanline pair: the same row index, wrapped by its own test inside the same two steps, governing the odd half of bg_scroll_buffer_rows ($82aa + 8k). Seven operand references, all six of them in bg_scroll_step_up/_down plus the `move.w #$9e,$83aa` at $fb96 that starts it 158 rows below bg_scroll_y. Nothing else reads it -- in particular bg_scroll_y_coarse is published from bg_scroll_y alone.
var 0x83ac bg_scroll_phase
cmt 0x83ac Selects the source scroll buffer: even, 0..14 (`addq/subq #2` then `andi.w #$f`), scaled by $2c00 at $830c, i.e. eight $5800 buffers over $44000..$70000 -- the map recreate/project.toml records. Renamed from bg_scroll_buffer (which was tagged # ctx) once the routines that FILL those buffers were read: it is not only a buffer selector but the pre-shift in pixels, and the two column fills use it three ways at once -- to pick the buffer, to index bg_scroll_edge_masks, and as the `rol.l` count that shifts the tiles by that many pixels.
fn 0x83b6 bg_scroll_copy_x0
cmt 0x83b6 Variant 0 of 16. 30 x `move.l (a0)+,(a1)+` then `lea 40(a1),a1 / addq.l #8,a0 / dbf d7`; `tst.w d6 / bpl` then re-runs the same body from $8404 under `dbf d6` after `lea -$5800(a0),a0`, which is the source buffer's vertical wrap. All 16 variants have this two-half shape, and the family is ONE PATTERN with one number in it: recreate/test/test_scroll.py assembles every one of the sixteen whole bodies from that pattern and pins each against the image, which is what lets recreate/src/scroll.c reconstruct all sixteen as a single function taking bg_scroll_x as an argument. Reconstructed there, with bg_scroll_blit.
fn 0x8450 bg_scroll_copy_x1
fn 0x84ea bg_scroll_copy_x2
cmt 0x84ea First variant that needs the horizontal wrap: 28 moves, `lea -128(a0),a0` back to the start of the source row, 2 moves, then `lea 40(a1),a1 / lea 136(a0),a0`. x3..x15 shift the split point two moves further each time.
fn 0x8590 bg_scroll_copy_x3
fn 0x8636 bg_scroll_copy_x4
fn 0x86dc bg_scroll_copy_x5
fn 0x8782 bg_scroll_copy_x6
fn 0x8828 bg_scroll_copy_x7
fn 0x88ce bg_scroll_copy_x8
fn 0x8974 bg_scroll_copy_x9
fn 0x8a1a bg_scroll_copy_x10
fn 0x8ac0 bg_scroll_copy_x11
fn 0x8b66 bg_scroll_copy_x12
fn 0x8c0c bg_scroll_copy_x13
fn 0x8cb2 bg_scroll_copy_x14
fn 0x8d58 bg_scroll_copy_x15
cmt 0x8d58 Last variant: 2 moves, `lea -128(a0),a0`, 28 moves. Its second half ends at $8dfc, so the family tiles $83b6..$8dfe with no gap -- asserted rather than eyeballed, against the sixteen table entries and the sixteen recorded body lengths (recreate/test/test_scroll.py).

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Actor records and the pass that projects them into screen coordinates.
# The game keeps its moving objects as 19 records of 32 bytes in one of THREE
# parallel tables ($996c / $9bd0 / $9e34) picked by the two mode flags $a30 and
# $a32, and projects the chosen table into a parallel array of 19 SIX-byte
# screen records at $98ec: map position minus the scroll, plus the sprite word.
# $8f02 (sprite_draw_pass, reconstructed in recreate/src/blit.c) is the array's only other reader.
# Slot 12 of that array IS $9934/$9936 -- scroll_follow_x/_y, named above from
# the scroll's side -- which is what fixes "the followed object" as slot 12 of
# the actor table and makes $67e0 the routine that names it.
# All five reconstructed in recreate/src/actor.c; reference counts are byte
# scans of the whole image (abs.l, abs.w, d16(pc), both jsr forms and branches).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x67e0 followed_actor_record
cmt 0x67e0 24 bytes, reconstructed. `tst.w $a32.w / bne` then `lea $9aec.l,a1` or `lea $9fb4.l,a1` -- the whole routine is a1. FIFTEEN references in the image and every one of them a call: two `jsr $67e0.l` ($50a, $5892), two `jsr $67e0.w` ($8e08 inside project_followed_actor, $10772), ten `bsr` ($30a6, $43a4, $4646, $67a6, $67c2, $67f8, $6840, $701c, $72c6, $7366) and one `bra.w` tail jump at $6d6c. Note the test is `bne` where project_actor_list's on the same word is `bpl`: the image only ever writes $a32 $0000 or $ffff (see state_flag_a32), so the two readings agree on every value the game produces and differ on a small positive one -- pinned from the other side by a case in recreate/test/test_actor.py. NO `proto`, deliberately, for the reason this file's header gives: it takes nothing and its whole result is a1, so the register map is this comment.
fn 0x67c2 actor_set_side_flag
proto 0x67c2 actor_set_side_flag actor@A0
cmt 0x67c2 30 bytes, reconstructed. `bsr followed_actor_record`, then `cmp.w (a1),(a0)` as words and `bset #3,8(a0)` when the actor's x is STRICTLY greater, `bclr #3,8(a0)` otherwise -- i.e. bit 3 of the flag byte says the followed actor is to my LEFT. `bset`/`bclr` on memory are BYTE operations. THIRTY-FOUR `bsr` callers, all in $27da..$4d62 bar $72c2, which is what makes bit 3 an every-actor field rather than one routine's private state. Its only reader anywhere in the image is the `btst #3,8(a1)` at $51e -- inside the routine that starts at $50a -- which reads it on the FOLLOWED record itself.
fn 0x67f8 actor_followed_x_within
cmt 0x67f8 42 bytes, reconstructed. `bsr followed_actor_record`, then adds the caller's d0 to whichever of the two x words is BEHIND and compares again: returns 0 in d0's low word while the followed actor is within d0 of the actor horizontally, $ffff beyond it. Only the low word is written (`clr.w d0` / `move.w #$ffff,d0`), so the caller's own high half comes back -- and the ADD is a 16-bit one whose wrap the following `cmp.w` reads, while both compares are SIGNED comparisons of the operands (`bgt`/`blt` read N xor V). FIVE `bsr` callers: $23c4, $27e2, $2c48, $438e, $46c4. No `proto`, per this file's header: d0 is IN AND OUT, so the register map is this comment.
fn 0x8dfe project_followed_actor
cmt 0x8dfe 104 bytes, reconstructed. `tst.w $a30.w / bpl` -- `bpl` reads N alone, so this is the flag's own sign -- then `jsr $67e0.w` and one projection into $9934: `x - $20 - bg_scroll_pos_x`, `y - $40 - bg_scroll_pos_y`, and the record's sprite word unless bit 6 of its flag byte is set AND frame_toggle is nonzero, in which case a zero. ONE reference in the image, the `jsr $8dfe.l` at $4ca in game_main_loop, immediately before `jsr $7522.l` (bg_scroll_run_queue) -- which is exactly why it exists: it refreshes the one screen record the scroll steers on before the scroll reads it. $67e0 has no $a30 form, so in that mode slot 12 would be refreshed from a table project_actor_list is not projecting; the `bpl` gate is what stops it.
fn 0x8e66 project_actor_list
cmt 0x8e66 156 bytes, reconstructed. Publishes one of $9bd0 / $9e34 / $996c into $a098 -- `tst.w $a30.w / bpl` then `tst.w $a32.w / bpl`, both sign tests -- re-reads the longword it just wrote, and runs project_followed_actor's eleven-instruction projection over all nineteen records, ending on `cmpa.l #$995e,a1`. ONE reference, the `jsr $8e66.l` at $4d6 in game_main_loop, after bg_scroll_run_queue and before the sprite pass at $8f02.
var 0xa30 state_flag_a30
cmt 0xa30 Word flag, the sibling of state_flag_a32 and read the same way. TEN operand sites, one abs.l and nine abs.w. FIVE `tst.w` readers -- $4ec (the abs.l one), $8dfe, $8e66, $dbc0, $ff42 -- against FIVE writers: three `move.w #$ffff` ($1ae6, $1e96, $e0d0) and two `clr.w` ($e016, $ff0c). So like $a32 (thirteen readers, three writers) it only ever holds $0000 or $ffff, and every reader is a bare sign or zero test. WHICH mode it selects is not established, which is why the name carries the address.
var 0x996c actor_table_default
cmt 0x996c 19 records of 32 bytes, the table project_actor_list projects while neither mode flag is negative. Five abs.l references: $caa, $8e92 (the `move.l #$996c,$a098` here), $e710, $ff14, $101d2.
var 0x9bd0 actor_table_a30
cmt 0x9bd0 The table project_actor_list projects while state_flag_a30 is negative. Eight abs.l references, including $8e6e's `move.l #$9bd0,$a098`. followed_actor_record never returns a record from this table, which is what project_followed_actor's gate is about.
var 0x9e34 actor_table_a32
cmt 0x9e34 The table project_actor_list projects while state_flag_a30 is not negative and state_flag_a32 is. Four abs.l references, including $8e84's `move.l #$9e34,$a098`.
var 0xa098 actor_table_selected
cmt 0xa098 Longword: whichever of the three tables project_actor_list published. Twelve abs.l references -- four inside $8e66 itself ($8e6e, $8e84 and $8e92, the three `move.l #imm`, and $8e9c, the `movea.l` that reads it straight back) and eight elsewhere ($8d0, $1b68, $1b8e, $23d4, $267c, $ff14, $101d2, $101e0), so the published pointer is what the rest of the game walks the actor table through.
var 0x9aec followed_actor_default
cmt 0x9aec followed_actor_record's a1 while state_flag_a32 is zero, and $996c + 12 * 32 -- slot 12 of actor_table_default, the slot whose projection is scroll_follow_x. Nine abs.l references to the word itself, plus one to $9aed (its low byte); the pair at $51be and $51c6 are bcd_add_random_1_to_4's reads of this word's high and low byte, so that draw's non-hardware entropy is the followed actor's x.
var 0x9fb4 followed_actor_a32
cmt 0x9fb4 The same slot of actor_table_a32 ($9e34 + 12 * 32), and followed_actor_record's a1 while state_flag_a32 is nonzero. Five abs.l references.
var 0x98ec actor_screen_records
cmt 0x98ec 19 records of six bytes -- {screen x, screen y, sprite} -- that project_actor_list fills and the sprite pass at $8f02 reads. Exactly two abs.l references, $8ea2 (the `lea` here) and $8f02 (the sprite pass's own), and the same for its end $995e ($8ef8's `cmpa.l` and $8fc2). Record 12 is $9934, i.e. scroll_follow_x/_y and the word after them; no operand anywhere names $9938, which is reachable only by walking that record. The sprite pass reads each record as {screen x, screen y, sprite} in that order, skips one whose sprite word is 0, and adds the sprite descriptor's own x/y offsets to the first two.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The actor table's LIFECYCLE (batch 10), reconstructed in recreate/src/actor.c.
# Reset, free, allocate, spawn, and the two routines that move a record between
# standing and falling. NO `proto` lines: every one of them leaves a register
# behind (a walked-out cursor, a clobbered d0), so the register map is the cmt.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x1f36 actor_table_reset
cmt 0x1f36 30 bytes, reconstructed. `move.w #$12,d0` then 19 x (`move.l #$ffbe0000,(a0)+` + seven `clr.l (a0)+`) = all nineteen 32-byte records of the table a0 names, each marked free and otherwise zeroed. THREE `bsr` callers ($19e8, $1bba, $1eae) and FOUR `jsr $1f36.w` ones ($e0ae, $e716, $e720, $e72a); the last three are the boot's, which is what resets all three tables. a0 in; a0 walks out 608 bytes on and d0 out is $ffff, neither of which any caller reads.
fn 0xdf9e actor_slots_mark_free
cmt 0xdf9e 14 bytes, reconstructed. `move.w #$ffbe,(a6) / lea 32(a6),a6 / dbf d7` -- d7 + 1 records from a6 on get the free marker and NOTHING else is touched, unlike actor_table_reset which zeroes the rest of each record. TWO `bsr` callers, $ded4 and $dee2. a6 and d7 in; a6 walks out and d7 ends at $ffff.
fn 0x1b68 actor_alloc_slot_low
cmt 0x1b68 38 bytes, reconstructed. `movea.l $a098.l,a1 / lea 96(a1),a1` then nine `cmpi.w #$ffbe,(a1)` a record apart: a1 comes back holding the first FREE record of slots 3..11 of whichever table actor_table_selected names, or $0 when the pool is full. THREE callers and all `jsr $1b68.w` ($ffa4, $ffcc, $101dc) -- a byte scan for the abs.w operand, since a `bsr` scan finds none. Only $101dc tests the result (`cmpa.l #$0,a1`); the two inside $ff42 pass it straight to actor_spawn_from_template, which writes through it unchecked -- a full pool there stamps the record over the 68000 vector page. Batch 13 PINNED that and sharpened it: the stamp is EIGHTEEN bytes, not 32 -- $0..$9, $e..$13 and $1e..$1f, i.e. both reset vectors, the bus-error vector's high word, the address-error vector's low word, the whole illegal-instruction vector and the TRAPV vector's low word, with $a..$d and $14..$1d untouched. No `proto`: the whole result is a1.
fn 0x1b8e actor_alloc_slot_high
cmt 0x1b8e 38 bytes, reconstructed, and BYTE-IDENTICAL to actor_alloc_slot_low bar two operand words: `lea 416(a1),a1` and `move.w #$5,d0`, i.e. slots 13..18. SEVENTEEN `bsr` callers: $1276, $12c4, $1308, $158e, $2c82, $3640, $3960, $3afa, $3d0a, $3f4a, $43ae, $4562, $467c, $499c, $4b84, $7176, $71b2. THE TWO POOLS ARE WHY SLOT 12 IS THE FOLLOWED ACTOR: 3..11 and 13..18 meet exactly either side of it, so no allocation can ever hand out the record followed_actor_record returns, and slots 0..2 are below both. That is the other half of batch 8's finding, from the allocators' side.
fn 0xffe4 actor_spawn_from_template
cmt 0xffe4 134 bytes, reconstructed. Turns the 32-byte template a0 names -- a record of the table table_ptr_21e8c heads -- into the actor record a1 names. `clr.w 8(a1)` clears BOTH flag bytes; then x := 24(a0), y := 26(a0), type := 12(a0). Five `cmp.w #$36/$37/$38/$3b/$3c` on the type pick whether the footprint pair at 14/16 is copied from the TEMPLATE or looked up in actor_size_table ($1009a) with `lsl.w #2` -- a WORD index, so a type from $4000 up wraps back into the table. Then sprite, 30, 31 and 18 are cleared, bit 2 of 9(a1) is set, and 19(a1) := `(a0 - $21e8c) asr.l #5`, the template's own slot number as a SIGNED longword shift stored as a byte. TWO `bsr` callers, $ffa8 and $ffd0, both inside $ff42 and both immediately after a `jsr $1b68.w`. No `proto`: a0 and a1 in, and d0/d1 are clobbered.
fn 0x2af2 actor_start_motion_at_speed
proto 0x2af2 actor_start_motion_at_speed actor@A0 speed@D0
cmt 0x2af2 24 bytes, reconstructed. `bclr #2,8(a0)` (no longer supported), `bset #0,8(a0)`, `bset #1,8(a0)`, `move.b d0,11(a0)` -- the launch: the record leaves the ground carrying d0's low byte as its vertical step. SEVEN control-flow sites: four `bsr` ($2b6c, $2c5a, $3d06, $4998) and three `bra.w` tail jumps ($32ac, $4190, $4cd6), the last of which reach it with d0 already loaded. It is the one routine in this batch that leaves NO register behind, which is why it is the one that carries a `proto`.
fn 0x14d6 actor_accelerate_fall
cmt 0x14d6 32 bytes, reconstructed. `bclr #2,8(a0) / bset #4,8(a0)` then `moveq #0,d0 / move.b 11(a0),d0 / cmpi.b #$8,d0 / beq` over `addq.b #1,11(a0)`: one more unit of fall speed per frame until the byte is EXACTLY 8, so a record already carrying a larger byte keeps climbing and `addq.b` wraps rather than saturating. Two entry paths: `bsr.w` from $13a6 and `blt.w` from $14c0, the latter inside the 98-byte routine at $1492 whose body it sits in. No `proto`: d0 comes back holding the pre-increment speed byte.
fn 0x2b5a actor_hop_or_flip_side
proto 0x2b5a actor_hop_or_flip_side actor@A0 step_outcome@D0 ground_flags@D1
cmt 0x2b5a 40 bytes ($2b5a..$2b81), reconstructed -- and the 40 INCLUDES the shared tail at $2b7a, which is why the extent looks larger than the code. `tst.b d0 / bne.w $2b70` splits on the map step's outcome byte: BLOCKED (d0 low byte zero) takes `btst #0,d1` -- bit $1 is "the cell ahead is a block and the one above it is not", i.e. a step up -- and either `move.w #$4,d0 / bsr.s $2af2` (launch at speed 4) or falls into $2b7a's side flip. NOT blocked takes the second arm at $2b70, `btst #1,d1 / bne.w $2b7a / rts`: bit $2 is "two rows down is neither $1 nor $2", a two-cell drop, and the actor turns round at it. So this is the hop-or-turn reaction and $2b82 is the plain turn one. TWO `bsr` callers, $2a56 and $2a6c, one either side of a `btst #3,8(a0)` and each preceded by `move.w #$1,d7` + a step ($1170 on one arm, $10a2 on the other) -- which is what says d0/d1 are those routines' results. d0 comes back holding $4 in its low word on the hop arm alone.
fn 0x2b82 actor_toggle_side_flag
proto 0x2b82 actor_toggle_side_flag actor@A0 step_outcome@D0 ground_flags@D1
cmt 0x2b82 12 bytes ($2b82..$2b8d), reconstructed in recreate/src/actor.c: the write set is one byte of the actor record and the inputs are a0/d0/d1. `tst.b d0 / beq.s $2b7a / btst #2,d1 / bne.s $2b7a / rts` -- fall into the shared tail when d0's low BYTE is zero (the step was blocked) or bit $4 of d1 is set (one row down is neither $1 nor $2, a one-cell drop). GHIDRA RECORDS 20 BYTES AND IS WRONG about the shape though not about the extent: both branches go BACKWARDS out of the entry (which is why batch 10 rejected the routine), so Ghidra's body is these 12 bytes PLUS the 8 of the tail, and the routine's own last instruction is the `rts` at $2b8c. $2b8e starts a routine of its own. TEN `bsr` callers: $263c, $2652, $2cf8, $2d08, $3694, $36a4, $3d72, $3d82, $4a04, $4a14.
cmt 0x2b7a `bchg #3,8(a0) / rts`, 8 bytes -- the shared tail that FLIPS the bit actor_set_side_flag ($67c2) sets and clears, i.e. "the followed actor is to my left". THREE entrants and not one of them a call: the `beq.w` at $2b64 and the `bne.w` at $2b74, both inside the 40-byte routine at $2b5a (two `bsr` callers, $2a56 and $2a6c), plus $2b82's two short branches. So $2b70 is NOT an entry point -- it is $2b5a's second arm, `btst #1,d1 / bne.w $2b7a / rts`, reached only by the `bne.w` at $2b5c. Reconstructed as one static helper the three routines share; the whole-body pins in recreate/test/test_actor.py spell it at all three addresses.
fn 0x2b8e actor_turn_and_launch
proto 0x2b8e actor_turn_and_launch actor@A0 step_outcome@D0 ground_flags@D1
cmt 0x2b8e 58 bytes, to the `rts` at $2bc6, reconstructed. It repeats $2b82's head in the long branch forms: `tst.b d0 / beq.w $2b9e / btst #2,d1 / bne.w $2b9e / rts`. Its tail is its own and longer: `btst #2,8(a0) / beq.w $2bc6` returns for a record that is not supported -- so unlike $2b82 an unsupported record is left entirely alone, side flag included -- and otherwise `bchg #3,8(a0)`, `bclr #2,8(a0)`, `bset #0,8(a0)`, `bset #1,8(a0)`, `move.b #$7,11(a0)` -- the side flip plus exactly the three flag bits and the speed byte actor_start_motion_at_speed writes, with the speed a literal 7 rather than a register, which is why the reconstruction spells it out instead of calling that routine. Not one of Ghidra's functions at all. TWO `bsr` callers, $37c8 and $37de.
var 0x1044c actor_kind_table
cmt 0x1044c WHAT A RESPAWNED SLOT COMES BACK AS, and WHAT A COLLECTED PICKUP IS WORTH: one 16-byte row per kind, 22 rows ($1044c..$105ab), bounded above by the FOURTEEN longword code pointers of pickup_effect_table at $105ac (the first is $105e4, the code immediately past them). A row is [0] type, [2] sprite, [4] a packed-BCD score LONGWORD and [10] a WORD index into that table. TWO `lea $1044c.l` sites, by a whole-image scan of both absolute encodings and both PC-relative forms: $6d3c inside actor_respawn_as_new_kind and $5476 inside actor_behavior_type38_pickup (their longword operands are at $6d3e and $5478). The two readers take different fields and bound the index differently. actor_respawn_as_new_kind reads only [0] -> 4(a0) the actor's new type and [2] -> 6(a0) its new sprite, and bounds the index at NEITHER end. actor_behavior_type38_pickup reads [4] (handed to bcd_add_score_bd70 and text_post_bonus_points_a4be when nonzero) and [10], and its `cmpi.b #$2,20(a0) / bge` is a SIGNED byte compare, so its row read runs only for kinds 2..127 and lands within 2032 bytes of the table. Rows 0..20 all hold type $26 = actor_type_unscored and row 21 holds $3d. Rows 0, 2 and 16..20 carry a nonzero score and rows 3..15 carry pickup-effect indices 1..13; every other row is zero in both fields. Row 0's $00000020 is UNREACHABLE from this site -- the `bge` excludes kinds below 2 -- so the scores slot 38 can actually pay are row 2's and rows 16..20's. THIS PLATE'S FIRST TWO CLAIMS WERE BOTH WRONG until batch 28 and are corrected above: it counted one reference where there are two, and it said only the first two words of a row are read. Batch 38 folded that correction in here (the file carried it as a second `cmt` at this address, which ApplyNames' last-wins rule made the only one Ghidra ever saw), cited the two INSTRUCTIONS where the correction had cited their operands, and counted the pointer block correctly.
var 0xe222 stage_kind32_table
cmt 0xe222 stage_random_kind32's candidate table: 11 rows of 32 bytes ($e222..$e381), one row per stage_number, self-bounding at both ends -- $e1f0's body ends on its base and stage_kind_table ($e382) begins where it ends. Every byte in it is at or below $14, so the closing `andi.l #$1f` is a no-op over the table's own data and only becomes observable where the unbounded row index leaves it.
var 0x1009a actor_size_table
cmt 0x1009a One longword per actor type -- {half width, second word} -- stamped into 14(a1)/16(a1) by actor_spawn_from_template for every type but the five that carry their own. ONE abs.l reference in the image, that routine's own `lea $1009a.l,a2` at $10028, so nothing declares its length. It runs to $1011a, where spawn_hitpoint_table begins -- 32 entries, the same width as that table.
var 0x1011a spawn_hitpoint_table
cmt 0x1011a One WORD per actor type: the base hit points actor_template_set_hitpoints ($1006a) adds to half the template's kill count. ONE reference in the image, that routine's own `lea $1011a.l,a2` at $1008a, so like actor_size_table nothing declares its length -- but the 64 bytes $1011a..$10159 are 32 entries, the last four of them zero, and a different table of 4-byte records starts at $1015c (`lea $1015c.l,a0` at $1a82). The word at $1015a, between the two, is zero and belongs to neither: the space allows a 33rd entry and nothing says whether it is one. The values are small ($0..$23), which is what "hit points" rests on together with $6b46's `sub.w d0,4(a1)` on the field this seeds.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The COLLISION MAP the actors walk on (batch 10), reconstructed in
# recreate/src/map.c. Laid out exactly like bg_map_row_stride: a word of bytes
# per row, then one byte per 16x16 cell. state_flag_a32 picks which of the two.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0x10a2 actor_step_left_against_map
cmt 0x10a2 206 bytes, reconstructed. Steps the actor a0 names LEFT by d7 pixels, refusing to enter a tile-$1 cell: the probe is `x - 14(a0) - d7` and `(y - 1) asr.w #4` for the row, and while the cell it lands in holds $1 the routine drops d7 by one and tries again (clearing 22(a0) each retry when the record's type word is 1). It commits with `sub.w d7,(a0)` -- on an EXACT zero d7 too, so the x word is written on every path out. A probe that went NEGATIVE instead parks x at 14(a0). d0 comes back $ff when the very first probe was already clear and $0 otherwise, but only its low BYTE is written over a low word still holding the probe's map column. FORTY-ONE `bsr` callers, the same count as $1170; only actor_fall_and_settle $1334 has more, 46. THE ONE ASYMMETRY, reproduced rather than tidied: the ground test in the tail reads its row stride from `move.w $23494.l,d7` -- collision_map_default's word, unconditionally -- even when the cell lookup above used collision_map_a32. d1's low word comes back as the ground flags ($1 = the cell is a block and the one above it is not; $4 = one row down is neither $1 nor $2; $2 = nor is two rows down), its high word being whatever the `mulu.w` left there. No `proto`: d0 and d1 are both results.
fn 0x1170 actor_step_right_against_map
cmt 0x1170 152 bytes, reconstructed. The mirror of actor_step_left_against_map: steps the actor a0 names RIGHT by d7 pixels, refusing to enter a tile-$1 cell, with the probe `x + 14(a0) + d7` and `(y - 1) asr.w #4` for the row, retrying one pixel at a time exactly as $10a2 does (clearing 22(a0) each retry when the type word is 1). IT IS NOT A PARAMETRISATION OF $10a2, which is why it needed its own read: instead of the left form's `tst.w d3 / bpl` it CLAMPS at `$83b2 (bg_scroll_limit_x) + $f0` -- or at $f0 ALONE when state_flag_a32 is nonzero, that mode having no limit word ($11b6 is a SECOND read of the flag) -- compared against the UNSHIFTED probe in d3 by `cmp.w d3,d0 / bge`, signed and inclusive. A probe past it parks x at `limit + $f0 - 14(a0)` so the actor's right EDGE sits on the level's own; $f0 is the same bias $fb18 SUBTRACTS from `(cells << 4)` when it builds the limit word, so `limit + $f0` recovers the level's width in pixels. IT HAS NO `rts`: both exits `bra.w $111a`, INTO $10a2's ground test, so d1 and the collision_map_default stride asymmetry are the same code and the tail is shared (three `bra` sites reach $111a and nothing else). d0's low word under the outcome byte therefore differs per exit -- the probe's column when the step ran out ($11e4), the CLAMP LIMIT when the compare committed the move ($11d0), and the PARKED x when the clamp fired ($11f8), where the byte is a literal $0 rather than d6, so a first probe that was clear still reports blocked. FORTY-ONE `bsr` callers and no `jsr`/`jmp`, the same count as $10a2 and each within $80 bytes of one of its sites -- the two arms of a direction test. Eleven callers read the byte (`tst.b d0`), three the whole low word ($41ae, $4cf4, $4e98), none reads d1.
fn 0x1400 actor_settle_on_platform
cmt 0x1400 146 bytes, reconstructed. Scans the map cells the actor's footprint covers for tile $23, starting at the cell a6 names and stepping one cell per 16 pixels of d7 (`cmpi.w #$10,d7 / blt`, a SIGNED comparison of the operand); when d7 runs out it tests ONE more cell if the actor's own position within its cell (d2) reaches at least `-(d7 asr.w #1) & $f`. Found: if the actor's y is inside [platform_y - $12, platform_y - $c] -- `subi.w #$12` then `cmp/bgt` and `addq.w #6` then `cmp/blt`, both SIGNED comparisons of the operands -- the record is parked at platform_y - $10, `bset #2,8(a0)` (supported), `bset #1,9(a0)`, `bclr #4,8(a0)`, `bclr #1,8(a0)` and 11(a0) cleared. Not found (or out of that band): `bset #4,8(a0)` unless bit 2 is still set, and `bclr #1,9(a0)`. Entered by `bra.w` from $13ba and by its own `bra.s` at $1416, so its rts returns to $1334's caller; a6/d7/d2 are handed over by $13c8. No `proto`: d0/d1 are clobbered on the platform arm.
fn 0x13be actor_map_cell_from_actor_x
cmt 0x13be 10 bytes, reconstructed. `moveq #0,d0 / move.l d0,d1` clears BOTH as LONGS, then `move.w (a0),d0 / sub.w 14(a0),d0` leaves the actor's LEFT EDGE (x - half_width, a word subtraction that wraps) in d0 -- and it FALLS THROUGH into actor_map_cell_lookup, having no `rts` of its own, exactly as text_plot_char falls into text_plot_glyph. recreate/src/map.c writes it as a prelude that CALLS the lookup, and recreate/test/test_map.py pins the fall-through itself: $13be + 10 == $13c8, and its ten bytes carry no `rts`. Because the two clears are LONG writes, no caller entering here ever sees the caller high halves in d0/d1 that a standalone $13c8 hands back -- while d2 and d7, written a word at a time further down, still pass through. Two `bsr` callers, $13ae and $13b6.
fn 0x13c8 actor_map_cell_lookup
cmt 0x13c8 56 bytes, reconstructed. The cell lookup both map probes spell: d2 := d0 & $f (taken BEFORE the shift), d0 := `d0 asr.w #4`, d1 := `2(a0) asr.w #4` -- both ARITHMETIC, so a pixel left of or above the origin names a NEGATIVE column/row -- a6 := collision_map_a32 or collision_map_default by `tst.w $a32.w / beq` (the NONZERO reading), then `move.w (a6)+,d3 / mulu.w d1,d3 / add.w d0,d3 / lea 2(a6,d3.w),a6` and d7 := `2 * 14(a0)`. It writes NO memory: its whole output is a6 (the cell), d2 (the sub-cell), d3 (the row x stride product with the cell index in its low word), d7 (the span) and the d0/d1 by-products -- which is why it was portable only once the kit's oracle began reporting the full register set. Four semantics the port reproduces: `mulu.w` is UNSIGNED and writes all 32 bits (a negative row multiplies as $ffff, and d3's ENTRY value never survives); the `add.w` after it writes the low word only, never carrying into the product's high half; `lea 2(a6,d3.w)` sign-extends the index's LOW WORD after the `(a6)+` has already stepped a6 past the stride word, so an index past $7fff addresses BELOW the map; and every other write here is a WORD write into a longword register, so the ENTRY high halves of d0/d1/d2/d7 survive into the result. recreate/src/map.c builds it out of the same collision_map/cell_pointer/pixel_to_cell helpers actor_step_left_against_map inlines, so the arithmetic has ONE spelling. ONE `bsr` caller, $1344 -- which passes the actor's own x, no half-width taken off ($1342 is `move.w (a0),d0`) -- plus the fall-through from $13be.
fn 0x1334 actor_fall_and_settle
cmt 0x1334 138 bytes, reconstructed. The tier above the map probes: add the record's own 11(a0) to its y and settle it against the collision map. FORTY-SIX `bsr` callers and nothing else -- no `jsr`, no `jmp`, no `bra` in. It has NO `rts` on its main path: it ends `bra.w $1400`, so actor_settle_on_platform's own `rts` returns to this routine's caller, and its four exits are the two `rts`s of its own ($1362 and $1380), $1400's and -- through $1492 -- actor_accelerate_fall's. THE PLAYER-ONLY HEAD: for `cmpi.w #$1,4(a0)` only, `move.w (a0),d0 / bsr.w $13c8` looks up the cell under the record's OWN x (not its left edge -- this is the ONE site in the image that enters $13c8 rather than $13be) and compares it against tile $33; on that tile `move.w #$ffff,$1514.l` and then `tst.w $1516.l / beq` -- a set tile_33_mode RETURNS at $1362 with nothing else touched -- and off it, $1516/$1518/$1514 are cleared in that order. Then `btst #0,8(a0) / beq` returns for a record under actor_start_motion_at_speed's own control. Otherwise `moveq #$0,d1 / move.b 11(a0),d1 / add.w d1,d0 / move.w d0,2(a0)` steps y by the speed byte, and `btst #1,9(a0) / bne` or `andi.w #$f,d0 / cmp.w d1,d0 / ble` chooses the arm: already landed, or the step carried the record into a new cell -> `bsr $13be / bsr $1492`; otherwise `bsr $14d6` alone. Either way $13be is called a SECOND time before `bra.w $1400`, because $1492's scan CONSUMES a6 and d7 -- handing $1400 what $1492 left would start it partway through the footprint. No `proto`: it takes a0 and nothing else, and its whole result is memory.
fn 0x1492 actor_settle_on_tile_1_or_2
cmt 0x1492 98 bytes as the scan records it, 130 as a body ($1492..$1513) -- actor_settle_on_platform's sibling, walking the same footprint with the same `cmpi.w #$10,d7` signed step and the same `-(d7 asr.w #1) & $f` sub-cell test, but accepting a PAIR of tile codes ($1 OR $2, spelled as two `cmpi.b`/`beq` at each of its three test sites) rather than $23. Reconstructed. Found: `andi.w #$fff0,2(a0)` -- the top of whichever cell the record is in, its own y MASKED and not a platform word read out of memory -- then `bset #2,8(a0)`, `bclr #4,8(a0)`, `bclr #1,8(a0)`, `clr.b 11(a0)`; 9(a0) is never touched, which is the other half of what separates it from $1400. Not found: the whole of actor_accelerate_fall. ITS BODY ENCLOSES THAT ROUTINE: $14d6's 32 bytes sit inside this span, reached BOTH by the `blt.w $14d6` at $14c0 and by the not-taken `beq.w $14f6` at $14d2 falling straight in, and its `rts` at $14f4 is one of this routine's two exits (the other being the landing arm's at $1512) -- which is why Ghidra reports 98 for a 130-byte body. recreate/src/map.c writes both arms as a CALL to actor_accelerate_fall, exactly as $13be is written as a prelude calling $13c8; recreate/test/test_map.py pins all 130 bytes with the enclosed 32 asserted as that routine's own address and length, so 130 = 98 + 32 fails there if either number moves. ONE `bsr` caller, $13b2 inside actor_fall_and_settle, plus its own `bra.s` loop-back at $14b0. No `proto`: a6/d7/d2 are handed over by $13c8 and its whole result is memory.
var 0x1514 tile_33_flag
cmt 0x1514 Word, and one of three that sit between $1492's last byte and the routine at $151a. RAISED while the player's own map cell holds tile $33. Whole-image operand scan, BOTH absolute encodings (all three lie below $8000, so the short form reaches them): five writers -- `move.w #$ffff` at $1350 and `clr.w` at $1370 (both inside actor_fall_and_settle), `st` at $155c and `clr.b` at $179e, which write the BYTE and leave $1515 alone, and the `clr.l` at $ff00 that clears this word and tile_33_mode together -- against two readers, both bare `tst.w`: $d84 and $1208. var 0x1516 tile_33_mode
cmt 0x1516 Word. Raised only by the two arms below $d84's tile_33_flag test, which write DIFFERENT nonzero values (`move.w #$ff` at $db2, `move.w #$ffff` at $dea); cleared by `clr.w` at $107c and $1364 and by $ff00's `clr.l`. FIVE readers -- $d78, $fa8, $1014, $1358, $208e -- and every one of them a bare `tst.w`, so nothing in the image can tell its two nonzero values apart. While it is set, actor_fall_and_settle returns at $1362 before touching the record at all, and $fa8/$1014 (the two walk handlers) call $107c, which clears it and launches the record.
var 0x1518 tile_33_step_flag
cmt 0x1518 Word. Raised $ffff by the same two arms of $d84 ($da4 and $ddc) on the frames they move the record two pixels; cleared at $dfe, $e06 and $136a. fn 0x1af0 map_stamp_block
cmt 0x1af0 86 bytes, reconstructed. Stamps four consecutive tile codes into the collision map as a 2x2 block: `$78,$79` over `$7a,$7b`, or `$7c..$7f` when 2(a1) of the record record_ptr_10420 names holds 4. The cell is `lea $22090.l,a2 / lea 0(a2,d0.w),a2` with d0 = `24(a1) + 4`, i.e. bg_map_row_stride's own address plus the same +4 bias that puts cell 0 past the stride word, and the second row is reached by `adda.w $22090.l,a2` -- a SIGN-EXTENDED word add. THREE callers: `bsr` at $1ab0 and $1ef6, `jsr $1af0.w` at $de8a. Takes no register argument at all.
var 0x1d338 collision_map_a32
cmt 0x1d338 The collision map read while state_flag_a32 is NONZERO. Same shape as bg_map_row_stride: a word of bytes per row at +0, then one byte per cell from +4 on. It lies INSIDE the .PRG (its first word is $0010 and the rest is zero in the file), so it is scratch the game fills at run time. THREE abs.l references, all `lea`s: $10c6, $1194 and $13de -- the three map probes.
var 0x23494 collision_map_default
cmt 0x23494 The collision map read while state_flag_a32 is zero, and the map whose ROW STRIDE both probes' ground tests read whichever map they looked the cell up in. It lies PAST the program's last byte ($218d0), so it is loaded from disk. var 0x9a6e platform_y
cmt 0x9a6e Word: the y actor_settle_on_platform lands a record on, minus $10. EXACTLY TWO operand sites in the whole image, both inside that routine ($144c reads it for the band test, $1466 writes it into 2(a0)), and NOTHING WRITES IT. var 0x10420 record_ptr_10420
