var 0x10424 record_ptr_10424
cmt 0x10424 Longword pointer, zero in the image: the COPY of record_ptr_10420 that six `move.l $10420.l,$10424.l` sites make ($1684, $170e, $1772, $17a4, $17f4, $18f6), so it names the same 32-byte SCENE DESCRIPTOR out of the $21828 table. ONE reader among the recovered functions -- `movea.l $10424.l,a1 / move.w 12(a1),d0` at $517a -- which is what established field 12 of that record as the packed-BCD GOLD AWARD a collected slot-31/32 record pays out.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The text subsystem, $bd8a..$c030: the message-box driver, the plotter's two
# entry points, the ten state bytes at $c030 and the message table at $a09c.
# Reconstructed in recreate/src/text.c. CLOSED -- game_main_loop is the only
# caller of any of it, and it calls nothing outside itself.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0xbf5e text_plot_glyph
cmt 0xbf5e 210 bytes, reconstructed. A fully unrolled 8x8 glyph plot: 32 x `move.b (a0)+,(a1)` at +0/+2/+4/+6 over eight rows with `lea 82(a1),a1` between them, then `move.l a1,d7 / btst #0,d7` and one of two `lea`s. The 88-byte row advance (82 plus the six the four plane bytes walked) is NOT a screen scanline and is no longer unexplained: the destination is the off-screen message buffer at $c03a, 88 bytes wide, which $bd8a blits out 88 bytes at a time with a 72-byte skip -- 88 + 72 being the screen's own 160. The tail returns the cursor of the NEXT 8-pixel cell: two cells share each 8-byte plane group, so it is `lea -621(a1)` (start + 1) from an even cursor and `lea -615(a1)` (start + 7) from an odd one. EIGHT `bsr` callers, all inside $bd8a ($be2a, $be38, $be46, $be5e, $be6c, $be7e, $be8c, $be9a), each passing one of the eight 32-byte frame glyphs at $12b9c. It also clobbers d7, which no caller reads. No `proto`, per this file's header: a1 is IN AND OUT, so the register map is this comment.
fn 0xbf4e text_plot_char
cmt 0xbf4e 16 bytes, and NOT A LEAF despite the hardware scan's T0/empty-callee classification -- it has no `rts` and FALLS THROUGH into text_plot_glyph, whose `rts` returns to its caller. `lea $12c9c.l,a0 / subi.b #$20,d0 / lsl.l #5,d0 / lea (0,a0,d0.w),a0`: a character code becomes a 32-byte glyph pointer, and glyph 0 (char $20) is 32 zero bytes, which is what a space plots. Three semantics the reconstruction keeps: the `subi` is a BYTE op (d0's other three bytes survive), the `lsl` is a LONGWORD one, and the index is the low WORD SIGN-EXTENDED -- unreachable from the game, whose one caller does `moveq #0,d0 / move.b (a6)+,d0`, and reached from a case instead. ONE caller, the `bsr.w` at $beca. The whole routine is entered as $bf4e + $bf5e = 226 bytes, which is why the two are reconstructed as a prelude that calls the plotter and returns its result. Read the scan's "T0, no callees, 16 bytes" as a caveat on the scan: a fall-through target is invisible to it. No `proto`, per this file's header: d0 in, a1 in and out.
var 0x12c9c text_glyph_table
cmt 0x12c9c The font: 32 bytes per glyph from char $20 up, shipped in the .PRG (unlike the tile bitmaps, which are loaded at runtime). Two abs.l references, $1cf6 and text_plot_char's own `lea` at $bf4e.
var 0x12b9c text_frame_glyphs
cmt 0x12b9c The eight 32-byte glyphs immediately below the font, which $bd8a's eight `bsr text_plot_glyph` sites pass directly: the message box's corners, edges and fill. One abs.l reference for the first ($be24); the other seven are named only by their own `lea`s inside $bd8a.
var 0xc03a text_buffer
cmt 0xc03a The off-screen message buffer text_plot_glyph draws into: 88 bytes (176 px) x 4 planes, 6400 bytes in all, running from $c03a to exactly $d93a (panel_restore_dirty_regions). $bd8a clears it with `move.w #$63f,d0 / clr.l (a0)+ / dbf` and blits it to screen_back 88 bytes plus a 72-byte skip per scanline. Three abs.l references, all in $bd8a: $be12 (the clear), $be1e (the compose cursor) and $bef2 (the blit source). It is a CACHE, not a scratchpad: text_run_message_box fills it only on the frame a message is requested and re-blits it unchanged on every frame after, which is what text_box_rows/text_box_top_line exist to carry across. 88 bytes is exactly the 22-cell box the frame draw always spells, so the box fills the buffer's width and only its height varies.
fn 0xbd8a text_run_message_box
cmt 0xbd8a 452 bytes, reconstructed. RENAMED from text_compose_message_box, which described one of its three arms: this is the message box's whole per-frame lifecycle, run once a frame by game_main_loop's `jsr $bd8a.l` at $4fc (its ONE caller in the image). (1) text_request nonzero -- a message was posted since the last frame. $ff dismisses (clear both flags, draw nothing); otherwise the 1-based id indexes text_message_table, whose record gives text_box_rows, text_box_top_line and the string, and the box is COMPOSED into text_buffer and deliberately NOT blitted -- that arm `rts`s at $bed0. (2) text_request zero and text_box_active nonzero -- tick text_lifetime_left if text_lifetime_armed, then blit the ALREADY-composed buffer to screen_back. This is the arm that runs on every frame of a message's life but the first, which is what the buffer exists for: it is a cache, not a scratchpad. (3) both zero -- `rts` having written nothing. The frame is 22 cells wide always (a corner, twenty `move.w #$13,d0 / dbf` edges, a corner = the whole 88-byte buffer line) and text_box_rows tall; the interior loop is `subq.w #3,d0 / dbf`, so a height below 3 would draw 65536 rows -- unreachable through the shipped table, whose minimum is exactly 3. Nothing clamps a text line to the twenty interior cells, and message 113 has a 21-character line that overwrites the frame's right edge. No `proto`: it takes and returns nothing, and d0/d1/a0/a1/d6/a6 at `rts` differ per arm and no caller reads them.
var 0xc030 text_request
cmt 0xc030 Byte: the message id to compose next frame, 1-based, or $ff to dismiss the box. FIFTY-EIGHT operand sites by INSTRUCTION address; six are inside text_run_message_box ($bd8a, $bda0, $bdac, $bde4, $be08, $bed2) and the other FIFTY-TWO are all writers -- there is no reader anywhere else. Immediates seen range $0 to $64, so the shipped writers stay well inside the 117-entry table; the rest are `move.b d0`/`move.b (a0)` and are not bounded by anything read here.
var 0xc031 text_box_active
cmt 0xc031 Byte: a composed box is up and is re-blitted every frame until this is cleared. ELEVEN operand sites; four inside text_run_message_box ($bd94 the test, $bdb2 the dismiss, $bddc the `st $c031` that raises it, $beec the countdown's expiry) and seven outside -- three `tst.b` readers ($bc6, $dc3e, $dc78) and four `clr.b` writers ($cf8, $6fce, $dc84, $dfd8) that take the box down early. `st` writes $ff, but every reader is a plain nonzero test.
var 0xc032 text_box_rows
cmt 0xc032 Byte: the composed box's height in 8-pixel cell rows, latched from the message record. THREE operand sites, ALL inside text_run_message_box: $bdfc writes it from the record, $be50 sizes the frame's interior loop from it and $bf0e turns it into the blit's scanline count (`lsl.l #3` = 8 scanlines per cell row). Nothing outside $bd8a names it, so it is purely how the compose frame tells every later blit frame what it drew.
var 0xc033 text_box_top_line
cmt 0xc033 Byte: the composed box's top SCANLINE on screen, latched from the message record. TWO operand sites, both inside text_run_message_box: $be02 writes it, $befe multiplies it by $a0 (screen_line) for the blit's destination. That product is a 32-bit `mulu` but `lea 48(a1,d0.w),a1` indexes with its LOW WORD SIGN-EXTENDED, so a top line at or above 205 would place the box below screen_back (a NEGATIVE offset, before the buffer's base) rather than far past it; the shipped records only ever ask for 50 or 60.
var 0xc034 text_lifetime_request
cmt 0xc034 Word: how many frames the NEXT box should live for, posted alongside text_request and consumed by the compose arm ($bdc4 latches it into text_lifetime_left, $bdce clears it). FORTY-FOUR operand sites; three inside text_run_message_box and FORTY-ONE outside, every one a writer. Almost all write #$32 (50 frames); the outliers are #$12c at $b6e, #$ffff at $1a9e, and two BYTE writes -- `move.b #$0,$c034.l` at $62e and `clr.b $c034.l` at $6fc8 -- which clear only the HIGH half and leave $c035 standing, so they do not necessarily disarm the request the `tst.w` at $bdba reads.
var 0xc036 text_lifetime_armed
cmt 0xc036 Word: nonzero once any timed message has been composed. EXACTLY TWO operand sites in the whole image, both inside text_run_message_box -- the `move.w #$ffff,$c036.l` at $bdd4 and the `tst.w` at $bed8 -- so NOTHING EVER CLEARS IT. It is a one-way latch: after the first message that posted a text_lifetime_request, every later box decrements text_lifetime_left each frame whether or not it asked for a lifetime.
var 0xc038 text_lifetime_left
cmt 0xc038 Word: frames still to run before the box comes down. EXACTLY TWO operand sites, both inside text_run_message_box -- $bdc4 loads it from text_lifetime_request and $bee2 is the `subq.w #1` per blit frame, whose `bne` skips the `clr.b text_box_active`. Because the decrement is unconditional once text_lifetime_armed is latched, a box composed with no lifetime keeps counting down from whatever the previous message left, and only an EXACT zero takes it down.
var 0xa09c text_message_table
cmt 0xa09c 117 longword pointers ($a09c..$a26f) followed by the 117 records they point at, back to back ($a270..$b345). ONE abs.l reference in the whole image -- text_run_message_box's own `lea $a09c.l,a6` at $bdee -- so nothing declares the table's length; it is read off the data, which is self-bounding three ways: entry 0 points at $a270, the first byte PAST the pointers; every record ends exactly where the next begins; and the last one ends at $b346, which is panel_refresh_frame. A record is {byte height in cell rows, byte top scanline, string}, the string terminated by $ff with $0a starting a new line. Heights run 3..9 (55 of the 117 are the minimum 3), top scanlines are only 50 or 60, and line counts 1..7 -- never more than height minus 2, so the text always fits the frame vertically. Horizontally it does not: record 112 (id 113) has a 21-character line against the frame's 20 interior cells and overwrites the right edge. Pinned against the image by recreate/test/test_text.py.
var 0xa2ac text_gold_digits
cmt 0xa2ac TWO CHARACTERS INSIDE A SHIPPED STRING, not a field of their own: $a2ac..$a2ad are the sixth and seventh of the eight spaces that open text_message_table record 2 (id 3), "        gold get." at $a2a7. text_write_gold_digits_a2ac patches the amount in there before hud_award_gold_from_descriptor posts the message, tens at $a2ac (blanked to a SPACE when it is zero) and units at $a2ad. Three operand sites, all inside $51d8. The shipped bytes are spaces, so a run that never paid out shows the message with no number in it.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The STAGE LOADER ($f95c..$ff42) and the banners it draws ($e110..$e198)
#
# The background-scroll cluster ($7522..$8228) MAINTAINS the eight pre-shifted
# buffers a frame at a time; this tier BUILDS them, once, when a stage loads.
# $f95c is the top of it and RUNS WHOLE as of batch 26: its palette write and
# its sound tail are both reconstructed, so a differential enters at $f95c and
# leaves at that routine's own `rts`.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0xf95c stage_load_window
proto 0xf95c stage_load_window map@A0 start@A1 tiles@A6
cmt 0xf95c 210 bytes, RECONSTRUCTED WHOLE (recreate/src/stage.c, differential in recreate/test/test_stage.py). The stage-transition hinge: `cmpa.l #$1d43e,a6` raises or clears bg_build_raw_tiles depending on whether the tile bank it was handed is the shipped bg_tile_bitmaps, latches a0/a1 into stage_map_ptr/stage_start_ptr, copies 4(a1)/6(a1) to followed_actor_default's first two words, and then `bsr` bg_build_buffer / stage_publish_scroll_state / bg_build_preshifted_copies back to back. Afterwards, unless scroll_follow_frozen is set, it computes scroll_follow_x/_y as `4(a0) - $20 - bg_scroll_pos_x` and `6(a0) - $40 - bg_scroll_pos_y`. Then `bsr set_palette` with `palette_table + (9(a0) << 5)`, and last the sound module through `lea $17adc.l,a1`: a NEGATIVE 8(a0) writes sound_last_tune and calls `jsr 28(a1)` (snd_stop) with no de-duplication, while a non-negative one returns having done nothing if 8(a0) already equals sound_last_tune, and otherwise latches it and calls `jsr (a1)` (stub +0 -> snd_play_song, d0 = the tune). CORRECTIONS to this plate's earlier text, all cited to bytes: (a) everything after the three builders reads the record back through `movea.l $fe1a.l,a0` ($f9a0/$f9da/$f9f6), so the operands are 4(a0)/9(a0)/8(a0) and not 4(a1)/9(a1); (b) the caller list was decomp.c's numbering -- the SIX real call sites are $f8b0 (boot, a1 = $217d8), $e006 (scene_exit_and_reload), $e0ca, $1adc, $1e84 and $1f1a, and the last four raise scroll_follow_frozen first while the first two do not; (c) $f9d6's `move.w 8(a0),d0` is a DEAD read -- d0 is `moveq #0`d at $f9e6 before anything spends it.
fn 0xfa30 bg_build_buffer
cmt 0xfa30 214 bytes, reconstructed in recreate/src/stage.c. Draws the map's visible window -- 11 tile rows of 16 cells -- into pre-shifted copy 0 at $44000, one 128-byte bg_tile_bitmaps bitmap per cell laid down 128 bytes a scanline. a0 = the level map (header and all), a1 = the two words naming the top-left map cell, a6 = the tile bank. THE MAP HEADER IS TWO WORDS: `move.w (a1)+,d0 / move.w (a1)+,d1 / move.w (a0),d2 / mulu.w d2,d1 / add.w d0,d1 / lea 4(a0,d1.w),a0` reads the stride out of the map's own first word and biases past both header words -- the same +4 the collision map's lookup uses, and what WB_MAP_DATA_ROW sat above WB_MAP_ROW_STRIDE for. Three semantics reproduced rather than tidied: the cell index is a WORD and the `lea` SIGN-EXTENDS it, so a start row past $7fff names a cell BELOW the map; `lsl.l #7,d7 / lea 0(a1,d7.l),a1` shifts a 16-bit tile number as a LONG; and the per-row map skip `moveq #0,d7 / move.w d2,d7 / subi.w #$10,d7 / adda.l d7,a0` is UNSIGNED, so a stride below $10 advances the cursor by nearly 64 KB instead of stepping it back. ONE `bsr` caller, $f994.
fn 0xfb06 stage_publish_scroll_state
cmt 0xfb06 320 bytes, reconstructed. Publishes the scroll state the engine at $7522 then steps: bg_scroll_limit_x/_y as `(header word << 4) - $f0` and `- $a0`, bg_scroll_map_cursor / bg_scroll_pos_x / bg_scroll_pos_y from the start record's two words (the cursor multiplied by bg_map_row_stride's OWN word, not the level map's), the ring cursors and the pre-shift carry cleared, bg_scroll_y_bottom set to $9e, and all sixteen bg_scroll_buffer_rows longwords loaded -- `$44000 + copy * $5800 + row * $80` for rows 0 and $9e, which is the exact invariant the vertical steps preserve. Takes NO register argument: both pointers come from stage_map_ptr/stage_start_ptr. It also writes copylock_flag_a (`clr.w`) and copylock_flag_b (`st`, so ONE byte), and those two writes are the ONLY operand sites either address has in the plaintext image. ONE `bsr` caller, $f998.
fn 0xfd46 bg_build_preshifted_copies
cmt 0xfd46 198 bytes, reconstructed. Derives pre-shifted copies 1..7 from copy 0, two pixels at a time: seven passes of 176 scanlines, each scanline sixteen 8-byte cells rotated `rol.l #2` a plane word at a time. a0/a1 are `lea`d ONCE and never reloaded, so pass n reads copy n and writes copy n+1 and the cursors end one buffer on -- which is what makes the eight copies tile $44000..$70000. EACH SCANLINE CLOSES AS A RING: the first cell's shifted-out bits are held in bg_build_carry ($fe0c, four words of scratch between this routine's `rts` and stage_map_ptr) across the whole 128-byte row and ORed into the LAST cell, so a pixel leaving a row's left edge re-enters at its right. It is the whole-buffer form of bg_scroll_preshift_rows, which does the same thing to one freshly drawn row pair. Takes no register argument. ONE `bsr` caller, $f99c.
fn 0xfe1e resource_table_relocate
cmt 0xfe1e 44 bytes, reconstructed. `cmpi.b #$45,(a6)` on the byte at $24898: unless it already holds 'E', stamp it and then add $248d8 to the FIRST longword of each of `8(a6) + 1` twenty-byte records from $248d8 on, turning a relative offset into an absolute pointer. Not idempotent, which is what the signature exists for. The table and the count word are both past the program's last byte ($218d0) and loaded from disk, so a case seeds them. The records are SPRITE DESCRIPTORS (see $248d8): the longword it fixes up is the cell data sprite_draw_pass hands a blitter in a0, and four more fields are now read; ten bytes per record remain unaccounted for. $24898 has four operand sites ($e62a, $e64e, $e87e and this routine's) and $248d8 five ($1cc8, $8f0a, $e8d8 and this routine's two).
fn 0xfe4a game_restart_reset
cmt 0xfe4a Ghidra's ONE 136-byte function here is TWO routines, and a whole-image scan is what says so: $fe4a has a single entrant (`bsr` at $e59e) and $fe8c has its own (`jsr $fe8c.l` at $c00, the instruction immediately after the `subq.w #1,lives` at $bfc). So game_restart_reset is the 66-byte HEAD, $fe4a..$fe8b, and it falls THROUGH into game_life_restart_reset. Reconstructed in recreate/src/stage.c. The head is what only a NEW GAME does: clears level_seq_index, sets lives to 3, clears effect_state_21e4, writes $ffff to effect_record_list's first word (which panel_refresh_record reads as "empty"), clears all six hud slot words and $bd6a.
fn 0xfe8c game_life_restart_reset
cmt 0xfe8c 70 bytes ($fe8c..$fed1), reconstructed -- the tail both entrants run, and the reason Ghidra reports one function: `bsr $e80c` (redraw the lives), hud_meter_value and hud_meter_max to $14, bcd_score_bd70 cleared as a LONGWORD, bcd_counter_bd6e, $bd66, $bd68 and $bd6c cleared, sound_last_tune set to $20, and effect_record_write_ptr pointed back at effect_record_list's base. ONE caller of its own, `jsr $fe8c.l` at $c00 -- the path that decrements lives, calls this, sets effect_state_21e4 to 1, decrements level_seq_index and `jmp $e5ba` -- plus the fall-through from $fe4a. Note the write pointer is restored either way while the $ffff "empty" word is written only by the head, so a LIFE restart leaves whatever record the list already held.
fn 0xe80c hud_draw_lives
cmt 0xe80c 112 bytes, reconstructed in recreate/src/hud.c. Draws THREE 16x16 cells at $704d8 and $784d8 -- BOTH screen buffers, at absolute addresses rather than through screen_back, so it writes the buffer on show as well as the one being drawn. `move.w $be2.w,d1` counts: while d1 is nonzero the slot gets the bitmap at $ec38 and d1 is decremented, otherwise it gets $0000ffff/$00000000, i.e. 16 pixels of colour 2. `lea $ec38.l,a0` sits INSIDE the outer loop, so all three slots draw the same cell. A row is 8 bytes written as two longwords and the cursor steps `4 + 156` = one 160-byte scanline; after 16 rows `lea -2552(a1),a1` leaves it exactly one cell along, which is the next slot. The `tst.w d1` is a NONZERO test and not a sign one, so a negative lives word fills all three. ONE `bsr` caller, $fe8c.
var 0xbe2 lives
cmt 0xbe2 Word. FOUR operand sites in the image: `move.w #$3,$be2.w` in game_restart_reset, `subq.w #1,$be2.w` at $bfc (the instruction immediately before the `jsr $fe8c.l` at $c00 that redraws them), `tst.w $be2.l` at $b4e (which picks message code $17 or $40) and `move.w $be2.w,d1` in hud_draw_lives, which draws min(value, 3) icons. Nothing else reads or writes it.
var 0xec38 lives_icon_bitmap
cmt 0xec38 128 bytes: one 16x16 cell in the ST's word-planar low-res layout, the icon hud_draw_lives stamps into both screens. ONE reference in the image, that routine's `lea $ec38.l,a0` at $e820. What it depicts is not established here.
fn 0xfed2 stage_reset_state
cmt 0xfed2 112 bytes, reconstructed. The per-stage state reset: `lea $b08.w,a0` then four `clr.l (a0)+` and a `clr.w (a0)+` (18 bytes), the panel frame block ($bd26/$bd2a/panel_frame_index/$bd2e cleared and $bd28 set to $500), `clr.l $1514.w` -- ONE longword over tile_33_flag AND tile_33_mode, the only place in the image that clears both at once -- then $a34, scroll_follow_frozen, state_flag_a30 and state_flag_a32, actor_table_selected back to actor_table_default, and finally FOUR `move.l #imm,(a1)+` over bg_tile_index + 496. So the index table's last EIGHT entries (map bytes $f8..$ff -> tiles $46,$47,$48,$49,$4a,$4b,$40,$4d) are written here. The .PRG ships NONE of bg_tile_index -- $21e90 is past the program's last byte ($218d0), so all 256 entries are loaded from disk and this reset overwrites the last eight of them; what the DISK holds there beforehand is not established anywhere. It ends exactly where bg_map_row_stride begins, which is what fixes the count. TWO callers, both `bsr`: $461 and $8232 in ../decomp.c's numbering -- the game-start path.
fn 0xff42 actor_spawn_pass
cmt 0xff42 162 bytes, reconstructed in recreate/src/actor.c. Gated on `tst.w $a30.w` (a NONZERO test, not the `bpl` the projections read the same word with): walks the published spawn table table_ptr_21e8c decrementing 31(record) for every record whose 30(record) is set -- and that walk runs BEFORE the capacity test, so it happens on every frame -- then `move.w -8(a6),d0 / cmp.w -6(a6),d0 / beq` returns while the live count has reached the maximum. Otherwise, while -2(a6) is not $ffff it advances the cursor at -4(a6) and spawns the ONE record it names (`jsr $1b68.w` = actor_alloc_slot_low, `bsr` actor_spawn_from_template, `bsr $1006a` = actor_template_set_hitpoints), raising -2(a6) to $ffff when that record is the last; and once it is $ffff it walks the whole table spawning every record whose 30 is set and whose 31 has reached zero, clearing 30 as it goes. THE FOUR HEADER WORDS BELOW THE TABLE POINTER: -8 = the maximum live count, -6 = the live count (raised here per spawn, lowered by `subq.w #1,-6(a6)` at $6c3e on a death; $6c38 is the `movea.l $21e8c.l,a6` that gets there), -4 = the cursor, -2 = "the cursor has been round". The sweep arm raises -6 per spawn while the capacity test ran ONCE, so a single sweep can carry the live count past the maximum. IT DOES NOT TEST THE ALLOCATOR'S RESULT: a full pool hands actor_spawn_from_template a1 = $0 and eighteen bytes are stamped over the 68000 vector page -- pinned in recreate/test/test_actor.py, and the exact write set is on $1b68. The cursor is post-incremented as a WORD and `lsl.l #5,d0` builds the byte offset as a LONGWORD, but the `lea 0(a0,d0.w),a0` at $ff8c carries extension word $0000 -- a WORD index, SIGN-EXTENDED -- so the shift's long result is thrown away and a cursor past 1023 names a template 32 KB BELOW the table rather than a wrapped one. `41f0 0000` here against the `2372 0800` of actor_spawn_from_template's own size-table lookup are two different instructions. ONE caller, game_main_loop's block gated on $a30.
fn 0x1006a actor_template_set_hitpoints
proto 0x1006a actor_template_set_hitpoints template_record@A0
cmt 0x1006a 48 bytes, reconstructed. `moveq #0,d0 / move.w 6(a0),d0 / asr.w #1,d0` then either `cmp.w #$3b,d1 / addi.w #$1e,d0` or `add.w d1,d1 / lea $1011a.l,a2 / adda.l d1,a2 / add.w (a2),d0`, and `move.w d0,4(a0)`. So 4(template) := half the template's kill count plus a per-type base out of spawn_hitpoint_table, with type $3b taking a constant 30 instead. WHAT MAKES THE TWO FIELDS READABLE ARE THEIR OTHER USERS: 4(template) is what $6b46's `sub.w d0,4(a1)` spends and, on zero or negative, raises bit 3 of 9(actor) for; 6(template) is what $6bfa's `addq.w #1,6(a1)` raises and `cmpi.w #$2,6(a1) / ble` retires the template past. The shift is a SIGNED word one in a register a `moveq` cleared, and the table index is a WORD (`add.w d1,d1`) consumed by a LONGWORD `adda.l`. TWO `bsr` callers, $ffac and $ffd4, both inside $ff42 and both entered with a0 still holding the template actor_spawn_from_template just read -- so it writes the TEMPLATE, not the new actor record.
var 0xfe14 bg_build_raw_tiles
cmt 0xfe14 Word: NONZERO when the map's bytes ARE tile numbers and the bg_tile_index lookup is skipped. THREE operand sites in the whole image and no more -- the `move.w #$ffff` at $f966 and the `clr.w` at $f972 (stage_load_window's two arms, chosen by `cmpa.l #$1d43e,a6`) against the single `tst.w` at $fa52 inside bg_build_buffer. So the index table is a property of the SHIPPED tile bank alone: any other bank's map bytes are read raw.
var 0xfe16 stage_map_ptr
cmt 0xfe16 Longword: the level map stage_load_window was handed in a0. TWO operand sites -- the `move.l a0` at $f978 and the `movea.l` at $fb06 that reads it back.
var 0xfe1a stage_start_ptr
cmt 0xfe1a Longword: the start-position record stage_load_window was handed in a1. FIVE operand sites, all inside those two routines: the `move.l a1` at $f97e and four `movea.l` reads ($f9a0, $f9da, $f9f6, $fb0c). Its layout, read off those readers: words 0/2 are the map cell the window opens on, words 4/6 the followed object's position, byte 8 the tune selector (sign-tested) and byte 9 the palette index (`lsl.w #5` into the table at $fc46).
var 0xfe0c bg_build_carry
cmt 0xfe0c Four words of scratch between bg_build_preshifted_copies' `rts` and stage_map_ptr, written and read only by that routine: the first cell of a 128-byte scanline holds its shifted-out bits here until the last cell of the same row is written, which is what closes the row as a ring. The same shape as bg_preshift_carry ($8228), which the row-at-a-time pre-shift uses.
var 0xfa2e sound_last_tune
cmt 0xfa2e Byte inside the code, between stage_load_window's `rts` and bg_build_buffer's first instruction. stage_load_window compares 8(stage_start_ptr) against it and RETURNS without calling the sound module when they match, so it is a de-duplicating latch: the same tune is not restarted on a re-entry. game_life_restart_reset's `move.b #$20,$fa2e.l` at $febe sets it to $20 -- so BOTH a new game and a lost life re-arm it. THREE operand sites, all in stage_load_window ($fa0c, $fa18, $fa22) plus that reset. recreate/include/wonderboy.h calls it WB_STAGE_TUNE_LATCH.
var 0xa34 state_flag_a34
cmt 0xa34 Word, the third of the $a30/$a32/$a34 band and the least established. ELEVEN operand sites (one abs.l at $8d8, ten abs.w) and none of them inside a routine reconstructed so far. stage_reset_state clears it beside the other two, which is all this project knows about it -- the name carries the address.
var 0xb08 stage_reset_block
cmt 0xb08 Eighteen bytes cleared as one run by stage_reset_state (`lea $b08.w,a0`, four `clr.l (a0)+` and a `clr.w (a0)+`), i.e. $b08..$b19. Batch 34 established TWO of the nine words inside it -- $b12 and $b16, below -- by porting the three behaviour rows that raise them; the other seven are still only the block reset walks. Neither is a field of the reset's own five writes: each is the LOW HALF of one of the `clr.l`s ($b10's and $b14's), which is why the gate that reads them can be disarmed by a longword clear that never names them.
var 0xb12 event_anim_done_b12
cmt 0xb12 Word inside stage_reset_block: raised `move.w #$ffff,$b12.w` by actor_behavior_type35 at $5354, on the frame its animation cursor wraps. THREE operand sites in all, and all three are absolute SHORT: this raise, player_pending_event_gate's `tst.w $b12.w` at $c2e and its `clr.w $b12.w` at $c6e. The gate's shape is the whole meaning: while the word is clear and the record at $998c is free it spawns a type-35 actor from actor_type35_template and plays SFX 5; while it is set it runs the scene's byte-coded script at $19ac, then `clr.l $b0e.w` -- which clears $b10, the very word $c28 tests to reach this arm, as well as $b0e -- and `clr.w $b12.w`. So it is the handshake "the event actor has finished animating". RECONSTRUCTED as WB_EVENT_ANIM_DONE_B12 (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 34) -- the WRITER is ported, the gate that reads it is not.
var 0xb16 event_anim_done_b16
cmt 0x1fa2 NOT A ROUTINE, and it carries NO `fn` DIRECTIVE for that reason -- an `fn` here is what re-creates the extent conflict on the next reapply.sh, because ApplyNames DEFINES a function at the address and Ghidra then truncates player_stage_transition at $1fa2. This plate is the label. It is the SECOND ARM of player_stage_transition ($1f54), and the extent conflict batch 40 phase A registered is resolved in that routine's favour. A whole-image census over every branch form, both absolute encodings, both PC-relative ones and the mode-shaped sweep finds EXACTLY ONE instruction naming $1fa2, the `beq.w $1fa2` at $1f62 inside $1f54 itself, and NO ALIGNED longword or word operand anywhere in the program holds the address either, which is the half an instruction census cannot see and is exactly how the sixty-two actor_behavior_table rows ARE reached -- so it is a continuation reached when the `tst.w $b0e.w` above it fails, and $1f54's stated span $1f54..$21e3 encloses it. The earlier plate's "its entrance is not established" and the earlier name (actor_event_anim_step_2394, which read as a behaviour-tier routine) are both RETRACTED here. RECONSTRUCTED in batch 40 phase C with the rest of $1f54 (recreate/src/player.c). What the arm does: `tst.w $b16.w / beq` gates it on event_anim_done_b16 being UP, whose raisers are actor_behavior_type36 at $53d6 and actor_behavior_type37 at $5400 -- NOT slot 35, whose own raise writes $b12 (see cmt 0xb16, three lines up, which had the census right; "slots 35 and 36" was inherited from the retracted plate and is corrected here); then `lea $2394.l,a1 / move.w (a1)+,d0 / move.w 0(a1,d0.w),6(a0) / addq.w #2,d0 / andi.w #$1f,d0 / move.w d0,$2394.l` -- its own cursor and its own sixteen-word table at $2394, not actor_event_anim_cursor's. Its own `beq.w $1fd6` then continues the same chain of flag tests, which is what says the whole of $1f54..$205c is one flow graph.
var 0x2394 actor_event_anim_cursor_2394
cmt 0x2394 Word: the cursor $1fa2 reads with `move.w (a1)+,d0`, with its sixteen-word frame table immediately above it at $2396 -- actor_event_anim_cursor's layout one table over, and the same SHAPE: sprite ids $152, $153, $154, $155 held FOUR frames each ($2396..$23b5), with code resuming at $23b6. Reached only through the `lea $2394.l,a1` at $1faa, so like actor_event_anim_frames the table has no operand of its own. $1fa2 is NOT a routine -- batch 40 phase B's census made it player_stage_transition's second arm -- so "that routine" above means that ARM. RECONSTRUCTED in batch 40 phase C; the wrap of this cursor raises stage_anim_done_b18 AND blanks the record's sprite word, which is the one arm of the four that publishes twice in a frame.
cmt 0xb16 Word inside stage_reset_block, $b12's sibling for the gate's SECOND arm: raised `move.w #$ffff,$b16.w` by actor_behavior_type36 at $53d6 (its cursor wrap) AND by actor_behavior_type37 at $5400 (its arrival), which are the two alternative actors $cd8 picks between on 6(record_ptr_10420). FOUR operand sites naming the word, all absolute short -- those two raises and TWO readers: player_pending_event_gate's `tst.w $b16.w` at $c76 and a SECOND one at $1fa2, plus the `clr.l $b14.w` at $cfe, which clears this word as the low half of a longword without naming it. THE SECOND READER IS AN ANIMATION STEP of exactly slots 35 and 36's shape -- `tst.w $b16.w / beq / lea $2394.l,a1 / move.w (a1)+,d0 / move.w 0(a1,d0.w),6(a0) / addq.w #2,d0 / andi.w #$1f,d0 / move.w d0,$2394.l` -- over its OWN cursor and table at $2394 rather than actor_event_anim_cursor, and it runs only while this flag is UP where those two raise it. IT IS NOT A ROUTINE, which batch 40 phase B measured and batch 34 could not: exactly one instruction in the image names $1fa2, the `beq.w` at $1f62 inside player_stage_transition, so this reader is that routine's second ARM and the earlier plate's "a third animation stepper ... unnamed and unported" is RETRACTED -- see cmt 0x1fa2. RECONSTRUCTED in batch 40 phase C, as the whole of $1f54 now is -- and its wrap raises stage_anim_done_b18, which is the handshake the gate's third arm reads at $cf0. While it is clear and $998c is free the gate spawns the pair -- an inert type-0 record showing sprite $1a9 and, beside it, a type-37 riser showing $1a8 or a type-36 animator showing $1a4. RECONSTRUCTED as WB_EVENT_ANIM_DONE_B16 (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 34); its reader is not.
var 0xbd26 panel_frame_hold
cmt 0xbd26 Nonzero FREEZES $bbca's delay countdown ($bc60's `tst.w`, which skips the `subq.w #1,$bd28`); it does not stop the index being measured or drawn. Set to $ffff at $afe, $18a6, $199c, $1e8e and $5220 -- the last of those one instruction after panel_frame_rewind is raised, which is the pair. Cleared by $bbca's own rewind clamp, by $e010 and by stage_reset_state. Mirrored as WB_PANEL_FRAME_HOLD in recreate/include/wonderboy.h (it was WB_PANEL_FRAME_TIMER, a name from before the body was read; this var was panel_frame_timer for the same reason).
var 0xbd28 panel_frame_delay
cmt 0xbd28 The countdown panel_frame_index is measured off: restart arm sets $500, the measure arm ticks it down unless panel_frame_hold is up, and `($500 - $bd28) asr.w 7` is the index. panel_frame_rewind winds it back UP in steps of $14.
var 0xbd2a panel_frame_phase
cmt 0xbd2a Nonzero selects $bbca's STEPPING half, zero its measuring half. Raised as $ffff by the restart arm and cleared by the same arm that clears panel_frame_index at $c, so it is the animation's own "running" flag. Four operand sites, three inside $bbca and the fourth stage_reset_state's clear.
var 0xbd2e panel_frame_dwell
cmt 0xbd2e Frames left on the current panel_frame_index: reloaded to 4 by the restart arm and by every index step, decremented on every other frame of the stepping half. NOT spare -- WB_PANEL_FRAME_SPARE in recreate/include/wonderboy.h was a name from before the body was read, and is now WB_PANEL_FRAME_DWELL.
var 0xbd30 panel_frame_rewind
cmt 0xbd30 Nonzero winds panel_frame_delay back UP to $500 in steps of $14 a frame; at or past $500 (a SIGNED compare, so a delay wound negative is wound on rather than clamped) the delay is clamped and this and panel_frame_hold are both cleared. Exactly TWO operand sites besides $bbca's own: the `move.w #$ffff,$bd30` at $5218, one instruction before the matching raise of panel_frame_hold. It is the ONE word of the five that stage_reset_state does not touch.
var 0x22080 bg_tile_index_tail
cmt 0x22080 The last EIGHT entries of bg_tile_index ($21e90 + 496), written by stage_reset_state's four `move.l #imm,(a1)+`: map bytes $f8..$ff name tiles $46,$47,$48,$49,$4a,$4b,$40,$4d. Not "rather than shipped" -- the .PRG ships no part of bg_tile_index at all (it is past $218d0 and loaded from disk), so this is an OVERWRITE of eight entries of a runtime-loaded table. The band ends exactly at bg_map_row_stride, which is what fixes bg_tile_index's 256-entry length from the other side.
var 0x24898 resource_table_header
cmt 0x24898 Past the program's last byte, so loaded from disk. Byte 0 is resource_table_relocate's 'E' signature and word 8 is its `dbf` count. Four operand sites: $e62a, $e64e, $e87e and $fe20.
var 0x248d8 resource_table
cmt 0x248d8 Twenty-byte SPRITE DESCRIPTORS past the program, whose FIRST longword resource_table_relocate turns from an offset into an absolute pointer by adding this address. The sprite pass at $8f02 is the reader, and it reads ten of the twenty bytes in five fields: 0 = source cell data (longword, the relocated pointer, handed to a blitter in a0); 4 = width-code byte, `ext.w`ed (0..3 -> the three jump tables, i.e. 2..5 16-pixel columns; widthcode+1 is also the 10-byte source cells per row); 5 = height byte, `ext.w`ed, so a negative height is representable and reaches a blitter through the bottom clamp; 8 = x offset word and 10 = y offset word, each added to the screen record's own. The other ten bytes are read by nothing reconstructed. Five operand sites: $1cc8, $8f0a, $e8d8 and the relocator's two ($fe32, $fe3c).
fn 0xe110 bg_plot_round_banner
cmt 0xe110 48 bytes, reconstructed. `lea $e19a.l,a6 / bsr bg_plot_banner` draws "ROUND BONUS" into background buffer copy 0; then `move.w $b6fa.l,d0 / cmp.w $b6f8.l,d0 / bne` -- EQUALITY of hud_meter_value and hud_meter_max alone, so a meter above its own maximum takes the ordinary arm -- and on equality `move.l #$10000,d0 / bsr bcd_add_score_bd70` and a second banner, "PERFECT!  10000 PTS". ONE caller, $8287 in ../decomp.c's numbering.
fn 0xe140 bg_plot_banner
cmt 0xe140 42 bytes, reconstructed. Plots the banner record a6 names into background buffer copy 0: a word of byte offset into $44000 (sign-extended through `lea 0(a1,d0.w),a1`), then characters, each `- $20` as a BYTE and `lsl.w #5` into the font at $1387c, until `tst.b (a6) / bpl` sees a byte with bit 7 set. The terminator is a SIGN test and not a compare against $ff, and it is NOT consumed -- a6 comes back pointing at it. TWO `bsr` callers, both inside bg_plot_round_banner ($e116, $e13a).
fn 0xe16a bg_plot_banner_glyph
cmt 0xe16a 48 bytes, reconstructed. One 8x8 glyph into the background buffer: 8 rows of `move.b (a0)+,{0,2,4,6}(a1)` a WB_BG_BUFFER_LINE (128) apart, then `move.w a1,d0 / btst #0,d0` and `lea -1023(a1),a1` or `lea -1017(a1),a1` -- the cursor for the NEXT 8-pixel cell, one byte on from an even one and seven from an odd one, since two cells share each 8-byte plane group. The same cell layout text_plot_glyph uses over the message buffer's 88-byte line, but a DIFFERENT font and a different destination pitch, so the two are not one routine. ONE `bsr` caller, $e160. No `proto`: a0 and a1 in, and a1 is the whole result.
var 0xe19a bg_banner_round
var 0xe1a8 bg_banner_perfect
cmt 0xe19a The two banner records bg_plot_round_banner names, in the code between $e16a's `rts` and $e1ce: {word buffer offset, characters, $ff}. $e19a is $0c28 + "ROUND BONUS" and $e1a8 is $1418 + "PERFECT!  10000 PTS". Each has exactly one operand site, the `lea` that names it.
var 0x1387c bg_banner_font
cmt 0x1387c A SECOND font, 32 bytes per glyph from char $20 up like text_glyph_table, and shipped in the .PRG. EXACTLY ONE abs.l operand site in the whole image -- bg_plot_banner's own `lea` at $e156 -- so nothing else in the game draws from it.


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The SCENE tier (batch 19), $dbc0..$dfbd + $dfbe, reconstructed in
# recreate/src/scene.c EXCEPT $dfbe. game_main_loop runs $dbc0 once a frame,
# between panel_refresh_frame and $882, and it returns at once unless one of
# the two mode flags is negative. state_flag_a30 selects the two arms driven by
# the descriptor record_ptr_10420 names (kind 1 = an NPC speech script, kind 2 =
# the SHOP), state_flag_a32 the arm kind 4 selects (a defeated boss's eight
# fragments) -- which is the first thing anything has established about EITHER
# flag beyond "it is a boolean".
#
# THE READING IS PINNED BY THE MESSAGE TABLE, batch 17's method: the two ids the
# farewell arm hardcodes resolve to message 9 " Please come again." and message
# $12 "  Never Come Back!!", and the ids of speech script 0 (4,5,6,7) to "Hey
# brave man, / listen carefully.", "The country's fate / depends on you.", "Your
# mission is to destroy the vicious Dragon and restore peace." and "Take the
# sword and medicine. Good Luck!". So this is the game's DIALOGUE tier. What any
# shipped shop SELLS is NOT pinned: both the descriptor table ($21828) and the
# shop records ($21a28..) lie past the program and are loaded from disk.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
fn 0xdbc0 scene_run_frame
cmt 0xdbc0 932 bytes ([$dbc0,$de80) + [$deba,$df9e), the two spans Ghidra's body covers), RECONSTRUCTED in recreate/src/scene.c. ONE BOUNDARY LEFT, and it is not the one this plate was written for: four of its exits transfer to scene_exit_and_reload ($dfbe), which batch 27 RECONSTRUCTED, so those four now run to the end -- the arm, the exit, the dispatched exit action and the whole stage reload. The fifth, through scene_spend_visit_budget to $1ab4, is still unported and its cases still stop the oracle there with the kit's stop_pc. The C reports WHICH exit it took either way, and every such case also requires the oracle's executed-PC coverage to hold the transfer instruction it names; recreate/STATUS.md's batch-27 section lists what the remaining arm leaves unpinned. Structure: `tst.w $a30.w / bmi` picks the descriptor arms and `tst.w $a32.w / bmi` the boss arm; 2(record_ptr_10420) is the KIND. Kind 1 posts the byte under speech_script_cursor on each FIRE edge (`tst.b d0 / bpl`, the sign bit alone) with a lifetime of ZERO and advances the cursor, a byte with bit 7 set ending the scene. Kind 2 is the shop: two waits (scene_message_pending, then scene_ack_wait -- the second tests the WHOLE edge byte, not its sign), then shop_request 0 = greet, 3 = farewell, 2 = item 2 and EVERYTHING ELSE = item 1 (there is no third compare). A purchase compares the price against bcd_counter_bd6e with a SIGNED `bgt` and, if it can be paid, calls bcd_sub_counter_bd6e, posts the item's first/repeat message, bumps its count, dispatches through effect_handler_table and spends 3 off the visit budget; a purchase it cannot pay for falls into the GREETING arm, countdown and all. Kind 4 frees ten actor slots and refills eight of them from boss_fragment_params. TWO absolute operands in it are a shipped SLIP -- see the cmt at $dcd4.
fn 0xde80 scene_spend_visit_budget
cmt 0xde80 58 bytes, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/scene.c). `sub.w d0,32(a1) / bmi` -- a WORD subtract off the shop record's visit budget, so the BORROW is the result's sign and a budget that wraps past $8000 closes the visit exactly as an exhausted one does. Every message costs 2 and every purchase 3, from the four `bsr` sites inside scene_run_frame ($dc5a, $dd0c, $dd76, $de04) and $de7a's. On the borrow: `jsr $1af0.w` (map_stamp_block) stamps the 2x2 block into the collision map, then the cell scene_marker_cell_ptr names is cleared TOGETHER WITH ITS TWIN -- the byte is compared against its RIGHT neighbour first and its LEFT one second and the matching one is cleared too. A cell matching NEITHER takes `jmp $1ab4.w`, the tail the reconstruction does not follow (it is stage_load_window again). a1 and d0 in; d0's low word only. No `proto`: the register interface is above.
fn 0xdfbe scene_exit_and_reload
cmt 0xdfbe 104 bytes ($dfbe..$e025, ending exactly where scene_message_pending sits), RECONSTRUCTED WHOLE in batch 27 (recreate/src/scene.c, differential in recreate/test/test_scene.py). LEAVE THE SCENE AND RELOAD THE STAGE. In the original's own order: `movea.l $10420.l,a6 / move.w 18(a6),d0 / lsl.w #2 / lea $1019c.l,a6 / lea 0(a6,d0.w),a6 / movea.l (a6),a6 / jsr (a6)` runs the descriptor's EXIT ACTION out of scene_exit_action_table; `clr.b $c031.l` takes the message box down; a0 := $22090, a6 := bg_tile_bitmaps and a1 := the stage_start_table entry that 28(record_ptr_10420) selects; `clr.w $d76.w`; `jsr $f95c.l` (stage_load_window); then state_flag_a34, panel_frame_hold, state_flag_a30, state_flag_a32 and scene_message_pending are cleared and it returns. CORRECTIONS to this plate's earlier text, all cited to bytes: (a) the two clears are NOT a pair -- $dfd8's `clr.b $c031.l` is the instruction right after the dispatch, while $e002's `clr.w $d76.w` is the LAST instruction before the call, so scroll_follow_frozen is cleared AFTER the three pointers are computed and this path ALWAYS hands the hinge an unfrozen scroll, however it was entered; (b) `lea $22090.l,a0` is not an anonymous address -- $22090 is bg_map_row_stride, so a0 is the LEVEL MAP itself and its header width word IS the global stride word for this caller (where every other caller of $f95c keeps the two apart); (c) record_ptr_10420 is read TWICE, at $dfbe and again at $dfea, so the dispatched action could in principle change which start record is picked -- none of the eight does, and $10420 has one writer in the whole image ($162e); (d) NEITHER index is bounded, and the WORD shift makes that sharper than "outside 0..7": `lsl.w #2` wraps inside 16 bits before the SIGN-EXTENDED add, so what selects the entry is the OFFSET and THIRTY-TWO index values reach the eight-entry table -- 0..7 and the three aliasing bands $4000..$4007, $8000..$8007 and $c000..$c007, all of which the original dispatches normally. Only an offset that genuinely leaves the table makes the `jsr` go through a longword outside it; the port refuses exactly that case (blit's sprite_dispatch precedent) and reproduces the aliases. The START index has the same shape and is NOT refused at all, because it is a data READ (a case drives $ffff, which reads the longword four bytes BELOW the table). FOUR transfers reach it from scene_run_frame -- `bmi.w` at $dc12, `beq.w` at $dc52, `bra.w` at $dc5e and `bsr.w` at $df98 -- and no other site in the image names it.
var 0x1019c scene_exit_action_table
cmt 0x1019c EIGHT longwords, $1019c..$101bb, bounded by the first of its own targets ($101bc, whose whole body is `rts`). Every entry is now reconstructed code, which is what let $dfbe be ported without a boundary: 0 = $101bc scene_exit_action_none, 1 = $101be scene_exit_action_select_a30_table, and 2..7 = the six effects.h `set_state_*` stubs at $10200/$1020a/$10214/$1021e/$10228/$10232 (bbc8_1ff, bbc8_2ff, bbc8_3ff, bbc8_4ff, bbc8_6ff, 6f9c_ffff, in that order). ONE operand site: the abs.l longword at $dfcc, inside scene_exit_and_reload's `lea $1019c.l,a6` (whose opcode word is at $dfca -- the $dfc8 this plate used to name is the `lsl.w` before it). Indexed by 18(record_ptr_10420) with `lsl.w #2` and a SIGN-EXTENDED word add, so nothing bounds the index -- and because the shift wraps inside the WORD, 32 index values reach the table rather than 8: 0..7 plus $4000..$4007, $8000..$8007 and $c000..$c007, which alias onto the same eight entries. WHICH entries the SHIPPED data selects is not answerable from the image: the descriptor table at $21828 lies past the program and is loaded from disk, and the $a8 bytes of it the .PRG does carry are zeros -- which name entry 0.
fn 0x101bc scene_exit_action_none
cmt 0x101bc Two bytes, an `rts` and nothing else: entry 0 of scene_exit_action_table, the exit action that does nothing, RECONSTRUCTED in batch 27 as the array's first element. It also BOUNDS the table -- being the first of its own targets is what says the table is eight entries and not nine.
fn 0x101be scene_exit_action_select_a30_table
cmt 0x101be 66 bytes ($101be..$101ff, ending exactly where the first set_state_* stub begins), entry 1 of scene_exit_action_table, RECONSTRUCTED in batch 27 (recreate/src/scene.c). In order: `move.w #$1ff,$bbc6.l` (hud_slot_bbc6, the same word effect_set_bbc6_01ff at $10390 writes); `move.w #$1,$21e4.w` (effect_state_21e4); `clr.b $b444.l` -- the effect record list's first BYTE, which the $b444 plate reads as the 0..4 attack level, so this clears that and not the list, and the byte beside it survives; `move.l #$996c,$a098.l` (actor_table_selected := actor_table_default); `jsr $1b68.w` (actor_alloc_slot_low); `move.l #$9bd0,$a098.l` -- actor_table_selected is immediately republished as actor_table_a30, so the table the allocation ran against is NOT the one left selected; then `cmpa.l #$0,a1 / bne.w $101f6` and, only when the allocator found a slot, `lea $21c58.l,a1 / addq.w #1,(a1)`. THE ALLOCATED RECORD IS DISCARDED -- a1 is never written through -- so the allocation's only lasting effect is that counter. THE PUBLISH/ALLOCATE/REPUBLISH ORDER IS THE ONLY THING A DIFFERENTIAL CAN SEE OF IT, and only indirectly: the two `move.l`s leave one longword behind whichever way round they run, so the test seeds a free record in exactly one of the three tables and reads the ordering off whether the counter moved.
var 0x21c58 scene_exit_alloc_count
cmt 0x21c58 Word counter, incremented by scene_exit_action_select_a30_table when its actor_alloc_slot_low found a free slot (reconstructed batch 27). ONE operand site in the whole image -- the `lea $21c58.l` at $101f8 -- so nothing ever READS it, established by a byte scan for the abs.l operand at every even offset. It lies past the program's own end ($218d0), so the .PRG ships no part of it. The bump is `addq.w #1,(a1)` through that `lea`, so it wraps inside the word.
cmt 0xdcd4 A SHIPPED SLIP, and it is here TWICE. `cmpi.w #$1,$2c.l` ($0c79 0001 0000002c) and `cmpi.w #$1,$28.l` at $dd42 read the 68000 VECTOR PAGE -- the high words of the Line-F and Line-A exception vectors -- where the sibling test one branch above each reads a RECORD field at the very same displacement (`cmpi.w #$0,44(a1)` at $dcba, `cmpi.w #$0,40(a1)` at $dd2a). The encodings differ by exactly the lost `(a1)`: $0c69 0001 002c is what was meant. Under TOS both vectors hold ROM addresses, so neither word can be 1 and the MIDDLE arm of each pair is dead on hardware. At $dcd4 that is harmless -- both arms post the same id $12 -- but at $dd42 it costs the shop its second greeting message: 16(a1) is unreachable and 18(a1) is posted from the second greeting on. recreate/test/test_scene.py reaches both middle arms by seeding the vector page, which is all the instruction actually reads.
var 0x1017c speech_script_cursor
cmt 0x1017c A LONGWORD cursor over a script of one-byte message ids, not a word: `addq.l #1,$1017c.l`. SIX operand sites -- $19ec's `clr.w` (which clears only the HIGH half), $1a8c's `move.l (a0),$1017c` that loads it from speech_script_table, $1a92/$1aa6 (a post-and-advance identical to this one but with lifetime $ffff instead of 0), and scene_run_frame's own $dc0a/$dc22. A byte with its SIGN BIT set ends the scene; the terminator is not consumed.
var 0x1015c speech_script_table
cmt 0x1015c EIGHT longwords, $1015c..$1017b, bounded by speech_script_cursor itself and by its own first target. Indexed at $1a82 by `lsl.w #2`. The scripts are SHIPPED, at $10180..$1019b: 04 05 06 07 ff / 22 23 24 25 ff / 2f 30 31 ff / 45 46 ff / 68 69 6a ... -- resolved through text_message_table, script 0 is the game's opening speech and script 4 the emblem award.
var 0x10180 speech_scripts
var 0x10448 shop_record_ptr
cmt 0x10448 Longword: whichever of shop_record_table's eight pointers $1bcc last planted. FIVE readers besides scene_run_frame's four ($dc48, $dcac, $dd24, $dd9a, $de10): $1cd2, $1d52, $52c0 and $52e0, the last two posting 66(a1) and 68(a1) as message ids -- batch 34 read that pair: they are actor_behavior_type34's, posted when the shop CURSOR arrives on the right-hand item ($be, bought as shop_request 2) and on the left-hand one ($33, bought as 1). The record it names is 70 bytes and lies PAST THE PROGRAM, so nothing about its contents is shipped.
var 0x10428 shop_record_table
cmt 0x10428 EIGHT longwords, $10428..$10447, bounded by shop_record_ptr itself: $21a28, $21a6e, $21ab4, $21afa, $21b40, $21b86, $21bcc, $21c12 -- a stride of $46, which is what gives the shop record its length. All eight are past the program's end ($218d0). ONE operand site, $1bc0's `lea`.
var 0xe026 scene_message_pending
cmt 0xe026 Word, $ffff while a scene message is up and the driver is waiting for it to be acknowledged. EIGHT operand sites: five writers inside scene_run_frame ($dcb2, $ddb4, $dd1c, $de2a -- all `move.w #$ffff`), $1dc8's, the `clr.w` at $e01e inside scene_exit_and_reload, and two readers ($dc2a, $525a).
var 0xe028 shop_request
cmt 0xe028 Word: WHAT the player asked the shop for. Written 1, 2 or 3 at $5308/$531a/$532c, each behind a `cmpi.w #$33/$be/$78,(a0)`. CORRECTION (batch 34): that word is WB_ACTOR_X, not a spawn type -- the three values are the three POSITIONS actor_behavior_type34 parks the shop's cursor record at, and the same handler writes them. So the request is what the cursor was pointing at when fire went down, and the mapping is not positional: $33 (left) = 1, $78 (middle) = 3, $be (right) = 2. scene_run_frame clears it on every arm that serves it; 0 means "nothing asked", and any value OTHER than 2 or 3 is served as item 1.
var 0xe02a scene_ack_wait
cmt 0xe02a Word: the driver is waiting for the player to acknowledge the box. FIVE operand sites -- `move.w #$ffff` at $1dd4 and $1de0, the read/clear pair at $dc64/$dc8a, and a second reader at $5264. Unlike scene_message_pending's arm, the test that clears it reads the WHOLE joystick edge byte (`bne`), not its sign.
var 0xe02c shop_greet_countdown
cmt 0xe02c Word, `subq.w #1` once a frame by scene_run_frame's greeting arm, which fires when the DECREMENTED word is zero -- so a countdown seeded 0 wraps and takes 65536 frames. Exactly TWO operand sites: $1e9c's `move.w #$fa` (250 frames) and $dd12's own.
var 0xe02e scene_marker_cell_ptr
cmt 0xe02e Longword, planted `move.l a6,$e02e.l` at $1964 and read at $19b4 and by scene_spend_visit_budget at $de8e. It names a COLLISION-MAP cell: the exhausted visit clears it and whichever of its two horizontal neighbours holds the same tile code.
var 0x1079a scene_exit_request
cmt 0x1079a Word: raised $ffff at $10768, one instruction after message $63 " Offensive Power Increased." is posted and `addq.b #1,$b444` bumps the attack level. THREE operand sites -- that writer and scene_run_frame's read/clear pair at $df86/$df92 -- so the boss-defeat arm is what consumes it.
var 0xdfac boss_defeat_flag
cmt 0xdfac Word, and it sits INSIDE THE CODE, between actor_slots_mark_free's `rts` and boss_fragment_params. Raised $ffff at $6bd4 -- inside the routine that follows actor_damage_template_hitpoints, behind `tst.w $a32.w` and `cmpa.l #$9e94,a0`, i.e. when the record at slot 3 of actor_table_a32 is the one that just died. THREE operand sites; the other two are scene_run_frame's read and clear at $deba/$dec4.
var 0xdfae boss_fragment_params
cmt 0xdfae SIXTEEN bytes, $dfae..$dfbd, ending exactly where scene_exit_and_reload begins -- two per fragment, and SYMMETRIC: (08,0f) (0a,0c) (0c,08) (0e,04) and then those four backwards. The first byte of a pair goes into BOTH 10(a1) and 11(a1) (`move.b (a2),10(a1)` then `move.b (a2)+,11(a1)` read the same byte), the second into 31(a1); and the loop's `cmp.w #$3,d6 / ble` raises bit 3 of 8(a1) for the first four fragments and clears it for the last four, so the eight leave in mirrored pairs. ONE operand site, the `lea $dfae.l,a2` at $df10.
var 0x9eb4 boss_fragment_slots
cmt 0x9eb4 actor_table_a32 + 4 records: the eight slots the boss-defeat arm frees and then fills. TWO operand sites, both inside that arm ($ded8's `lea` for the free, $df0a's for the fill). The free ahead of the fill is not redundant -- a descriptor whose 4(a1) is zero returns with the eight left holding actor_free_marker.
var 0x9e94 boss_fragment_origin
cmt 0x9e94 Slot 3 of actor_table_a32, read as a LONGWORD (`move.l $9e94.l,d0` at $df16) so that its x and y together become all eight fragments' starting position. Read ONCE, before the loop. TWO operand sites; the other is the `cmpa.l #$9e94,a0` at $6bc0 that decides whether a death raises boss_defeat_flag -- so the slot whose death starts the sequence is the slot the fragments come out of.
var 0x21828 scene_descriptor_table
cmt 0x21828 The 32-byte SCENE DESCRIPTORS record_ptr_10420 points into: `lsl.w #5,d0 / lea $21828.l,a1 / lea 0(a1,d0.w),a1 / move.l a1,$10420.l` at $162e. Only $a8 bytes of it are inside the program and those are ZERO, so the table is loaded from disk like the shop records. Fields read by recovered code: 2 = the KIND (1 speech / 2 shop / 4 boss defeat), 4 = the boss arm's variant (0 spawns nothing, 1 picks fragment type $1c, anything else $1d), 12 = the packed-BCD GOLD AWARD hud_award_gold_from_descriptor pays out (batch 33, through record_ptr_10424), 18 = the exit action scene_exit_and_reload dispatches, 24 = the collision-map cell map_stamp_block stamps, 28 = the index into stage_start_table that scene_exit_and_reload hands stage_load_window. Words 18 and 28 gained header #defines in batch 27 (WB_SCENE_EXIT_ACTION, WB_SCENE_START_INDEX) when $dfbe was ported; because the shipped bytes are zeros, NO shipped datum selects any exit action but entry 0 or any start entry but 0, and every other value a case drives is its own seed.
var 0x1d3ec stage_start_table
cmt 0x1d3ec EIGHT longwords, $1d3ec..$1d40b, indexed by 28(record_ptr_10420) at $dff6 with `lsl.w #2` and handed to stage_load_window in a1. Entry 0 is $217d8 and the eight are $a apart -- and $a is stage_load_window's own START RECORD length, which batch 26 read off that routine (words 0/2 = the map cell the window opens on, 4/6 = the followed object's position, byte 8 = the tune, byte 9 = the palette row). The count is SELF-BOUNDING: the eight entries end exactly at $1d40c, where the five records the .PRG itself ships begin. Their targets $217d8.. are the startup relocator's own bytes, dead by the time this table is used, so the records there are loaded over it rather than shipped. Nothing bounds the INDEX. The name was table_1d3ec while the meaning was unread.
var 0x1d40c stage_start_records
cmt 0x1d40c The FIVE start records the .PRG itself ships, $1d40c..$1d43d, ten bytes each -- the ones the `lea $1d40c/$1d416/$1d420/$1d42a/$1d434.l,a1` sites at $1f14/$1e74/$1e7e/$1ad6/$e0c4 hand stage_load_window. The run is bounded on BOTH sides by its neighbours: it begins where stage_start_table's eight longwords end and ends exactly where bg_tile_bitmaps begins, which is the third independent reading of the ten-byte record length. Contents: all five open on map cell (0,0); $1d40c follows position (100,100) and tunes 3, $1d416 tunes 2, $1d420 tunes 1, $1d434 tunes 4 -- all on palette row 0 -- and $1d42a carries tune $ff, the NEGATIVE byte that makes stage_load_window stop the sound module instead of starting a song, on palette row 1. So both arms of that tail and two palette rows are reachable from shipped data alone, which is what recreate/test/test_stage.py's cases are built from.
var 0xfc46 palette_table
cmt 0xfc46 EIGHT rows of 32 bytes ($fc46..$fd45), each one whole 16-word ST palette. ONE reference in the image, the `lea $fc46.l,a1` at $f9e0, and the extent is self-bounding: the eighth row ends exactly where bg_build_preshifted_copies ($fd46) begins. stage_load_window indexes it with `9(stage_start_ptr) << 5` and nothing bounds that byte, so a record naming row 8 or above hands set_palette the code after the table. Every word's high nibble is 0 and the other three are 0..7, which is the ST shifter's own format -- the reading that says this is a palette table and not something near one.

# --- addenda to blocks batch 19 did not otherwise touch ---------------------
cmt 0x10394 ADDENDUM (batch 19): these four are the ONLY handlers in effect_handler_table that touch an ADDRESS register. `movea.l $b546,a1` survives the dispatcher's `jsr (a0)`, so a1 comes back pointing at the record just pushed -- and scene_run_frame's `move.w #$3,d0 / bsr $de80` two instructions later spends the visit budget through THAT pointer, writing 32 bytes into effect_record_list instead of onto 32(shop record). Nothing restores a1 in between. Pinned per handler by recreate/test/test_scene.py's test_only_the_push_handlers_move_the_dispatchers_record_pointer.
cmt 0x1023a ADDENDUM (batch 19): the count is BOUNDED, not counted -- entry 0 holds $10296 and the table starts at $1023a, so the 23 longwords end exactly where the first handler begins. The index is `62(a1)`/`64(a1)` scaled by `lsl.w #2` and added as a SIGN-EXTENDED word, so nothing bounds it from either end; recreate/src/scene.c refuses an out-of-range index the way sprite_draw_pass refuses a width code past its own table.


# ===========================================================================
# BATCH 28 (coverage-wall scout) -- THE PER-ACTOR BEHAVIOUR DISPATCH
#
# PORTABILITY.md 0j measured 26,878 bytes (49.0 % of believed CODE) that Ghidra
# never disassembled, concentrated in nine gaps. ONE mechanism opens EIGHT of
# those nine, and it is four instructions long:
#
#   00928  moveq #0,d1 / move.w 4(a0),d1 / lsl.w #2,d1
#   00930  lea (0x938,PC,d1.w),a1     <- 43fb 1006, PC-relative INDEXED lea
#   00934  movea.l (a1),a1
#   00936  jmp (a1)
#
# The base address $938 exists NOWHERE in the image as an operand -- it is the
# 8-bit displacement of a PC-relative INDEXED brief extension word (`1006` =
# index d1.w, scale 1, disp 6; PC = $932, the extension word's own address).
# Nothing else in the image names it.
# Ghidra did not resolve it (evidence: neither the table nor ANY of its 61
# distinct targets is in a function body, while every table reached by a plain
# `lea abs.l` in this program is), which is the "lea-extension-word gap" of
# docs/m68k-disassembly.md. The index is the actor TYPE at offset 4 of the
# record; nothing bounds it at either end.
#
# The 24 `bne.w $6bb8` sites the actor_defeat_and_score plate lists all fall
# inside 24 DISTINCT table slots (2,3,4,5,6,7,9,10,11,12,14,15,16,17,18,20,21,
# 22,23,24,25,26,27,38); the four `bra.w` sites add slots 13, 19 and two more
# in 38. That is the corroboration: the plate's "per-monster state routine" is
# literally a slot in this table.
#
# Verification: every fn address below was disassembled with
#   m68k-elf-objdump -D -b binary -m m68k:68000 --adjust-vma=0x3dc \
#     --start-address=... --stop-address=... bin/disk1/AUTO/SWB.PRG
# and decodes to a clean instruction stream terminating in rts / bra.w / jmp.
# ===========================================================================

fn 0x8d0 actor_behavior_pass
cmt 0x8d0 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, differential in recreate/test/test_behavior.py). 88 bytes, $8d0..$927. THE PER-FRAME ACTOR PASS, and the caller game_main_loop reaches through $882 (`bsr $88c` = joy1_latch_edge, then `bsr $8d0`). Walks actor_table_selected ($a098) in 32-byte records: `cmpi.l #$ffffffff,(a0)` ends the walk, `cmpi.w #$ffbe,(a0)` (actor_free_marker) skips a free slot, everything else goes to actor_dispatch_behavior and then `lea 32(a0),a0`. Guarded by state_flag_a34: when it is CLEAR the walk is the normal loop at $8e0; when it is SET ($904) the pass is a FIXED three-record path. TWO CORRECTIONS, cited to bytes (batch 29): the third record is +$160 from SLOT 1, i.e. base+384 = SLOT 12 -- actor_table_selected's FOLLOWED slot -- not slot 13; and it is NOT skipped on the free marker. Only the first two carry `cmpi.w #$ffbe,(a0) / beq`; $920 is `lea 352(a0),a0` followed straight by the `bra.w $928` tail at $924, so a FREE followed slot is dispatched on whatever type word its 32 bytes hold. The A34 arm also never tests the $ffffffff end marker at all. Body $8d0..$927.
fn 0x928 actor_dispatch_behavior
cmt 0x928 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 16 bytes, $928..$937. THE WAY IN to the whole per-actor behaviour tier, and the reason 15,698 bytes of it were never disassembled. Four instructions: `moveq #0,d1 / move.w 4(a0),d1` (the actor TYPE), `lsl.w #2,d1`, `lea (0x938,PC,d1.w),a1` -- encoded `43fb 1006`, PC = $932 so the base is $938 -- `movea.l (a1),a1`, `jmp (a1)`. The `jmp` is a TAIL CALL: the handler's own `rts` returns to actor_behavior_pass. Ghidra does not follow a PC-relative INDEXED lea, which is why actor_behavior_table and all 61 of its targets were invisible. THE DISPATCH IS ON THE WRAPPED OFFSET, batch 27's lesson at a bigger table: `lsl.w #2` wraps inside 16 bits and the brief extension word then adds the result SIGN-EXTENDED, so the OFFSET selects the entry and not the index. Types $4000..$403d, $8000..$803d and $c000..$c03d alias onto slots 0..61 exactly as 0..61 do -- 248 of the 65,536 type values dispatch a table entry, and a guard on the raw type would refuse 186 the original dispatches. Every other type reads a longword outside the table (state_flag_a30 at $a30 is the first word past it) and jumps to it; the reconstruction REFUSES those, since no C stands in for a `jmp` through arbitrary data. It ends exactly where the table begins.
var 0x938 actor_behavior_table
cmt 0x938 PINNED ENTRY BY ENTRY by recreate/src/behavior.c's BEHAVIOR_SLOTS against this file's own fn addresses. SIXTY-TWO longwords, $938..$a2f, one per actor type, indexed by 4(record) via actor_dispatch_behavior's `lea (0x938,PC,d1.w),a1`. ZERO operand sites carry the address $938 -- the only reference is that PC-relative extension word, which is why no linear or reference-following scan found it. Bounded above by its own first target: slot 0 holds $a36 and the words at $a30/$a32/$a34 (state_flag_a30/a32/a34) sit between the table and it, so table + flags + `rts` = $938..$a37. 61 distinct targets; slots 0 and 58 share the bare `rts` at $a36. Twenty-five slots (2..7, 9..27 minus 8) open with the monster prologue and are the "per-monster state routine" family actor_defeat_and_score's plate counted from the other end. Slots 8 and 59 are two-instruction stubs that set a bit in 30(a0) and fall into slot 7's body at $7060.
fn 0x698a actor_spawn_anim_step
cmt 0x698a RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 50 bytes, $698a..$69bb. CORRECTION (batch 29): this is the SPAWN animation, not a vanish or death one. Bit 2 of 9(a0) has exactly TWO operand sites in the whole image -- `bset #2,9(a1)` at $10044 inside actor_spawn_from_template and the `bclr #2,9(a0)` at $69b0 in this body -- so the spawn raises it and this routine is the only thing that lowers it: a record plays this and nothing else until it wraps, then runs its real handler. The shared tail every monster handler branches to on `btst #2,9(a0)` -- TWENTY-FIVE in-code `bne.w $698a` sites, more than any other branch target in the image outside actor_defeat_and_score. Steps the animation cursor 18(a0) two bytes at a time through actor_spawn_anim_frames at $69be, writing each frame to 6(a0); when the cursor wraps ($20 masked to 0 by `andi.w #$1f`) it clears bit 2 of 9(a0) and zeroes the cursor. Reached only by branch, never by a call, and ends in `rts` back into actor_behavior_pass through the handler's own frame.
var 0x69be actor_spawn_anim_frames
cmt 0x69be The sprite ids actor_spawn_anim_step cycles, addressed `lea $69be,a1 / lea 0(a1,d0.w),a1` with d0 = 18(a0) -- so the cursor is a BYTE OFFSET, not an index. As laid out the run is 32 words, $69be..$69fd, ending exactly where actor_damage_followed begins: $1ab x5, $1ac x5, $1ad x5, $1ae x5, $1af x5, $1ad x4, $1ac x3. But actor_spawn_anim_step's wrap is `andi.w #$1f`, which reaches only the FIRST 16 words ($69be..$69dd), so the animation is $1ab x5, $1ac x5, $1ad x5, $1ae x1 and round. ANSWERED (batch 29): NOTHING reads the second half. The `lea $69be.l` at $6994 is the only reference to any address in $69be..$69fd anywhere in the image and no other operand computes one, so $69de..$69fd is 32 bytes of unreached data -- the batch-28 open question, closed by scan rather than left registered.

# --- the 61 behaviour handlers, one per distinct actor_behavior_table slot ---
fn 0xa36 actor_behavior_null
cmt 0xa36 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). Two bytes, a bare `rts`: actor_behavior_table slots 0 AND 58, reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`, never by a call. It also BOUNDS the table from above -- the table plus the three state_flag_a30/a32/a34 words end exactly on it. Being reconstructed is what makes actor_behavior_pass testable end to end: a table of type-0 records runs the whole walk in both cores.
fn 0xa38 actor_behavior_type01_player # ctx
cmt 0xa38 actor_behavior_table slot 1; reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`, never by a call; decoded code runs $a38..$a75 and the `rts` at $a74 is its own -- the earlier plate's "$a38..$b18" ran through player_meter_empty_check and stage_reset_block. It is NINE `bsr`s in a row and nothing else: player_meter_empty_check ($a76), player_pending_event_gate ($b1a) -- whose NEGATIVE d7 skips everything below -- player_gate_on_1516 ($d78), player_step_and_arm ($ec8), player_weapon_fire ($1208), actor_fall_and_settle ($1334, only while actor_platform_ridden is clear), player_apply_joystick ($d84), player_collide_and_scroll ($151a, only while state_flag_a32 is clear) and player_stage_transition ($1f54). As of batch 40 phase B SIX of the nine are reconstructed ($a76, $d78, $ec8, $1208, $d84 and the shared $1334) and THREE are not ($b1a, $151a, $1f54), so the row itself is still the dispatch table's ONE unported entry.
fn 0x2462 actor_behavior_type02
cmt 0x2462 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 30). Code $2462..$255f (254 bytes), then 96 bytes of frame words to $25bf. actor_behavior_table slot 2, reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`. THE WALKER THAT NEVER WALKS: while it is alive it calls actor_fall_and_settle and nothing else that moves it -- its side flag and its frame list follow the followed record's x and it takes NO map step at all. That x is read as `move.w $9aec.l,d1`, i.e. followed_actor_default DIRECTLY, so this handler faces the default record even while state_flag_a32 names the other one; the compare is INCLUSIVE where actor_set_side_flag's is strict. Its contact head is the band's shared one (`bsr $23b6 / tst.w d7`, then `bsr $5c6e` with bit 1 -> `bra.w $69fe` and bit 2 -> the struck arm). The eighteen bytes at $24d4 ARE actor_advance_anim16's, spelt inline rather than called. Bit 0 of 9(a0) switches to a sixteen-frame death animation that recoils 3 pixels AWAY each frame (`btst #3,8(a0)` SET steps RIGHT, the opposite arm to $2f22's) and stops recoiling once bit 3 of 9(a0) is up; on the wrap it lowers bit 0, `bclr #3,9(a0) / bne.w $6bb8` transfers to actor_defeat_and_score -- and that transfer SKIPS the `move.b d0,18(a0)` at $255a, so the cursor keeps the last frame's offset on the frame the record dies.
var 0x2560 actor_type02_dead_left_frames
cmt 0x2560 16 words, $2560..$257f: sprites $f8/$f9 in groups of four. Reached by `lea $2560(pc,d0.w),a1` at $2516 -- a PC-relative INDEXED lea, the same class PORTABILITY.md 0k is about, which is why nothing named these bytes before.
var 0x2580 actor_type02_dead_right_frames
cmt 0x2580 16 words, $2580..$259f: sprites $f4/$f5, the mirror of actor_type02_dead_left_frames. `lea $2580(pc,d0.w),a1` at $2536.
var 0x25a0 actor_type02_walk_left_frames
cmt 0x25a0 8 words, $25a0..$25af: sprites $f2/$f3, four frames each. `lea $25a0.l,a1` at $24b8, the arm that also raises bit 3 of 8(a0).
var 0x25b0 actor_type02_walk_right_frames
cmt 0x25b0 8 words, $25b0..$25bf: sprites $f6/$f7. `lea $25b0.l,a1` at $24c8.
fn 0x25c0 actor_behavior_type03
cmt 0x25c0 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 30). Code $25c0..$2735 (374 bytes), then 96 bytes of frame words to $2795. actor_behavior_table slot 3. THE PATROLLER: it walks 2 pixels a frame and turns round TWICE OVER -- 30(a0) counts $46 frames down and `bchg #3,8(a0)` flips it, and actor_toggle_side_flag flips it again on whatever ground the map step reports. THE LEFT ARM'S STEP IS `move.b #$2,d7` AND THE RIGHT ARM'S IS `move.w #$2,d7`: the byte form replaces only d7's low BYTE, and what is above it is whatever actor_fall_and_settle left there two instructions earlier -- $1400's counted-down span on the ordinary path (below $10, so the two arms agree) but the FOLLOWED RECORD'S SPRITE ID on either of $1334's early exits, since $23b6's `moveq #0,d7` and $5c6e's `move.w 6(a1),d7` are what fill the register on the way in. A record whose bit 0 of 8(a0) is up therefore steps LEFT by (sprite & $ff00) | 2 -- 258 pixels for a player sprite of $1xx -- and RIGHT by 2. Reproduced, not tidied; map.h carries the derivation. Its death arm reads the followed record a THIRD way, `movea.l $a098.l,a1 / adda.l #$180,a1` (the published table pointer plus 12 * 32), and retreats 4 pixels from it into one of four eight-word lists -- a separate pair for a record already marked defeated, which takes no step.
var 0x2736 actor_type03_walk_left_frames
cmt 0x2736 8 words, sprites $ad/$ae. `lea $2736.l,a1` at $2640, on the arm that stepped with $10a2.
var 0x2746 actor_type03_walk_right_frames
cmt 0x2746 8 words, sprites $b1/$b2. `lea $2746.l,a1` at $2656.
var 0x2756 actor_type03_dead_right_frames
cmt 0x2756 8 words, sprite $af throughout. The arm that is still retreating, facing right.
var 0x2766 actor_type03_dead_left_frames
cmt 0x2766 8 words, sprite $b3 throughout.
var 0x2776 actor_type03_held_left_frames
cmt 0x2776 8 words, sprite $b4: the list a record whose bit 3 of 9(a0) is already up plays, which is the one that takes no step.
var 0x2786 actor_type03_held_right_frames
cmt 0x2786 8 words, sprite $b0, the mirror of actor_type03_held_left_frames.
fn 0x2796 actor_behavior_type04
cmt 0x2796 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 30). Code $2796..$28eb (342 bytes), then 256 bytes of word data to $29eb. actor_behavior_table slot 4. THE HOVERER, and the one handler in the band that never touches the collision map while alive: it calls actor_set_side_flag and actor_followed_x_within with $c8, and only from inside that reach does it step ONE pixel toward the followed record -- and not even that when the two x words are equal (`cmp.w (a1),d0 / beq`, which skips the probe but not the animation). Whether it chased or not, every frame ends in the hover at $285e: 30(a0) indexes actor_type04_hover_deltas, a 64-word table of SIGNED words added straight to 2(a0), wrapped `andi.b #$7f`. `move.l #$0,d0` at $2840 is six bytes for the zero every other cursor here gets from a `moveq` -- the deliberate-waste class $7366 records. Its death arm is slot 2's shape with a 4-pixel recoil and no settle at all: a dead hoverer neither falls nor lands.
var 0x28ec actor_type04_dead_right_frames
cmt 0x28ec 16 words of sprite $8. `lea $28ec(pc,d0.w),a1` at $28c2.
var 0x290c actor_type04_dead_left_frames
cmt 0x290c 16 words of sprite $b. `lea $290c(pc,d0.w),a1` at $28a2.
var 0x292c actor_type04_fly_left_frames
cmt 0x292c 16 words: sprites $6 and $7, eight each. `lea $292c.l,a1` at $280c and $2820.
var 0x294c actor_type04_fly_right_frames
cmt 0x294c 16 words: sprites $9 and $a. `lea $294c.l,a1` at $2816 and $2832.
var 0x296c actor_type04_hover_deltas
cmt 0x296c 64 SIGNED words, $296c..$29eb, and the only table in these five handlers that is not a sprite list: $fffd $fffe $ffff $0000 $0001 $0002 $0003 and back down, in runs of two to four, added to 2(a0) one a frame. 30(a0) is its cursor -- the byte $2f86 and $6872 use as a countdown elsewhere -- wrapped over the WHOLE table by `andi.b #$7f`. CORRECTION (batch 37): the count this plate used to give was one short. There are TWO, and the second is in another handler -- `lea $296c.l,a1` at $2864 (slot 4) and `lea $296c.w,a1` at $4746 (slot 23, the SHORT absolute encoding). A census run in one encoding only is what batch 34's $5160 miss was; test_slot_4s_hover_table_has_TWO_operand_sites_and_the_second_is_SLOT_23s in recreate/test/test_behavior.py re-runs it over all four forms. Slot 23 also BRANCHES into slot 4's own publish-and-hover tail at $2840 (`bra.w` at $46fe), so these instructions are not slot 4's alone either.
fn 0x29ec actor_behavior_type05
cmt 0x29ec RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 30). Code $29ec..$2af1 (262 bytes), bounded by actor_start_motion_at_speed's own entry; its frame words sit ABOVE that routine, at $2b0a..$2b59. actor_behavior_table slot 5. THE HOPPER: actor_fall_and_settle, actor_hop_ascend_step, a ONE-pixel map step, and then actor_hop_or_flip_side over the step's two results -- which makes this and slot 3 the first handlers read that consume the map probes' SECOND register (d1, the ground flags) at all. A step-up hop launches at speed 4; a two-cell drop turns it round. Its death arm is the only one in the band with ONE frame list for both facings ($2b4a) and it commits the cursor BEFORE the branch, so the store happens even on the frame it transfers to actor_defeat_and_score -- and it reads bit 3 of 9(a0) with `btst`, not `bclr`, so the defeated bit survives the transfer. Until the animation wraps, a record not yet marked defeated recoils 4 pixels away.

fn 0x2bc8 actor_behavior_type06
cmt 0x2bc8 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 30). Code $2bc8..$2db1 (490 bytes), then 96 bytes of frame words to $2e11. actor_behavior_table slot 6, reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`. THE THROWER, and the only handler in this batch that SPAWNS. Every $46 frames it looks for the followed record inside $c8 (actor_followed_x_within), faces it and launches itself at speed 6 through actor_start_motion_at_speed -- which CLEARS bit 2 of 8(a0) (`bclr #2,8(a0)`), the bit $1400's LANDING arm raises. THE THROW IS THEREFORE ON THE LANDING, not on the launch: while the bit is down the record is in the air and $2c68 publishes one of two standing frames ($66/$6b) and ends the frame, and the frame `btst #2,8(a0)` finally passes it takes a record from actor_alloc_slot_high and builds a shot in it: `move.l (a0),(a1)` copies x and y in ONE operand, y goes up 6, x goes $a ahead or $fff6 behind by the side bit, the type word becomes $28, the flag byte is COPIED from the thrower, `move.l #$c0002,14(a1)` sets half-width $c and vertical extent 2, and 30/18 are cleared with the supported bit. AND IT SAVES ITS OWN FLAG BYTE ACROSS ALL OF THAT: `move.b 8(a0),29(a0)` at $2c3a and `move.b 29(a0),8(a0)` at $2cd0, so actor_set_side_flag's and actor_start_motion_at_speed's writes to 8(a0) are UNDONE -- except on the AIRBORNE arm at $2c68, which returns before reaching the restore, and on frames the save did not run, where the restore writes back a byte saved on an earlier frame. Its walk carries the same `move.b #$2,d7` / `move.w #$2,d7` asymmetry slot 3 has (see $25c0), and it re-reads the side flag AFTER actor_toggle_side_flag to pick its frame list, where slot 3 picks before. Death arm at $2d42: 4-pixel recoil into eight-word lists, `btst #3,9(a0)` (not `bclr`) and `bne.w $6bb8` at $2dac.
