fn 0x2e12 actor_behavior_type09
cmt 0x2e12 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, differential in recreate/test/test_behavior.py, batch 35). actor_behavior_table slot 9, 152 bytes of CODE ($2e12..$2ea9) and NOT the 552 to slot 10's entry: $2eaa/$2eb2 are its two frame-list PAIRS, $2eba..$2f21 the four lists, and $2f22..$3039 above them is SIX SHARED LEAVES ($2f22, $2f46, $2f86, $2fce, $2fe8, $3006) that belong to no slot -- five of them already ported before this batch, $2f46 the only new one. Reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)` -- verified by a whole-image scan of every branch and absolute-jump form, which finds NOTHING aimed here but the table longword at $95c. THE SHORTEST BODY IN THE FAMILY: every arm is a chain of calls. Live frame = actor_fall_and_settle, actor_hop_ascend_step, `move.w #$3,d7` (a WORD write, so the settle's leftover d7 cannot reach the step) into actor_step_facing, then actor_random_facing_hop and actor_anim_step_facing_list on $2eaa. HURT frame ($2e7a, on bit 0 of 9(a0)) = the settle, `bsr $d78`, actor_face_and_step_away4, the $2eb2 lists, then `tst.b 18(a0)` -- the cursor RE-READ out of memory after $3006 stored it -- and on zero `bclr #0,9(a0) / btst #3,9(a0) / bne.w $6bb8`: the DEFEATED bit is only TESTED here, where slots 2, 3 and 4 clear it. That arm CALLS player_gate_on_1516, whose clear-tile_33_mode edge ($e06) BOUNDED the port through batch 39 and is a call into player_jump_step since batch 40 -- so the frame runs whole, with the PLAYER's jump machine applied to this monster's own record. Slot 53 met the same edge and slot 12 meets it again. Its struck arm faces the followed record (`bsr $67c2`) between the two writes and the tail jump, as slots 10 and 12 do and 11 and 13 do not.
fn 0x303a actor_behavior_type10
cmt 0x303a RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 35). actor_behavior_table slot 10, 350 bytes of CODE ($303a..$3197), then four eight-word frame lists ($3198/$31a8/$31b8/$31c8) and the 32-word hover table at $31d8. Reached ONLY through the dispatcher's `jmp (a1)` (whole-image control-flow scan). THE ONE HANDLER IN THE FAMILY THAT NEVER TOUCHES THE COLLISION MAP WHILE ALIVE, and the only caller of actor_step_toward_followed ($6840) in the image. Then one pixel of x toward the side flag (`addq.w #1,(a0)` / `subq.w #1,(a0)`, no probe at all), then 30(a0) counted down and, on zero, `bchg #3,8(a0)`, a reload of $64 and ONE homing step of 1 pixel on both axes through $6840. fn 0x3218 actor_behavior_type11

Reached ONLY through the dispatcher's `jmp (a1)` (whole-image control-flow scan). It WALKS while 30(a0) runs down and DECIDES when it expires: the countdown-zero arm reloads $19, and then -- only for a record with bit 2 of 8(a0) SET -- draws one rng_next word, faces LEFT on `btst #2` SET (the OPPOSITE reading to $2f46's) and, unless `btst #1` is set too, tail-jumps into actor_start_motion_at_speed with d0 = 9. var 0x337c actor_type11_hurt_marked_frames
cmt 0x337c 8 words, sprites $b8 x4 $b9 x4, played while bit 3 of 30(a0) is SET. One operand site, `lea $337c.l,a1` at $3328 -- the 31 other occurrences of the word $337c in the image are data, none of them preceded by an abs.w-mode opcode (batch 35's scan). THE SIXTEEN BYTES ABOVE IT, $338c..$339b, REPEAT IT EXACTLY, AND THEY ARE REACHABLE PADDING RATHER THAN DEAD BYTES. `move.b 18(a0),d0 / lea 0(a1,d0.w),a1` indexes with the RAW record byte and `andi.b #$f,d0` runs AFTERWARDS, on the value stored BACK -- so the mask bounds where the cursor GOES, never where it came from, and a cursor of $10 publishes $338c. That word equals $337c's, which is what the duplicate is FOR: the over-read is harmless here. It is not harmless everywhere -- see actor_type13_frames, which has no padding above it. An earlier revision of this plate said the mask meant "nothing can reach them", which is the inverse of what the bytes do.

fn 0x35d8 actor_behavior_type14
cmt 0x35d8 actor_behavior_table slot 14, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, differential in recreate/test/test_behavior.py, batch 36). 316 bytes, decoded code runs $35d8..$3714 -- the difference to slot 15's entry is 396, and the other 80 bytes are this handler's three frame tables. Reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`: no instruction anywhere in the image aims at $35d8 (whole-image scan of every branch, absolute jump/call and both `lea` PC forms) and the only longword holding it is actor_behavior_table's own slot. THE PATROLLER THAT DROPS ESCORTS. Live frame: settle, ascend, then 30(a0) down -- on ZERO it `bchg #3,8(a0)`, reloads $46 and ENDS THE FRAME (no step, no animation). Otherwise 31(a0) down, and on ZERO it allocates a record through $1b8e, fills it from `move.l (a0),(a1)` with type $2d, 14(a1) := $60006, 30(a1) := $32 and both cursors cleared, sets its own 31(a0) := $1e and ENDS THE FRAME too -- `move.b #$1e,31(a0)` sits BELOW the failed-allocation branch, so a full pool leaves the gap at zero and it retries next frame. The walk is one pixel, `move.b #$1,d7` on the LEFT arm and `move.w` on the right, then `bsr $2b82`; the frame list is chosen AFTER that (slot 6's order, not slot 3's). Hurt arm: ONE table for both facings, no settle and no step at all, ending `bclr #0,9(a0) / btst #3,9(a0) / bne.w $6bb8` at $370e.
fn 0x3764 actor_behavior_type15
cmt 0x3764 actor_behavior_table slot 15, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 36). 234 bytes, decoded code runs $3764..$384e; the 96 bytes above it are its four frame tables. Reached ONLY through the dispatcher's `jmp (a1)`, measured as for slot 14. THE WALKER THAT TURNS AND HOPS: four pixels toward the side flag (`move.w #$4,d7` in BOTH arms, so nothing the settle left in d7 reaches the step) and then `bsr $2b8e` -- actor_turn_and_launch, whose only two callers in the image are this handler's two arms at $37c8 and $37de. A blocked step or a one-cell drop under a SUPPORTED record flips the facing AND relaunches it. THE `lea` SITS BETWEEN THE PROBE AND THE `bsr`, so the frame published on a turning frame is the OLD side's -- the opposite order to slot 14's. Its two arms also step the cursor differently: the walk is `addq.b #2,18(a0) / andi.b #$f,18(a0)` IN MEMORY, the hurt arm computes in d0 under a $1f mask. AND ITS HURT TAIL IS THE OTHER ORDER: `btst #3,9(a0) / bne.w $6bb8` at $3842 comes FIRST and `bclr #0,9(a0)` only runs when the mark is down, so a record that transfers keeps BOTH marks (slots 14, 17 and 18 lower bit 0 first).
fn 0x38ae actor_behavior_type16
cmt 0x38ae actor_behavior_table slot 16, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 36). 312 bytes, decoded code runs $38ae..$39e6; the 96 above are its four frame tables. Reached ONLY through the dispatcher's `jmp (a1)`, measured. THE HOPPER THAT LOBS: settle, ascend, `bsr $67c2` and then the frame list -- so the facing is set BEFORE the list is chosen and the frame published always follows the followed record. The cursor is stepped IN MEMORY under $f. Then 30(a0) down, and on ZERO `bclr #2,8(a0)` -- THE TEST AND THE WRITE: the branch reads the bit as it WAS, so an airborne record leaves having STORED its flag byte (unchanged in value) and done nothing else, and a supported one has already lost the bit when bits 0 and 1 go up, 11(a0) := $9 and 30(a0) := $32. It then allocates a type-$27 record carrying the flag byte the launch JUST wrote, 11(a1) := $9, 14(a1) := $60006 and both cursors cleared. Hurt tail is slot 15's order: `btst #3,9(a0) / bne.w $6bb8` at $39da above the `bclr`.
fn 0x3a46 actor_behavior_type17
cmt 0x3a46 actor_behavior_table slot 17, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 36). 290 bytes, decoded code runs $3a46..$3b68; the 284 above are two $3006 list PAIRS, their four lists, the two GLOBAL cursors at $3bc0/$3bc2 and 192 bytes of drift table. It faces the followed record, animates through $3006, and then steps BOTH axes out of actor_type17_dx / _dy -- indexed by two words that live in the IMAGE, so two live type-17 records drift in lockstep. fn 0x4e38 actor_behavior_type28
cmt 0x4e38 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 33). 144 bytes, $4e38..$4ec7, bounded by slot 29's bare `rts`. A COLLECTABLE, not a creature: no spawn gate, and the only contact test is `bsr $5c6e / btst #1,d0` -- the FOOTPRINT bit alone, so the followed record collects it by standing on it and a shot cannot touch it. The collect arm is gated a SECOND time on bit 0 of 8(a0) being DOWN, so a record mid-hop is not collectable; it fires sound_request_9, writes actor_free_marker over its own x, adds 5 to bcd_counter_bd6e and $20 to bcd_score_bd70. THE TWO ACCUMULATORS RUN BACK TO BACK ($4e5a then $4e64, only a `move.l #$20,d0` between, which does not touch X), so the score's first `abcd` folds in the X the counter's last one left -- CARRIED as of batch 33 and pinned by a case seeding the counter within 5 of $9999 (recreate/test/test_behavior.py); the port that folded a zero there was off by one in the score's low byte. The waiting arm is actor_fall_and_settle, actor_hop_ascend_step, actor_relaunch_and_anim_5160 and a step of 31(a0) pixels (`moveq #0,d7 / move.b 31(a0),d7`, so the register is CLEAN and carries nothing of the settle's -- slot 52's spelling one field over), skipped entirely while that byte is zero and followed by `subq.b #1,31(a0)`. ITS TURN TEST READS THE WHOLE LOW WORD where every other blocked-step test is `tst.b` -- THREE dispatch rows spell it (this one, and slots 20 and 27 through the body they share, read batch 37): the probes leave a map column (or a clamp limit, or a parked x) in the byte above the outcome, so the `bchg #3,8(a0)` fires only when the WHOLE low word is zero -- a step blocked at column $10 reports $1000 and does not turn the record round. Then `subq.b #1,12(a0)` -- the BYTE spelling of the word countdown slots 30 and 31 keep at the same offset -- and on zero `bset #6,8(a0) / bne` reads the bit it just overwrote, so the FIRST expiry raises the flicker and reloads 12(a0) with $14 while the SECOND frees the slot. actor_behavior_table slot 28; reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`, never by a call.
fn 0x4ec8 actor_behavior_type29
cmt 0x4ec8 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 33). TWO BYTES, $4ec8..$4ec9: a bare `rts`, the same two the table's slots 0 and 58 hold at $a36 -- but at an address of ITS OWN, so the table has 61 distinct targets and three do nothing. src/behavior.c maps this row to actor_behavior_null's body rather than adding a second empty function. actor_behavior_table slot 29; reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`, never by a call, and bounded by slot 30's entry at $4eca.
fn 0x4eca actor_behavior_type30
cmt 0x4eca RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 33). 142 bytes, $4eca..$4f57, then a $0000 pad at $4f58, its global cursor at $4f5a and its drift table at $4f5c. A HOVERING COLLECTABLE. Contact is `bsr $5c6e / btst #1,d0` as slot 28's, gated a second time on `cmpi.b #$a,30(a0) / blt` -- a SIGNED byte compare against actor_type30_collect_min, and 30(a0) counts UP one per waiting frame, so a freshly spawned record cannot be collected for ten frames (and a byte that has counted past $7f is refused again until it wraps). The collect arm fires sound_request_9 and then TOPS UP THE METER WRONG: `move.w $b6fa,d0 / addq.w #4,d0 / cmp.w $b6f8,d0 / blt` computes the raised value and stores it NOWHERE -- the only arm that writes is the one where the sum REACHED hud_meter_max, and it writes the maximum rather than the sum. So the pickup refills the meter only when the player was already within 4 of full and is otherwise worth nothing at all. That is a shipped bug ($b6fe, hud_meter_add_clamped, is the routine that does this properly and this handler does not call it) and the reconstruction reproduces it. The waiting arm is `addq.b #1,30(a0)`, a one-pixel rise on the frames `tst.b $712.w` finds nonzero (a BYTE test on the HIGH half of frame_toggle's word), and a signed word of actor_type30_drift added straight to (a0) off a GLOBAL cursor -- so two live type-30 records share one drift phase. Then `cmpi.w #$14,12(a0)` raises the flicker bit and `subq.w #1,12(a0) / bne` runs the record out. Both arms end at $4f46: `bclr #6,8(a0)`, the cursor cleared, and actor_free_marker over the x. actor_behavior_table slot 30; reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`, never by a call.
var 0x4f5a actor_type30_cursor
cmt 0x4f5a Word: actor_behavior_type30's animation cursor, and the only one in the behaviour tier that is a GLOBAL rather than a record field -- so two live type-30 records step actor_type30_drift together and a record spawned mid-cycle joins the phase the other left. It is a BYTE OFFSET, stepped `addq.w #2` and wrapped `andi.w #$3f` (64 bytes = the table's 32 words), and the mask is applied AFTER the store is read, so the fetch is off a SIGN-EXTENDED word and only the store is bounded. Three operand sites, all inside slot 30: the read at $4f14, the write at $4f28 and the `clr.w` at $4f4c. The two bytes at $4f58 between the handler's last `rts` and this word are a $0000 pad nothing references.
var 0x4f5c actor_type30_drift
cmt 0x4f5c THIRTY-TWO signed words, $4f5c..$4f9b, bounded by actor_behavior_type31's entry: $0008 $0007 ... $0001 $0000 $ffff ... $fff8 $fff9 ... $ffff $0000 $0001 ... $0007. A triangle from +8 down to -8 and back to +7 whose 32 steps sum to ZERO, so a type-30 record hovers about its spawn x rather than travelling. Added straight to (a0) one word a frame by `add.w d1,(a0)` at $4f20. ONE reference in the image, the `lea $4f5c(pc,d0.w),a1` at $4f1a -- a PC-relative INDEXED displacement, the operand form PORTABILITY.md 0k is about, which is why nothing found this table before batch 33.
fn 0x4f9c actor_behavior_type31
cmt 0x4f9c RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 33). SEVENTY-EIGHT bytes, $4f9c..$4fe9 -- and NOT the 146 a scan that runs on to the next `rts` gives it: its last branch is `bne.w $4fea` into actor_select_sprite_by_flag, a routine of its own with a second caller at $54d6, whose `rts` returns to the dispatcher. The 48 bytes at $4fea belong to that routine and not to this handler; batch 33's opening scan had them the other way round and this plate is the correction. Structure: actor_fall_and_settle, actor_hop_ascend_step, `cmpi.w #$14,12(a0)` raising the flicker bit, then -- only while bit 0 of 8(a0) is DOWN -- `bsr $5c6e / btst #1,d0` for the collect, which fires sound_request_9 and hud_award_gold_from_descriptor and falls into the free arm. fn 0x5046 actor_behavior_type32
cmt 0x5046 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 34). 278 bytes, $5046..$515b, bounded by its own three globals below. A HOPPING GOLD COLLECTABLE: the same payout slot 31 makes (sound_request_9 then hud_award_gold_from_descriptor, so it inherits the declared hardware pair and the packed-BCD extend chain), with a HOP MACHINE and a WALK in front of it. Per frame: actor_fall_and_settle, actor_hop_ascend_step, then the contact test -- which runs when actor_type32_walking is up OR bit 0 of 8(a0) is DOWN, so only a record that has never landed and is mid-hop is uncollectable. Its waiting arm is gated on actor_type32_hops_spent being clear AND the record being SUPPORTED: it raises actor_type32_walking, runs `subq.b #1,10(a0)` and, on a nonzero result, relaunches with 8(a0) bits 0 and 1 set, bit 2 cleared and 11(a0) RE-READ out of 10(a0) rather than reused from the register; on zero it raises actor_type32_hops_spent instead and launches nothing, so the LAST hop is skipped and the hops shorten by construction. While actor_type32_walking is up it also walks: `move.w #$1,d7` into both probe arms, `tst.b d0` and `bchg #3,8(a0)` -- actor_step_facing's own body inline, the way slots 48 and 49 spell it, and a WORD write of the step so nothing the settle left in d7's high half reaches the probes. Then `cmpi.w #$14,12(a0)` raises the flicker bit and `subq.w #1,12(a0) / bne` runs the record out. Both endings ($5074's collect and $5112's expiry) reach $5116, which is `bclr #6,8(a0)`, `clr.w $515c.l`, a DEAD `clr.b $515d.l` over the byte that word already cleared, and actor_free_marker. actor_behavior_table slot 32; reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`, never by a call.
var 0x515c actor_type32_walking
cmt 0x515c Byte, $515c: slot 32's LANDED latch, renamed from actor_type32_state now the body is read -- `st $515c.l` at $508c raises it on the record's first SUPPORTED frame, and what it gates is the WALK at $50be and the contact test at $504c. `clr.w $515c.l` at $511c clears it AND the byte after it. Two `tst.b` readers, $504c and $50be. A GLOBAL and not a record field, so two live type-32 records share one gate -- actor_type30_cursor's property. RECONSTRUCTED (WB_ACTOR_TYPE32_WALKING, batch 34).
var 0x515d actor_type32_hops_spent
cmt 0x515d Byte, $515d: slot 32's OUT-OF-HOPS latch, renamed from actor_type32_done -- `st $515d.l` at $509a on the frame `subq.b #1,10(a0)` reaches zero, after which no landing relaunches anything. Read by the `tst.b` at $5078 and cleared twice on the way out ($511c's `clr.w $515c.l` reaches this byte and $5122's `clr.b` writes the same zero again). Also a GLOBAL. var 0x515e actor_type32_cursor
cmt 0x515e Word, $515e: slot 32's animation cursor into actor_anim_5160_frames, and the table's SECOND reader. CORRECTION: the earlier plate said this one zeroed the cursor "one word EARLY" against $6872's -- the bytes say the two look ahead by exactly the same word. $6872 publishes with `move.w (a1)+,6(a0)`, a POST-INCREMENT, so its `cmpi.w #$ffff,(a1)` at $68b8 and this handler's `cmpi.w #$ffff,2(a1)` at $5148 read the same address, and neither ever draws the terminator. What DOES differ is the cursor itself: $6872's is the record BYTE 18(a0), zero-extended, and it commits `addq.b #2,18(a0)` to memory BEFORE the test and then overwrites it with `clr.b` on the wrap (two writes to one byte); this one is a GLOBAL WORD, indexed SIGN-EXTENDED by `lea 0(a1,d0.w)`, stepped `addi.w #$2` in the register and stored ONCE after the test. Like actor_type30_cursor it is a global and a byte offset. RECONSTRUCTED (WB_ACTOR_TYPE32_CURSOR, batch 34).
fn 0x5208 actor_behavior_type33
cmt 0x5208 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 34). 82 bytes, $5208..$5259. THE GAME'S CLOCK PICKUP, and the only collectable in the band that pays neither gold nor the meter: on `bsr $5c6e / btst #1,d0` it fires sound_request_9, raises panel_frame_rewind and panel_frame_hold with two `move.w #$ffff` one instruction apart -- which winds panel_frame_delay back up to $500 in steps of $14 a frame and freezes the countdown while it climbs -- and adds $20 to bcd_score_bd70. It is also the ONE collectable here with no `btst #0,8(a0)` gate at all, so a record mid-hop is taken where slots 28, 31 and 32 refuse. The waiting arm is slots 30 and 31's exactly: `cmpi.w #$14,12(a0)` raises the flicker bit and `subq.w #1,12(a0) / bne` runs it out into the shared tail at $524e (`bclr #6,8(a0)` then actor_free_marker), which the collect arm reaches by a `bra.w`. fn 0x525a actor_behavior_type34
cmt 0x525a RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 34). 220 bytes, $525a..$5335. NOT A CREATURE: this record is THE SHOP'S ITEM CURSOR, and its own x word -- (a0), WB_ACTOR_X -- is the selection. It returns having written nothing while scene_message_pending or scene_ack_wait is nonzero, so a held direction cannot walk the cursor under an open box; otherwise `bsr $682` (joy1_newly_pressed) and three `btst`s in order, bits 2 (LEFT), 3 (RIGHT) and 7 (FIRE), each arm ending in its own `rts`. LEFT walks $be -> $78 -> $33 and RIGHT walks $33 -> $78 -> $be; arriving on either END posts that item's message id out of shop_record_ptr (66(a1) for $be, 68(a1) for $33) with text_lifetime_request $32, and arriving on the MIDDLE writes text_request $ff -- the dismiss -- with no lifetime beside it. Each destination is planted as ONE `move.l #imm,(a0)` over x AND y together: ($33,$40), ($78,$30), ($be,$40), so the middle sits sixteen pixels above the ends. FIRE writes shop_request 1 at $33, 2 at $be and 3 at $78, so the request runs 1, 3, 2 across the screen -- the middle is the FAREWELL. actor_behavior_table slot 34; reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`, never by a call.
fn 0x5336 actor_behavior_type35

THIRTY-EIGHT bytes, $5336..$535b -- CORRECTION: the earlier plate said the code runs to $535c, which is the first byte of its own CURSOR and not code, and a scan that ran to the next `rts` gave this row $5336..$53bb and swallowed both the data below and scene_copy_record_fields whole. `lea $535c.l,a1 / move.w (a1)+,d0` reads actor_event_anim_cursor and leaves a1 on actor_event_anim_frames; one word is published into 6(a0), the cursor is stepped `addq.w #2` and masked `andi.w #$1f`, and on the wrap to zero `move.w #$ffff,$b12.w` raises event_anim_done_b12. var 0x535c actor_event_anim_cursor

var 0x537e actor_type35_template
cmt 0x537e THIRTY-TWO bytes, $537e..$539d: the record template player_pending_event_gate hands scene_copy_record_fields (`lea $537e.l,a1 / bsr $539e` at $c58). It is the only shipped datum in the image that names a behaviour slot by number.
fn 0x53bc actor_behavior_type36
cmt 0x53bc RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 34). 38 bytes, $53bc..$53e1. Slot 35's six instructions over the SAME cursor and the SAME table, with two differences and both are in the wrap arm: the flag is event_anim_done_b16 rather than $b12, and `clr.w 4(a0)` RETYPES THE RECORD to slot 0 -- the bare `rts` -- so the row stops without giving its slot back and leaves the last sprite it published standing. (Slot 60 is the tier's other self-retyper and moves the other way, into slot 54.) It also addresses the cursor with the SHORT absolute form (`lea $535c.w` / `move.w d0,$535c.w`) where slot 35 uses the long one. actor_behavior_table slot 36; reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`, never by a call.
fn 0x53e2 actor_behavior_type37
cmt 0x53e2 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 34). 38 bytes, $53e2..$5407, the band's last row. Slot 36's ALTERNATIVE -- player_pending_event_gate picks between the two at $cd8 on 6(record_ptr_10420) and they raise the same flag -- and it has no animation, no table and no cursor. `movea.l $10420.l,a1 / move.w 4(a1),d0 / subi.w #$20,d0 / cmp.w 2(a0),d0` is the whole of it: while the record's y is not EXACTLY 32 pixels above the scene descriptor's own field 4 (the word it was spawned at, and the same word scene_run_frame reads as its variant selector -- one field, three readings) it lifts one pixel with `subq.w #1,2(a0)`; on the frame they are equal it raises event_anim_done_b16 instead. The test is an equality and not a `ble`, so a record seeded BELOW its target counts down through the whole 16-bit range before arriving. $5406 is this routine's OWN `rts`, reached by the `bra.w` at $53fc and by falling out of the flag write; nothing else in the image branches to it. actor_behavior_table slot 37; reached ONLY through actor_dispatch_behavior's `jmp (a1)`, never by a call.
fn 0x5408 actor_behavior_type38_pickup

cmt 0x6f7e RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 31). 30 bytes, $6f7e..$6f9b, bounded by state_word_6f9c itself. actor_behavior_table slot 60, and it has no movement, no contact test and no map: `move.w #$ffff,6(a0)` publishes NO SPRITE every frame, so a waiting record is invisible, and then `tst.w $6f9c.l` watches one word. On the frame it finds it nonzero it `clr.w`s it -- it is the only thing in the image that lowers it, set_state_6f9c_ffff ($10232) being the only thing that raises it -- and writes `move.w #$36,4(a0)`. THE $36 IS A BEHAVIOUR SLOT, and 4(a0) is WB_ACTOR_TYPE: the record RETYPES ITSELF into slot 54, the vertical moving platform, so its next dispatch runs actor_behavior_type54. That closes the open question the $6f9c plate left. Extent corrected: the earlier plate ran the body to $6f9c, which is the state word.

var 0x7016 actor_type61_messages
cmt 0x7016 FIVE bytes, $7016..$701a ($701b is padding before actor_face_followed_reset_22): $72 $73 $74 $75 $ff -- the four HIGHEST text_request ids in the game and their terminator, which is what says what this sequence is for given its copylock caller. Entry 0 is never READ: the cursor is pre-incremented, so the first press posts entry 1 and the $72 comes from the `move.b #$72,d0` immediate instead. ONE reference, the `lea $7016.l,a1` at $6ff0.

fn 0x7044 actor_behavior_type59

fn 0x7060 actor_behavior_type07
cmt 0x7060 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 32). 424 bytes, $7060..$7207 -- CORRECTION: the earlier plate said the code runs to $7208, which is the first byte of actor_type07_burst_left, not code. actor_behavior_table slot 7, and THE ONLY HANDLER IN THE TABLE WITH THREE ENTRANCES: its own row sets neither mark bit, slot 59 ($7044) reaches it by `bra.w $7060` after `bset #2,30(a0)`, and slot 8 ($705a) simply RUNS INTO it after `bset #1,30(a0)`. Bit 1 and bit 2 of 30(a0) are how the one body knows which row fired -- bit 1 arms the five-shot burst, bit 2 the constant sprite pair AND the dropper -- and slot 59 also leaves a1 = $21e6a, which nothing here reads. THE BODY: (1) the monster prologue `btst #2,9(a0) / bne.w actor_spawn_anim_step`; (2) `bsr $23b6` and, only if that reports no hit, `bsr $5c6e` -- and it tests bits 1 and 2 of the mask and NOT bit 0, so the STRIKE bit every $5a-band slot reads is IGNORED here; bit 1 tail-jumps `bsr.s $701c / bra.w $69fe` and is this handler's only tail; (3) the damage arm at $708e is NOT an ending -- `bset #0,9(a0)` is call-scoped around actor_damage_template_hitpoints and `bclr` lowers it again, and a record that survives the `btst #3,9(a0) / bne.w $6bb8` FALLS THROUGH into the rest of the frame; (4) $70ae publishes either two constants ($21 while bit 3 of 8(a0) is SET, $24 while it is clear) for a bit-2 record or one of FOUR twelve-word lists over the 23(a0) cursor; (5) `jsr` through actor_swoop_state_table on 22(a0), which NOTHING BOUNDS; (6) the two spawners on the 31(a0) cadence. THE FRAME-LIST CHOICE IS TWO OVERLAPPING WRITES OF a1, not a four-way test: $74a0 goes in, bit 1 of 30(a0) replaces it with $74ee, and then bit 3 of 8(a0) decides whether either stands (SET keeps them; clear restarts from $74ba and lets the mark bit replace that with $7508). THE BURST'S `bsr $1b8e` IS INSIDE THE `dbf`, so five separate records are taken and a mid-loop failure `beq.w $7206` leaves the WHOLE routine, skipping the dropper; the DROPPER allocates BEFORE it tests its cadence, so a full pool leaves 31(a0) unadvanced.
fn 0x7260 actor_behavior_type57
cmt 0x7260 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 39). 98 bytes, $7260..$72c1, bounded by actor_swoop_state0_acquire's entry -- CORRECTION: the earlier plate said the code runs to $73ce, which swallows all four SWOOP states ($72c2..$73cd, reconstructed in batch 32) and is 268 bytes of another machine. actor_behavior_table slot 57, reached ONLY through the `jmp (a1)` -- and it is behaviour slot 7's BURST SHOT (WB_ACTOR_TYPE07_SHOT_TYPE is $39 == 57), so the velocity it flies on is the actor_type07_burst_left/right LONGWORD that burst copies into 24(a1). THE WORD-PAIR SHOT: `move.w 24(a0),d0 / add.w d0,(a0) / move.w 26(a0),d0 / add.w d0,2(a0)` -- BOTH ADDED, where slot 44 subtracts its y -- then `addq.w #1,28(a0)` and `cmpi.w #$28,28(a0)`, an EQUALITY test that RE-READS the word the step just wrote, so a counter seeded past the limit runs the whole 16-bit way round. Its bit-1 contact arm is `bne.w $69fe`, a TAIL JUMP that latches nothing, so it can damage the followed record on every frame it overlaps. Its death arm (`btst #0,9(a0)`) clears EIGHT bytes with two `clr.l`s over 22(a0) and 26(a0), i.e. 22..29 -- which is NOT "the swoop's block", as an earlier revision of this plate said: that machine's state is 22 (the state byte), 24 (the path cursor) and 26 (the launch y), ending at offset 27, so the second `clr.l` reaches two bytes PAST it into 28(a0), which is this handler's own frame count and which nothing else in the tier reads. Then it writes actor_free_marker and lowers the bit.

fn 0xb1a player_pending_event_gate
cmt 0xb1a Second call of actor_behavior_type01_player, and the one that decides whether the rest of the player frame runs: three word flags in order -- $b08, $b0e, $b14 -- each with its own arm ($b36, $c28, $c76); none set clears d7 and returns. The caller's `tst.w d7 / bmi.w $a74` means a NEGATIVE d7 from here skips movement, collision, scene and stage work for the frame. BATCH 40 PHASE B READ THE BODY and the `# ctx` tag comes off: the name is what the body does. STILL UNPORTABLE, and the reasons are now measured rather than assumed -- `bsr.w $1f54` at $bb0 is on the arm nearly every path converges to, `bsr.w $19ac` at $c66 is on another, `jsr $fe8c.l` at $c00 is a third callee, and THREE exits leave through a STACK UNWIND rather than returning to the caller -- corrected from TWO by batch 40 phase C, which found the third. Two are direct: `lea 4(a7),a7 / jmp` at $bdc (into $e494) and at $c20 (into $e5ba), each popping one return address. The THIRD is INDIRECT and is why it was missed: `bra.w $1622` at $d16 branches into player_collide_and_scroll's own `lea 12(a7),a7 / jmp $e5ba.l`, so the pop happens in another routine's body and a census of this routine's own instructions does not see it -- and that one discards THREE return addresses. The $c20 arm is also where the gate forces effect_state_21e4 (the player's FORM) back to 1, one instruction after `jsr $fe8c.l`. THE ONE PIECE OF IT THIS PORT HAS is the spawn at $c52..$c61: `lea $998c.l,a2 / lea $537e.l,a1 / bsr.w $539e`, i.e. a2 is the DESTINATION (slot 1 of actor_table_default, which the `cmpi.w #$ffbe,$998c.l` at $c36 has just checked is free) and a1 the TEMPLATE (actor_type35_template) -- in that order, with a3 clobbered by the callee. A porter who reads the pair the other way round gets a routine that copies the record over the template; recreate/test/test_player.py pins the composition by taking both operands out of THESE bytes.

fn 0xe06 player_jump_step
cmt 0xe06 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/player.c, differential in recreate/test/test_player.py, batch 40). 194 bytes, $e06..$ec7, bounded by player_step_and_arm's entry. THE JUMP MACHINE, and the body player_gate_on_1516 branches into while tile_33_mode is clear -- the ONLY way in: a whole-image census over every branch, absolute and PC-relative form finds exactly one instruction naming this address, the `beq.w` at $d7e. Every frame first clears tile_33_step_flag and stamps 10(a0) with `$bd6a + 8` (an `addi.b`, so nothing carries into the state word's high half), then takes one of three exclusive arms on 8(a0), tested in this order. Bit 0 set -- ASCENDING: the y rises by the ZERO-EXTENDED byte 11(a0) and the byte is spent one per frame; the `bne` reads what `subq.b` left, so a speed of 0 wraps to $ff and the climb runs 255 more frames, and the frame it really reaches zero lowers bit 0 and reloads 11(a0) with 1. Bit 2 set -- STANDING: `bsr $682 / btst #0,d0` is a RISING edge of joystick UP, and on it SFX 0 through $17adc+56, bits 0 and 1 raised, bit 2 lowered and 11(a0) loaded from the 10(a0) this same frame wrote. Neither -- AIRBORNE, and this is the WING BOOTS: while hud_slot_bbc2 has a charge and UP is HELD ($8cf, the level and not the edge) the fall speed is forced back to 1 and one charge is spent per frame, and the frame the last one goes the slot is rearmed $00ff and message $13 -- "You lost wing boots." -- is posted for 50 frames. That message is what names the slot.
fn 0xd84 player_apply_joystick
cmt 0xd84 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/player.c, differential in recreate/test/test_player.py, batch 40). 130 bytes, $d84..$e05 -- NOT on to $e06, which is player_jump_step's. THE LADDER, called by actor_behavior_type01_player's `bsr.w` at $a60 (the frame's SEVENTH of nine, counting the shared actor_fall_and_settle -- this plate names the call SITE because the ordinal is a number two documents can disagree about, and did). It does nothing at all unless tile_33_flag is up, i.e. unless actor_fall_and_settle's player-only head found tile $33 under the record this frame. Up ($8cf bit 0) and down (bit 1) are ONE body with three operands changed: the facing bits 0 and 1 of 8(a0) are lowered, tile_33_step_flag is raised $ffff, the x is snapped `andi.w #$fff1,(a0) / addq.w #8,(a0)` -- a mask that KEEPS bit 0, so an odd x stays odd -- and the y moves two pixels and is masked even. What differs is the direction of that move and the word written to tile_33_mode: $00ff going up and $ffff going down, two different nonzero values for a flag every one of its five readers tests with a bare `tst.w`. On the frame neither direction is held, and on every frame the flag is down, the whole effect is `clr.w $1518`. ONE caller, the `bsr.w` at $a60.
fn 0xec8 player_step_and_arm
cmt 0xec8 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/player.c, differential in recreate/test/test_player.py, batch 40 phase B). 436 bytes, $ec8..$107b, bounded by player_reset_ground_state's entry. THE WALK, and the FOURTH call of actor_behavior_type01_player (`bsr.w` at $a4a, its one caller by a whole-image census). FIVE SECTIONS in a row, each falling into the next and none of them returning: (1) THE KNOCK-BACK -- while 29(a0) (the step count actor_stun_followed seeds) is nonzero the record takes one map step AWAY from bit 3 of 8(a0) (set = step RIGHT) by the count as it was, and one is spent from memory; (2) THE FIRE EDGE -- `bsr $682 / tst.b d0 / bpl` is a SIGN test of bit 7 alone, so FIRE together with a direction still arms, and on it bit 7 of 8(a0) goes up, bit 0 of 9(a0) down and 22(a0) to zero; (3) THE FLICKER COUNTDOWN -- `subq.b #1,21(a0)` here is that byte's ONE reader in the image, and the frame it reaches zero lowers bit 6 of 8(a0) AND bit 4 of 9(a0), i.e. ends the invulnerability with the flicker; (4) THE HURT DRIFT, gated on bit 0 of 9(a0) -- a record that has picked bit 2 up (landed) lowers the gate instead, otherwise it is pushed 31(a0) pixels the way 30(a0) names (both written by actor_damage_followed) and TWO are spent, the step being the count BEFORE the spend so a zero count still takes a probe of zero pixels; (5) THE WALK ACCELERATOR. 22(a0) is the speed, 23(a0) the direction it is travelling (zero LEFT, $ff RIGHT) and 24(a0) a counter `addq.b #1 / andi.b #$3 / bne` lets the speed rise on one frame in four, up to `$bd6a + 4` -- a WORD add whose LOW BYTE `cmp.b 22(a0),d0` then compares SIGNED, where player_jump_step's `addi.b #$8` on the same word is a byte add, so a state word of $00fc clamps the walk to a standstill and leaves the jump at 4. RIGHT ($8cf bit 3) is tested before LEFT (bit 2) and holding both walks right; each arm first leaves a ladder (`tst.w $1516` then `bsr $107c`, which is that routine's ONLY pair of call sites) and raises bit 5 of 8(a0), which the frame neither direction is held lowers. HOLDING THE DIRECTION IT IS NOT TRAVELLING SHEDS SPEED INSTEAD, AND THE TWO RATES ARE NOT EQUAL: `subq.b #2,22(a0)` at $1002 turning to face right and `subq.b #1,22(a0)` at $106a turning to face left, each stepping the OLD way while the byte stays non-negative and flipping 23(a0) on the frame it does not. Both tails RE-READ 22(a0) and the right one's `beq.s` aims BACKWARD at the left one's `rts` at $1000.
fn 0x107c player_reset_ground_state
cmt 0x107c RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/player.c, differential in recreate/test/test_player.py, batch 40). 38 bytes, $107c..$10a1 -- the earlier plate's "$107c..$10a2" named actor_step_left_against_map's first byte. LEAVING THE LADDER: tile_33_mode cleared, bit 2 of 8(a0) cleared and bits 0 and 1 set (the record put back into a fall), and 11(a0) loaded with `$bd6a + 8` -- the same jump-strength byte player_jump_step stamps into 10(a0), spelt `addq.b #8,d0` where $e12 spells `addi.b #$8,d0`. fn 0x1208 player_weapon_fire
cmt 0x1208 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/player.c, differential in recreate/test/test_player.py, batch 40 phase B). 300 bytes, $1208..$1333, bounded by actor_fall_and_settle's entry -- and the LAST TWO are DATA: $1332 holds a packed-BCD 1 and $1333 is unread padding. THE WEAPON, and the FIFTH call of actor_behavior_type01_player (`bsr.w` at $a4e, its one caller). FOUR GATES IN SERIES: `tst.w $1514` (no ladder tile under the player), `cmpi.l #$b444,$b546` (the record list's write pointer off its base, i.e. something IS held), `bsr $682 / cmp.b #$80,d0` -- an EQUALITY over the WHOLE newly-pressed byte, so FIRE and nothing else may go down this frame, which is why DOWN has to be held ALREADY -- and `btst #1,$8cf.w` (DOWN held). Then the newest record's HIGH byte, which is the high half of the WB_PICKUP_RECORD_* word the grant pushed, picks one of four arms: $8 LIGHTNING (`move.w #$2,$714.w` and nothing else -- it spawns no actor and so is the only item that always costs a shot), $7 WIND SPOUTS (a high-pool record of type $30), $6 FIRE BALLS (type $32) and everything else BOMBS (type $31). The wind spout and the bomb share the arming block at $1292 -- the bomb's arm writes its own type and lifetime and `bra.w $1292` INTO it -- which clears the sprite word, copies the player's WHOLE 8(a0) and edits three bits of it, sets 11(a1) to 8, takes the x,y as one `move.l (a0),(a1)` and stamps `move.l #$60008,14(a1)`. The fireball shares nothing below its own `bsr $1b8e`: it copies only bit 3, leaves the shot eight pixels high (`subq.w #8,2(a1)`) and clears 6(a1) as a BYTE where the block clears the word. ALL THREE SPAWN ARMS `rts` ON A FULL POOL, above the spend, so firing into a full table costs nothing. THE SPEND at $1258 is `addq.l #2,a6 / lea $1333.l,a2 / sbcd -(a2),-(a6)`: one packed-BCD unit off the record's LOW byte, `st $b54a.l`, and `tst.b (a6)` -- a RE-READ -- pops the record with `subq.l #2,$b546.l` when the count reaches zero. THE `sbcd` FOLDS THE X FLAG IN AS A SECOND UNIT: on the fireball arm the `subq.w #8,2(a1)` above produces it (a borrow out of the shot's y, and the only arithmetic instruction on that path), so a fireball launched from a y below 8 costs TWO; on the other three arms nothing writes X at all, and the enumeration is COMPLETE because an inherited-X claim can only be closed from the plate: the gates and dispatch are `tst.w`, `cmpi.l`, `cmp.b`, `btst`, `movea.l`, `cmpi.b` and the branches; joy1_newly_pressed ($682), which runs on EVERY path here INCLUDING the lightning's, is `move.b`/`move.b`/`eor.b`/`and.b`/`rts`, and EOR and AND set N/Z and clear V/C but leave X ALONE; actor_alloc_slot_high ($1b8e) is NINE instructions -- `movea.l`/`lea`/`move.w`, then `cmpi.w`/`beq`/`lea`/`dbf` round its loop, then `movea.l #$0`/`rts` -- with no arithmetic in it and a `dbf` that writes no condition code at all; and below the allocation there are only `move.w #imm`, `move.b #imm`, `clr.w`/`clr.b`, `bclr`/`bset`, `move.l` and `addq.l` on an ADDRESS register, the one arithmetic opcode form that sets no flags. So the bit is whatever player_step_and_arm left, the two `bsr`s at $a4a and $a4e being adjacent.
fn 0x151a player_collide_and_scroll # ctx
cmt 0x151a The largest single routine the wall hid, 1,170 bytes ($151a..$19ab), STILL UNPORTED. Entered only from actor_behavior_type01_player ($a6c) and only while state_flag_a32 is clear. Opens by turning the player's x,y into a COLLISION-MAP cell (`move.w (a0),d0 / move.w 2(a0),d1 / subi.w #16,d1 / asr.w #4` twice, then bg_map_row_stride * row + column into $23498); a cell code below 3 clears tile_33_flag and returns, a code at or below $22 selects a SCENE DESCRIPTOR out of $21828 and dispatches on its kind word, and codes $33..$39 are special tiles handled inline. Its body swallows the `O` run $1712..$17f4. CALLEE CENSUS, CORRECTED BY BATCH 40 PHASE C, and the earlier plate was wrong on three of its four claims: the outward transfers from $151a..$19ab are EXACTLY THREE -- `bsr.w $1b8e` (actor_alloc_slot_high, reconstructed) at $158e, `bra.w $6ade` at $15e8 and the `jmp $e5ba.l` at $1626 -- plus five `lea`/`jsr` data references ($99ac, $9aec, $17adc, $21828, $23498). It does NOT reach scene_spawn_from_script, does NOT reach $1b46 (whose two callers are both inside the $19ac tree) and does NOT reach resource_descriptor_fetch; all three were RETRACTED here. THREE THINGS THE NEXT PORTER NEEDS BEFORE STARTING, all measured in phase C. (a) $1932 IS A BUSY-WAIT ON THE SOUND MODULE: `lea $17adc.l,a5 / tst.b 378(a5) / bne.s $1932` spins until snd_engine_enabled ($17c56) goes zero, and only an interrupt clears it -- under the oracle that arm DOES NOT TERMINATE, so it needs a stop_pc or a declared boundary rather than a cap. (b) $1622 IS A TRIPLE POP: `lea 12(a7),a7 / jmp $e5ba.l` discards THREE return addresses, so that arm abandons $151a, its caller and its caller's caller. TWO instructions name $1622 -- the `beq.w` at $161c inside this routine and player_pending_event_gate's `bra.w` at $d16, which is how that gate reaches a stack unwind through ANOTHER routine's pop. (c) $6ade IS A SHARED TAIL, not this routine's: $6ad0 names it too, and its 40 bytes are unported and unnamed.
fn 0x19ac scene_spawn_from_script # ctx
cmt 0x19ac Reached from player_pending_event_gate. Takes record_ptr_10420 and scene_marker_cell_ptr ($e02e), stamps actor_free_marker into the remaining slots, then runs a BYTE-CODED script from a1: one byte indexes spawn_script_gate_table ($e42e) through `jsr (a0)`, the next indexes speech_script_table ($1015c) and posts its first byte as text_request with an INFINITE text_lifetime_request ($ffff). Covers $19ac..$1cc0 -- almost the whole of gap 10.
fn 0x1b46 scene_clear_marker_pair
cmt 0x1b46 34 bytes, $1b46..$1b67, RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/scene.c, differential in recreate/test/test_scene.py). CLEAR A MARKER CELL AND ITS TWIN: the byte at (a6) is read and cleared, then compared against 1(a6) and, failing that, -1(a6); whichever holds the same code is cleared as well, and a cell matching neither simply returns. a6 is a COLLISION-MAP cell, which both callers load from scene_marker_cell_ptr ($e02e) -- $19b4's `movea.l $e02e.l,a6` covers both, since $1ef2 lies inside $1ea8, which is scene_spawn_from_script's own third arm. TWO PLATE CORRECTIONS, and the first is the NAME. (a) THE REGISTER IS a6, NOT a5: the opcode word at $1b46 is $1016, `move.b (a6),d0`. (b) THE ROUTINE STEPS NO SPEECH SCRIPT, so `speech_script_step` is RETRACTED and the name is what the body does. The speech cursor is stepped by the `addq.l #1,$1017c.l` at $1aa6, two instructions ABOVE the `bsr` at $1aac -- position, not behaviour, is what named it. THIS LOGIC IS IN THE IMAGE TWICE: $de94, inside scene_spend_visit_budget, is the same six instructions spelt inline, and the ONLY difference is the ending -- this one `rts`s where that one takes `jmp $1ab4.w`. src/scene.c has ONE body serving both, returning whether a neighbour matched. CALLER CENSUS: exactly two instructions name it, the `bsr.w` at $1aac and the one at $1ef2, and no PC-relative read anywhere in the program does; BOTH lie inside the $19ac tree, so recreate/STATUS.md's "$1aac from the tree AND $1ef2 from the $151a one" is corrected here -- $151a does not name this address at all.
fn 0x1cc0 resource_descriptor_fetch # ctx
cmt 0x1cc0 `mulu.w #20,d7 / lea $248d8,a0 / lea 0(a0,d7.w),a1 / movea.l (a1),a0` -- the same 20-byte resource_table stride the sprite pass uses, so d7 is a resource index and a0 comes back pointing at the payload. Two `bsr.w` sites, $1cac and $1cb8, both inside the $19ac tree.
fn 0x1d1e glyph_stamp_8_rows # ctx
cmt 0x1d1e `lea 2(a0),a0 / move.w #7,d7 / move.b (a1)+,(a0) / addq.l #2,a0` x8 -- writes eight source bytes into a bitplane-interleaved destination two bytes apart, i.e. one 8-row glyph column.

var 0x21e6 player_posture_table_0

ONE operand site each, the three `lea`s at $2068, $207e and $2088. LAYOUT, by the `btst #3,8(a0)` arm that reads each field -- SET is FACING LEFT: 0 = idle right, 6 = idle left, 12 = jump left, 14 = jump right, 16 = fall left, 18 = fall right, 20 = the RIGHT walk cursor with its sixteen-word table at 22, and 54 = the LEFT one with its table at 56. 54 + 2 + 32 = 88, which is also $223e - $21e6 and $2296 - $223e. var 0x2310 player_transition_cursor
cmt 0x2310 Word: the cursor player_stage_transition's FIRST arm reads, with TWO twenty-four-word tables above it -- $2312 and $2342 -- chosen by `lea 48(a1),a1` on effect_state_21e4. ONE operand site for the base (`lea $2310.l,a1` at $1f66) plus the `move.w d0,$2310.l` at $1f90. Its wrap is `cmp.w #$30,d0`, an EQUALITY and not a mask, so a cursor that never lands on 48 walks out of the first table into the second; the frame it does wrap raises stage_anim_done_b10.
var 0x2372 player_attack_cursor
cmt 0x2372 Word: the SWING's cursor, with two eight-word tables above it -- $2374 (the WB_ACTOR_FLAG_SIDE_BIT CLEAR arm) and $2384 (SET). EXACTLY TWO operand sites in the whole image, `move.w $2372.l,d0` at $20e0 and `move.w d0,$2372.l` at $2122, both inside player_stage_transition -- so nothing else ever resets it, and since the wrap that ends a swing leaves it at zero, every swing starts there. THAT MATTERS BECAUSE OF THE DEFECT AT $20e8: the frame the cursor is found at zero, d0 is overwritten with the SFX id 6 for the trigger call, the stub restores it, and $2112 indexes the frame table with SIX -- so entries 0, 2 and 4 of both tables are dead and the cursor is stored as 8.

fn 0x1f54 player_stage_transition
cmt 0x1f54 656 bytes, $1f54..$21e3, RECONSTRUCTED WHOLE in batch 40 phase C (recreate/src/player.c, differential in recreate/test/test_player.py). THE FRAME'S LAST CALL ($a70), and ALSO called from player_pending_event_gate at $bb0 -- two `bsr` callers, which a whole-image census confirms are the only instructions naming it. FOUR FLAG ARMS IN ONE CHAIN with two shared tails ($205c's bare `rts`, the posture selector at $205e): $b10 set makes the whole routine an `rts`; $b0e runs a 24-frame TRANSITION off one of two tables $21e4 picks between, and its wrap raises $b10; $b16 runs a 16-frame one whose wrap raises $b18 and blanks the sprite; $b08 (the death handshake) runs a 16-frame one with no completion flag; and with none of them set the POSTURE SELECTOR chooses the player's sprite for the frame -- the hurt pair, then the ladder, then the swing, then standing / walking / jumping / falling out of one of three 88-byte posture records. IT IS CALLEE-CLEAN: the only thing it calls is snd_call_trigger_effect through stub +56. IT HOLDS THE ONLY READERS of bit 5 and bit 7 of 8(a0) ($2184, $20ca) and the `bclr #7` at $212a, so what player_step_and_arm's two flag writes BUY is decided here: bit 7 (fired) gates the swing and is lowered by its wrap, bit 5 (moved) chooses the walk cycle over standing still. THE 466 BYTES ABOVE IT ($21e4..$23b5) ARE ITS DATA and the block divides exactly -- see the var lines at $21e6, $223e, $2296, $22ee, $2310, $2372 and $2394. THE EXTENT CONFLICT batch 40 phase A registered IS RESOLVED IN THIS ROUTINE'S FAVOUR: $1fa2 is this routine's SECOND ARM (see that plate). A DEFECT REPRODUCED, not tidied: on the frame the swing starts, `move.w #$6,d0` loads the SFX id, the stub restores every register, and the `move.w 0(a1,d0.w),6(a0)` at $2112 then indexes the frame table with that SIX -- so entries 0, 2 and 4 of both attack tables are unreachable. AND ONE MORE CORRECTION: the earlier plate's "a flag word at $21e4 that $205e tests" undercounts -- effect_state_21e4 has FIVE readers and every one of them is in this routine.
fn 0x205c stub_rts_205c
cmt 0x205c A bare `rts`, two bytes, INSIDE player_stage_transition's own $1f54..$21e3 body. FIVE instructions name it and a census (batch 40 phase B) says which: one `bsr.w` at $2010 -- the no-op call the earlier plate recorded -- and FOUR `bra.w` at $1ff8, $2034, $203e and $2052, which are that routine's shared TAIL. So the `fn` directive ABOVE this plate is an entry inside another routine's body, the same shape the $1fa2 plate retracts; it is left standing because the `bsr` makes it a genuine call target, and registered in recreate/STATUS.md rather than resolved here. Batch 40 phase C ported the enclosing routine WITHOUT removing it and nothing broke, which is the measurement that directive was waiting for: unlike an `fn` at $1fa2, this one names an address Ghidra can already see a call to.

fn 0x23b6 actor_hit_by_player_shot
cmt 0x23b6 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, differential in recreate/test/test_behavior.py). 172 bytes, $23b6..$2461, and one of the three high-fan-in routines batch 28 left as FUN_*. TWENTY-FIVE `bsr.w` callers and every one of them is `tst.w d7 / bne.w` into its own damage arm, so d7 = $ffff is "an attack landed on me". TWO ways in. (1) `tst.w $714.w` (flash_timer): while the screen flash is running, `move.w #$8c,d0 / bsr $67f8` reports a hit for any actor within 140 pixels of the followed record horizontally -- no projectile and no consumption. (2) Otherwise it walks SIX records from `actor_table_selected + 416` -- exactly actor_alloc_slot_high's pool -- skipping $ffbe and any type outside $30..$32 (signed `bgt`/`blt`), and tests `|dx| <= 14(a0) + 14(a1)` and `|dy| <= 16(a0) + 16(a1)` with `neg.w` on the negative side. On a hit it CONSUMES the record: `move.w #$ffbe,(a1)` frees it, except type $31, which gets `move.b #$1,30(a1)` and stays alive. Either way the loop stops. The `clr.w d7` at $245e is a deliberate no-op -- the `moveq #0,d7` at $23b6 already cleared it and nothing in the loop writes it.
fn 0x2f22 actor_step_facing
cmt 0x2f22 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 36 bytes, $2f22..$2f45. `btst #3,8(a0)` picks the direction -- SET steps LEFT ($10a2), clear steps RIGHT ($1170) -- with the step count in d7 from the caller, and bit 3 SET means the followed record is to the actor's left ($67c2), so this walks TOWARD it. Then `tst.b d0 / bne` on the probe's outcome and `bchg #3,8(a0)`: a BLOCKED step ($0) flips the facing bit, a clear one ($ff) leaves it. It does not set the bit first, which is the whole difference from $2fce. Shared by behaviour slots 9 and 24.
fn 0x2f46 actor_random_facing_hop
cmt 0x2f46 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 35). 64 bytes, $2f46..$2f85, bounded by actor_tick_timer30's entry. ONE `bsr` caller -- slot 9's walk at $2e6a -- by a whole-image scan of every branch and absolute-jump form. RENAMED from actor_random_turn, whose `# ctx` plate called it "a coin-flip turn": it turns AND LAUNCHES, and nothing vetoes the launch. `btst #2,8(a0) / beq` returns having written nothing for a record that is not SUPPORTED; otherwise one rng_next word does both jobs -- bit 2 CLEAR `bset #3,8(a0)` (face left), SET `bclr` -- and then bits 0 and 1 of 8(a0) up, bit 2 down and `move.b #$a,11(a0)`, which is actor_start_motion_at_speed's three writes with the speed spelt inline (the shape $2f86, $3528 and $357a also have) at the SAME $a $2f86 uses. Contrast $2f86, where `btst #2` of the same word VETOES the relaunch instead of choosing a side.
fn 0x2f86 actor_tick_timer30
cmt 0x2f86 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 72 bytes, $2f86..$2fcd -- the plate this replaces described only its first four instructions. Counts 30(a0) down and returns; on the frame the byte is already zero it reloads it with $32 and THEN, only for a record with bit 2 of 8(a0) (supported) set and only when `btst #2` of rng_next's word is clear, relaunches it: bits 0 and 1 of 8(a0) up, bit 2 down, 11(a0) := $a and 18(a0) cleared -- actor_start_motion_at_speed's three writes with the speed spelt inline, the shape $2b8e also has. The reload runs BEFORE the supported test, so an unsupported record still gets a fresh countdown. Shared by behaviour slots 12 and 26.

fn 0x3006 actor_anim_step_facing_list
cmt 0x3006 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 52 bytes, $3006..$3039 -- the plate this replaces described its first four instructions and its name claimed it only picked. `btst #3,8(a0)` selects (a1) or 4(a1), a two-longword left/right frame-list pair the caller supplies, then `lea 0(a1,d0.w),a1` on the BYTE cursor 18(a0), `move.w (a1)+,6(a0)` publishes the frame and `tst.w (a1) / bpl` reads the word AFTER it: negative zeroes the cursor, otherwise `addq.b #2,18(a0)`. So the terminator is a negative word and is never itself published. FOURTEEN `bsr` sites, the busiest leaf in this tier.
fn 0x4fea actor_select_sprite_by_flag
cmt 0x4fea RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 48 bytes, $4fea..$5019 -- its three arms fall into one `rts` at $5018, which is the routine's own last instruction and not shared with anything. fn 0x501a actor_hop_ascend_step
cmt 0x501a RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 44 bytes, $501a..$5045, and the second of batch 28's three FUN_*. THIRTY-SIX `bsr` callers -- more than any other leaf in this tier, and SEVEN more than the 29 batch 28's plate counted (35 `bsr.w` plus the one `bsr.s` at $504a). `btst #0,8(a0) / bne` gates it: with the moving bit clear it returns having written nothing. Otherwise `moveq #0,d0 / move.b 11(a0),d0 / sub.w d0,2(a0)` lifts the record by its own speed byte (zero-extended, so 0..255 pixels, and the word wraps), then `subq.b #1,11(a0)` and, on a result of ZERO, `bclr #0,8(a0)` and `move.b #$1,11(a0)`. So the ascent decelerates by construction and the byte is left at 1, not 0.
fn 0x517a hud_award_gold_from_descriptor
cmt 0x517a RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 33), renamed from hud_award_from_record_10424 and the `# ctx` tag dropped -- the body names what the award IS. 50 bytes, $517a..$51ab. `movea.l $10424.l,a1 / move.w 12(a1),d0` takes a packed-BCD amount out of the 32-byte SCENE DESCRIPTOR record_ptr_10424 names (the copy of record_ptr_10420, so the same record scene_run_frame reads its kind from -- field 12 was not among the ones $21828's plate listed); bcd_add_random_1_to_4 jitters it by one to four IN d0; bcd_add_counter_bd6e folds the WORD into the gold counter; text_write_gold_digits_a2ac draws its low BCD byte as two characters INSIDE message id 3's own shipped string ("        gold get." at $a2a7); bcd_add_score_bd70 adds a constant $20; and text_request := 3 with text_lifetime_request := $32 posts that message. THREE INDEPENDENT WITNESSES SAY THE COUNTER IS MONEY: the message text is "gold get.", the amount drawn in it is the amount added to bcd_counter_bd6e, and scene_run_frame's shop arm compares its price against that same counter and calls bcd_sub_counter_bd6e. THE X CHAIN: bcd_add_random_1_to_4's `abcd` leaves a BCD carry that $b562's first `abcd` folds in -- CARRIED as of batch 33 and pinned by a case whose award's low BCD byte plus the declared draw passes $99. The score add at $5196 is NOT part of it: text_write_gold_digits_a2ac runs between, and its last X-writer on both exits is `addi.b #$30` on a nibble masked to $0..$f, which cannot carry out of $30..$3f. TWO `bsr` callers, $4fce and $5070 -- behaviour slots 31 and 32.
fn 0x51d8 text_write_gold_digits_a2ac
cmt 0x51d8 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 33), renamed from hud_write_bcd_word_a2ad and the `# ctx` tag dropped -- the old plate said FOUR digits at $a2ad and the bytes give TWO, at $a2ac..$a2ad. 48 bytes, $51d8..$5207, bounded by slot 33's entry. `move.w d0,d1 / andi.w #$f,d1 / addi.b #$30,d1 / move.b d1,$a2ad.l` draws the UNITS digit, then `ror.w #4,d0 / andi.w #$f,d0 / bne` draws the TENS -- or, on a zero, writes $20 (a SPACE) to $a2ac instead, so a one-digit amount is left-blanked rather than zero-padded. Only two characters are ever written, whatever the rest of d0 holds, so an amount above 99 BCD shows its low two digits. The pair sits inside text_message_table's record 2 (id 3), five spaces into "        gold get.", which is what makes this a patch of a shipped STRING and not a HUD field -- hence the rename off the hud_ prefix. ONE `bsr` caller, $518c inside hud_award_gold_from_descriptor.
fn 0x539e scene_copy_record_fields
cmt 0x539e RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/player.c, differential in recreate/test/test_player.py, batch 40). 30 bytes, $539e..$53bb. EIGHT longwords through a2, not the five the earlier plate claimed ("20(record_ptr_10420) and four following"): `movea.l $10420.l,a3 / move.l 20(a3),(a2)+` writes the SCENE descriptor's own position longword over the new record's x and y, and only then does `lea 4(a1),a1` skip the template's first longword and copy the SEVEN above it. So the 32 bytes at $537e supply everything but the position, their first four bytes are never read, and the record is placed from the scene rather than from the template. a1 = the template, a2 = the destination, a3 clobbered. fn 0x5a3c actor_advance_anim16

fn 0x5c6e actor_followed_overlap_mask
cmt 0x5c6e RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 244 bytes, $5c6e..$5d61, ending exactly where rad_depack begins, and the last of batch 28's three FUN_*. FORTY-TWO `bsr` callers, the busiest routine in the behaviour tier. `lea $9aec.l,a1 / tst.w $a32.w / beq` picks the followed record exactly as followed_actor_record ($67e0) does, and the actor's box is `(a0) -/+ 14(a0)` by `2(a0) - 16(a0) .. 2(a0)`. THREE INDEPENDENT TESTS into three bits of d0, and TWO of them are gated on the followed record's own SPRITE id at 6(a1) -- so what the player's animation is showing decides which run. bit 0: a small box `x+7..x+13` by `y-12..y-6` off the followed record, live only for sprite $11e..$125, and shifted 20 pixels the other way above $121. bit 1: the two footprints, `x -/+ 14(a1)` by `y - 16(a1) .. y`, with the bottom edge compared against the ACTOR's bottom rather than its top. bit 2: ONE POINT, `x+22` (or `x-22` for sprite $118) by `y-9`, for sprites $117 and $118 only. Thirty callers read bit 1 and twelve read bit 0; NOTHING in the image reads bit 2 on its own. `clr.w d0` clears only the low word, so the caller's high half comes back.
fn 0x6528 actor_aim_velocity
cmt 0x6528 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/actor.c, batch 37). 94 bytes, $6528..$6585, bounded by its own table. THE AIM TABLE: given (d0,d1) = a source point, (d2,d3) = a target and d4 = a row, it hands back in d0/d1 a signed byte pair -- a velocity -- and saves every other register (`movem.l d2-a6,-(sp)`), so those two ARE the result. The row is `asl.w #5,d4` = 32 bytes = SIXTEEN pairs; the pair is chosen by folding both deltas into the first quadrant (`neg.w` + `eori.w #$c` / `#$4` on d4, and an `exg` on each of two arms), then measuring the ratio with three `cmp.w`/`addq.w` steps against d2 halved, doubled and doubled again. CORRECTION (batch 37): the earlier name and plate said this SPAWNS from a table -- it allocates nothing and writes no memory at all. THE TWO CALLERS ARE BOTH READ SINCE BATCH 39: `bsr.w` at $43e6 inside behaviour slot 21 (row 6) and `jsr $6528.l` at $58a8 inside behaviour slot 45 (ROW 1), and a whole-image census finds no third. It also has an ENTRY PIN of its own since batch 39 (recreate/test/test_actor.py), which it went without because the 94 bytes need eight encodings no other body in this project spells -- `movem.l` in both directions (the register mask is numbered the OPPOSITE way round for the two forms), `roxl.w`, `exg` in both operand orders, `neg.w`, `eori.w`, `addq.w`, `adda.w` and `ext.w`. AND THE `roxl.w #1,d2` AT $6564 READS THE X FLAG THE `asr.w #1,d2` AT $655c LEFT, which restores the halved value exactly -- EXCEPT when the `addq.w #1,d4` at $6562 runs in between and overwrites X with its own (always zero) carry, in which case the low bit is lost. That asymmetry is behaviour, not noise, and recreate/src/actor.c reproduces it.
var 0x6586 actor_aim_velocity_table
cmt 0x6586 actor_aim_velocity's rows: SIXTEEN signed byte pairs each, stride $20, indexed by the routine's own d4. ONE operand site in the image, the `lea $6586.l,a1` at $652e. TWO ROWS ARE READ BY PORTED CODE (batch 39 -- the earlier revision of THIS plate said only row 6 was, and that the other caller was unported; that correction was landed on cmt 0x6528 and not here, which is the cited-plate rule again): row 6 from behaviour slot 21's `move.w #$6,d4`, which puts $6646..$6665 in use, and ROW 1 from behaviour slot 45's `move.w #$1,d4` at $58a4, which puts $65a6..$65c5 in use. Row 0 ($6586..$65a5) is sixteen ZERO pairs in the shipped image, so a caller reading it gets no velocity at all. What bounds the table ABOVE row 6 is still not established: no ported caller reads a row past it and nothing in the image computes d4.
var 0x5160 actor_anim_5160_frames
cmt 0x5160 The frame word list actor_relaunch_and_anim_5160 steps, addressed `lea $5160.w,a1 / lea 0(a1,d0.w),a1` with d0 = 18(a0) -- so like actor_spawn_anim_frames the cursor is a BYTE OFFSET. Terminated by an $ffff word, which is read one word PAST the frame just published and zeroes the cursor. THREE readers, established by a WHOLE-IMAGE SCAN OF BOTH ABSOLUTE ENCODINGS (batch 34's second correction here): the long form `00005160` occurs once, at $5138, and the short form `5160` at three WORD-ALIGNED sites, $513a (the low half of that same longword, not an operand), $58fa and $6874 -- plus $6f2f, which is ODD and so cannot be an operand. So: actor_relaunch_and_anim_5160's `lea $5160.w,a1` at $6872; actor_behavior_type32's `lea $5160.l,a1` at $5136, off its own GLOBAL word cursor (ported batch 34); and actor_behavior_type46's `lea $5160.w,a1` at $58f8, RECONSTRUCTED in batch 39, which steps the table off the record byte 18(a0) exactly as $6872 does -- `moveq #0,d0 / move.b 18(a0),d0 / lea $5160.w,a1 / lea 0(a1,d0.w),a1 / move.w (a1)+,6(a0) / addq.b #2,18(a0) / cmpi.w #$ffff,(a1) / bne / clr.b 18(a0)`, i.e. the same post-increment publish and the same look-ahead. TWO earlier revisions of this plate were wrong in the same way: the first said $6872's `lea` was the only reference, and batch 34's correction said TWO readers because it swept the long form plus one short site and missed the other -- the operand-scan trap docs/methodology.md documents.
fn 0x6786 sound_request_9
cmt 0x6786 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 33). SIXTEEN bytes, $6786..$6795, bounded by actor_stun_followed's entry -- CORRECTION: the batch-33 scan called it 20, which would have run FOUR bytes into that routine, swallowing its whole `move.w #$8,d0` ($6796..$6799 -- a `move.w #imm,Dn` is four bytes, not two). `move.w #9,d0 / clr.w d1 / lea $17adc,a1 / jmp 56(a1)`: actor_stun_followed's own opening four instructions one request higher, with a `jmp` where that one has a `jsr`, so the stub's `rts` returns to THIS routine's caller and nothing follows it. It takes no record and reads no image state, so the SFX the stub writes is the whole of its behaviour. FIVE `bsr` callers, all dispatch rows in the $4e38..$5406 band ($4e4e, $4ee0, $4fca, $506c and $5214 — slots 28, 30, 31, 32 and 33).
fn 0x6796 actor_stun_followed
cmt 0x6796 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 30). 44 bytes, $6796..$67c1. CORRECTION: it is not "a facing update" -- it writes no facing bit at all. `move.w #$8,d0 / clr.w d1 / lea $17adc,a1 / jsr 56(a1)` is snd_trigger_effect(id 8, channel A) through the stub; then `bsr followed_actor_record` and `move.w $bd68,d0 / add.w d0,d0 / move.w #$a,d1 / sub.w d0,d1 / move.b d1,29(a1) / clr.b 22(a1)` -- a STEP COUNT of $a - 2 * effect_state_bd68 stamped into the followed record's 29(a1) and its 22(a1) cleared. That is the same `n - 2 * <state word>` shape actor_damage_followed spells over effect_state_bd66, and it makes $6796 the FIRST reader of $bd68 among the recovered functions (the $bd66 plate's "only $bd66 has a reader" is corrected by it). 29(a1) is the byte $ec8 runs down one map step at a time, so what this buys is frames of the player's own control. ELEVEN control-flow sites, in behaviour slots 39..45, 51..53 and 57 -- but only THREE are `bsr.w` and the other EIGHT are `bra.w` TAIL JUMPS, which earlier revisions of this plate got wrong by calling all eleven calls. The three CALLS are $5514 (slot 39), $565c (slot 41) and $5698 (slot 42), and each returns into more of its own frame -- 39 and 41 into the shared drift/break-up tail at $5534, 42 into its own walk. The eight TAILS are $55c4 (40), $5746 (43), $57da (44), $586e (45), $5ace (51), $5b58 (52), $5c08 (53) and $7290 (57), for which this routine's `rts` is the handler's own.
fn 0x6840 actor_step_toward_followed
cmt 0x6840 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 50 bytes, $6840..$6871. CORRECTION (batch 29): it sets no flag at all -- it never touches 8(a0). `bsr $67e0` for the followed record, then a HOMING step of d7 pixels on BOTH axes: `cmp.w d0,d1 / bgt` on the x words picks `sub.w d7,(a0)` or `add.w d7,(a0)`, and the same on the y words against `2(a1) - $10` picks `sub.w d7,2(a0)` or `add.w d7,2(a0)`. That $10 is the same ride height $6d70 snaps the followed record to. ONE `bsr` caller, in behaviour slot 10.
fn 0x6872 actor_relaunch_and_anim_5160
cmt 0x6872 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 84 bytes, $6872..$68c5. The sprite comes from the CURSOR 18(a0), not from the two the old name cited: `lea $5160.w,a1 / lea 0(a1,d0.w),a1 / move.w (a1)+,6(a0) / addq.b #2,18(a0)` and `cmpi.w #$ffff,(a1)` zeroes the cursor on the terminator. Bit 2 of 8(a0) and 30(a0) gate a RELAUNCH in front of that: a supported record whose 30(a0) is not already $1 is decremented, the new byte is copied into 11(a0) as the speed, bit 2 goes down and bits 0/1 up. The countdown therefore stops ON $1 rather than on zero, so the launch fires on the tick from 2 to 1 and the speed it launches at is 1. TWO callers, behaviour slots 28 and 38.
fn 0x6938 text_post_bonus_points_a4be
cmt 0x6938 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 38), renamed off the hud_ prefix and the digit count it used to carry, and the `# ctx` tag dropped -- BOTH halves of the old plate were wrong. 82 bytes, $6938..$6989, bounded by actor_spawn_anim_step's entry. The `lea` is into a6, not a5 (`4df9 0000a4be`), and it writes FIVE characters, not six: `swap d0 / move.w #$5,d7` then two loops that between them emit exactly d7 characters, so the digits drawn are nibbles 4..0 of the addend -- its LOW FIVE, everything above them invisible. The blanking loop writes $20 for a leading zero nibble and decrements WITHOUT testing; the digit loop writes `$30 + nibble` and tests after decrementing. TWO RUNAWAYS FOLLOW and neither is reachable from the one caller: an addend of ZERO never leaves the blanking loop, and an addend whose low FIVE nibbles are all zero enters the digit loop with the counter already at 0 and wraps it to $ffff. The caller `beq`s on zero and no shipped actor_kind_table row has the second shape. $a4be is not a HUD field: it is the first character of text_message_table record 15's own shipped string, "      Bonus Points." (SIX leading spaces), which the routine then posts (text_request $10, text_lifetime_request $32) -- so this is a patch of a shipped STRING exactly as text_write_gold_digits_a2ac is, hence the same rename off the hud_ prefix. ONE `bsr` caller, $548e inside actor_behavior_type38_pickup, on the scored-pickup arm.
var 0xa4be text_bonus_digits
cmt 0xa4be FIVE CHARACTERS INSIDE A SHIPPED STRING, not a field of their own: $a4be..$a4c2 are the first five of the SIX spaces that open text_message_table record 15 (id 16), "      Bonus Points." at $a4be -- so a five-digit amount leaves exactly ONE space before "Bonus" and a leading-blanked one leaves more. text_post_bonus_points_a4be patches the score amount in there and then posts the message. ONE operand site in the whole image, the `lea $a4be.l,a6` at $6938 (its longword operand at $693a). The shipped bytes are spaces, so a run that never scored a pickup shows the message with no number in it -- text_gold_digits' property one message over.
fn 0x6d5a actor_sprite_from_6ed8
cmt 0x6d5a RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 22 bytes, $6d5a..$6d6f. `move.w 14(a0),d0 / lsl.w #3,d0 / lea $6ed8,a2 / adda.w d0,a2 / move.w (a2),6(a0)` -- an 8-byte-stride sprite table indexed by 14(a0), the `adda.w` SIGN-EXTENDING the scaled index. It ends `bra.w $67e0`, a TAIL JUMP into followed_actor_record, so the followed record is in a1 on the way out and is this routine's result. Shared by behaviour slots 54, 55 and 56.
fn 0x6d70 actor_platform_carry_followed
cmt 0x6d70 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 104 bytes, $6d70..$6dd7. CORRECTION (batch 29): it is not a test -- it is the MOVING PLATFORM's catch, and the plate this replaces stopped at its first four instructions. The record at a0 is the platform, a1 the followed record and a2 a BAND record the caller supplies (4(a2) how far left of the platform's x the band starts, 6(a2) how wide). Gates: `2(a1) - (2(a0) - $10)` must be >= 0 and <= $a, and the followed x must lie in the band. Then it WRITES: $6ef0 := 1, `bset #1,22(a0)`, the followed y is SNAPPED to `2(a0) - $10`, and on 8(a1) bit 2 (supported) up, bits 4, 0 and 1 (falling, moving, launched) down, bit 5 up, 11(a1) cleared. Shared by behaviour slots 54, 55 and 56.
fn 0x6dd8 actor_platform_release_check
cmt 0x6dd8 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 68 bytes, $6dd8..$6e1b. $6d70's other half: the same band test over 4(a2)/6(a2), then `btst #1,9(a1)`, `btst #4,9(a1)` and `btst #0,8(a1)`. FOUR ways to lose the ride and ONE to keep it -- the followed record must still be in the band, must carry neither bit 1 (landed) nor bit 4 (invulnerable) of 9(a1), and must NOT have bit 0 of 8(a1) (moving) set; the `beq $6e1a` on that last test is the only path that returns without writing. Every other path does `bclr #1,22(a0)` and `clr.w $6ef0`. Shared by behaviour slots 54, 55 and 56.
var 0x6ef0 actor_platform_ridden
cmt 0x6ef0 Word: 1 while actor_platform_carry_followed has the followed record on a platform, cleared by actor_platform_release_check. THREE operand sites -- the `move.w #$1` at $6da2, the `clr.w` at $6e14 and a `tst.w $6ef0.w` at $6f42 inside behaviour slot 56, its one reader.
fn 0x6e8c actor_platform_release_blocked_rider
cmt 0x6e8c RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 30). 76 bytes, $6e8c..$6ed7, bounded by the sprite/band rows at $6ed8. a0 is the PLATFORM and a1 the record riding it. It turns the RIDER's x and y into a collision-map cell (`lea $23494,a6 / move.w (a6),d1` for the stride, `lsr.w #4` on each coordinate, `addq.w #4` for the same cell bias actor_map_cell_lookup applies, `adda.w` on the column and `adda.l` on the `mulu.w` product) and tests THAT cell and the one beside it for tile $1 or $2, four `cmpi.b` in two post-increment pairs. On a hit: `subq.w #2,2(a1)` lifts the rider back out, `clr.w $6ef0` ends the ride and `bclr #1,22(a0)` clears the platform's riding bit; otherwise it writes nothing. TWO ASYMMETRIES, both reproduced: it probes collision_map_default UNCONDITIONALLY where every other probe picks a map on state_flag_a32 (the same asymmetry $10a2's ground test has), and its two shifts are `lsr.w`, UNSIGNED, where $13c8's are `asr.w` -- so a negative coordinate lands 4096 columns along instead of below zero. Two `bsr` callers, slots 54 ($6e6c) and 56 ($6f60), each on the frame its platform moves DOWN.
fn 0x701c actor_face_followed_reset_22
cmt 0x701c RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c). 40 bytes, $701c..$7043. CORRECTION (batch 29): there is no step. `bsr $67e0`, `bset #3,8(a0)`, then `move.w (a1),d0 / cmp.w (a0),d0 / bgt` and `bclr #3,8(a0)` on the other arm -- so the bit is left SET while the followed record's x is strictly GREATER, i.e. while it is to the actor's RIGHT. That is the OPPOSITE polarity to actor_set_side_flag ($67c2), which raises the same bit while the followed record is to the LEFT: two routines eight hundred bytes apart write one bit with opposite meanings. It then forces a NONZERO 22(a0) to $3. Called from behaviour slot 7.

# --- the swoop/dive state machine behind behaviour slot 7 --------------------
var 0x745e actor_swoop_path_far
cmt 0x745e TWENTY-FOUR bytes, $745e..$748f: twelve dx,dy word pairs and the $ffff sentinel, the path actor_swoop_state0_acquire takes for a drop over 64 -- reached by `movea.l #$745e,a1` at $72f2, an IMMEDIATE, where the other four come out of actor_swoop_path_table. BYTE-IDENTICAL to path 3 at $742c, so the far arm flies the longest table path anyway; the two are separate blobs only because one is indexed and the other is not. Inside actor_swoop_paths, whose plate covers the blob as a whole.
var 0x7490 actor_swoop_state_table
cmt 0x7490 FOUR longwords, $7490..$749f -- $72c2, $7328, $7366, $739e -- indexed by the state byte 22(a0) at $712e/$7130 (`clr.w d0 / move.b 22(a0),d0 / lsl.w #2 / lea $7490,a1 / movea.l 0(a1,d0.w),a1 / jsr (a1)`) inside behaviour slot 7's body. ONE reference in the image. NOTHING BOUNDS THE INDEX: a byte reaches $7490 + 0..1020 and the longword there is CALLED, so a state above 3 enters arbitrary data. The port reports that address rather than following it, the same refusal actor_dispatch_behavior makes, and test/test_behavior.py enumerates all 256 bytes against the C alone -- no differential can drive one.
var 0x74a0 actor_type07_frames_left
cmt 0x74a0 TWELVE words + an $ffff sentinel, $74a0..$74b9: $8e $8e $8f $8f $90 $90 $91 $91 $90 $90 $8f $8f. Behaviour slot 7's list while bit 3 of 8(a0) is SET and bit 1 of 30(a0) is clear. THE SENTINEL IS NEVER READ -- the 23(a0) cursor is wrapped at 12 by `cmpi.b #$c` BEFORE the `move.w 0(a1,d0.w)` -- so it only bounds one list from the next. One reference, the `lea $74a0.l` at $70ec.
var 0x74ba actor_type07_frames_right
cmt 0x74ba TWELVE words + sentinel, $74ba..$74d3: $94 $94 $95 $95 $96 $96 $97 $97 $96 $96 $95 $95. The same list for a CLEAR side bit. One reference, the `lea $74ba.l` at $710c.
var 0x74d4 actor_type07_frames_unreferenced
cmt 0x74d4 TWELVE words + sentinel, $74d4..$74ed: $92 $92 $93 $93 $92 $92 $93 $93 $92 $92 $93 $93. A FIFTH list of exactly the shape of the four around it AND NOTHING IN THE IMAGE REFERENCES IT -- no `lea`, no `movea`, no computed address; it is bounded only by the two lists either side, and the cursor's wrap at 12 means no live path could reach it from a neighbour either. Recorded as an unreferenced variant rather than trimmed. (A byte-pattern scan finds one coincidental hit, the `bsr` displacement at $6cf2, which is not an operand.)
var 0x74ee actor_type07_frames_marked_left
cmt 0x74ee TWELVE words + sentinel, $74ee..$7507: $1 $1 $1 $2 $2 $2 $1 $1 $1 $2 $2 $2. Slot 7's list while bit 1 of 30(a0) -- slot 8's own mark -- is SET and bit 3 of 8(a0) is also set. One reference, the `lea $74ee.l` at $70fc.
var 0x7508 actor_type07_frames_marked_right
cmt 0x7508 TWELVE words + sentinel, $7508..$7521, bounded above by the scroll engine's own entry at $7522: $4 $4 $4 $5 $5 $5 $4 $4 $4 $5 $5 $5. The marked list for a CLEAR side bit. One reference, the `lea $7508.l` at $711c.
fn 0x72c2 actor_swoop_state0_acquire
cmt 0x72c2 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 32). 102 bytes, $72c2..$7327. State 0, and it writes NOTHING but actor_set_side_flag's own byte unless all three gates pass: |x - followed x| <= 64 (the two `cmp.w` are STRICT, so both edges are inside), the followed record at or BELOW (`bmi` on `2(a1) - 2(a0)`), and at least 8 below (`subq.w #8,d0 / bmi`, which is also what keeps the shift's index non-negative). A drop over 64 takes the fixed path at $745e; otherwise `(d0-8) >> 4 << 2` picks one of the four in actor_swoop_path_table -- sixteen pixels of drop per path, four paths, and the `ble` above caps it. Stores the chosen path as an OFFSET from $73de (`suba.l #$73de,a1 / move.w a1,24(a0)`), the launch y in 26(a0), and 22(a0) := 1. Reached ONLY through actor_swoop_state_table.
fn 0x7328 actor_swoop_state1_run_path
cmt 0x7328 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 32). 62 bytes, $7328..$7365. State 1. Walks the path a word PAIR at a time from `$73de + 24(a0)` -- `adda.w d1,a1` SIGN-EXTENDS the cursor, so a negative 24(a0) reads below the blob and nothing bounds it. A negative first word ends the path and sets 22(a0) := 2, and THAT ARM WRITES ONE BYTE: the cursor is not written back, so a record leaves state 1 holding the sentinel's own offset. Otherwise dx is added to or subtracted from (a0) by bit 3 of 8(a0), dy is added to 2(a0), and the advanced cursor is written back as an offset. Reached ONLY through actor_swoop_state_table.
fn 0x7366 actor_swoop_state2_home_x
cmt 0x7366 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 32). 56 bytes, $7366..$739d. State 2. Moves 4 pixels a frame toward the followed record's x; the arrival test is `bge` after the left step and `ble` after the right, both INCLUSIVE, so landing exactly on the target arrives. On arrival it does `bchg #3,8(a0)` TWICE -- a no-op pair that leaves the facing bit as it found it -- then 22(a0) := 3. The pair is reproduced because it is what the bytes do; NO DIFFERENTIAL CAN SEE IT (both writes cancel in memory), so the entry-byte pin is its only surface and the mutant that drops it is EQUIVALENT. Reached ONLY through actor_swoop_state_table.
fn 0x739e actor_swoop_state3_descend
cmt 0x739e RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/behavior.c, batch 32). 48 bytes, $739e..$73cd, bounded by actor_swoop_path_table. State 3, and the machine's ONLY map probe -- `move.w #$2,d7` then $10a2 or $1170 by bit 3 of 8(a0), with the blocked/clear answer DISCARDED (no `tst.b d0` follows the `bsr`), so a wall stops the record only by leaving its x alone. Then `subq.w #2,2(a0)` a frame until `move.w 26(a0),d0 / cmp.w 2(a0),d0 / blt` says the record is back at or above its launch y, at which point `clr.b 22(a0)` returns it to state 0. Reached ONLY through actor_swoop_state_table.
var 0x73ce actor_swoop_path_table
cmt 0x73ce FOUR longwords, $73ce..$73dd -- $73de, $73f8, $7412, $742c -- read by actor_swoop_state0_acquire and immediately turned into an offset by `suba.l #$73de,a1`, so the table and its payload are one blob. ONE reference in the image.
var 0x73de actor_swoop_paths
cmt 0x73de THE PATH BLOB, $73de..$748f, and the base every cursor in 24(a0) is an offset from. FIVE paths of dx,dy word pairs terminated by $ffff: $73de (6 pairs), $73f8 (6), $7412 (6), $742c (12) and the FIXED far path $745e (12), which actor_swoop_state0_acquire reaches by `movea.l #$745e,a1` instead of through the table. $745e IS BYTE-IDENTICAL TO $742c -- the same twelve pairs -- so a record over the near window flies the longest table path anyway; the two are separate blobs because one is indexed and the other immediate. The $ffff sentinel is READ (it is what ends the path), unlike the frame lists' at $74a0..$7521. NOT code: the 608 bytes $72c2..$7522 the coverage scan calls a hole are the four state routines ($72c2..$73cd), actor_swoop_path_table and this blob.

# --- the sprite copy-row-unrolled family behind sprites_cru_install ----------

fn 0xe948 sprite_cru_copy_15w

fn 0xe95e sprite_cru_copy_20w
