# --- the byte-code gate table scene_spawn_from_script dispatches through -----
var 0xe42e spawn_script_gate_table
cmt 0xe42e FOUR longwords, $e42e..$e43d. Slot 0 is never read (the caller's `beq` skips index 0) and holds $02140202, which is not an address; slots 1..3 are $e43e, $e456, $e46c. ONE reference: `lea $e42e,a0 / movea.l 0(a0,d0.w),a0 / jsr (a0)` at $1a70 inside scene_spawn_from_script, with d0 a byte from the script stream scaled by 4.
fn 0xe43e spawn_gate_unless_bbc8_eq1
cmt 0xe43e If hud_slot_bbc8 != 1, rewrite the pending spawn record a1 points at as `(a1) := 7` and `1(a1) := 0` -- i.e. veto the scripted spawn and substitute type 7. Ends at the shared `rts` at $e454.
fn 0xe456 spawn_gate_unless_bbc8_eq3
cmt 0xe456 The same veto against hud_slot_bbc8 == 3.
fn 0xe46c spawn_gate_unless_bbc8_eq4
cmt 0xe46c The same veto against hud_slot_bbc8 == 4.

# --- the pickup effect table behind behaviour slot 38 -----------------------
var 0x105ac pickup_effect_table
cmt 0x105ac FOURTEEN longwords, $105ac..$105e3, the SIBLING of effect_handler_table ($1023a) and bounded the same way -- slot 0 holds $105e4, the byte immediately past the table. ONE `lea`, at $5496 (the instruction; its longword operand is at $5498), by a whole-image scan of both absolute encodings and both PC-relative forms: `move.w 10(a1),d0 / lea $105ac.l,a1 / add.w d0,d0 / add.w d0,d0 / movea.l 0(a1,d0.w),a1 / jsr (a1)` inside actor_behavior_type38_pickup, where a1 was a 16-byte actor_kind_table row. INDEX UNBOUNDED AT BOTH ENDS, and ALIASING with it: the two `add.w`s scale inside sixteen bits and the extension word then sign-extends, so entry `s` is reached by `s`, `s+$4000`, `s+$8000` and `s+$c000` -- 56 of the 65,536 index values reach an entry and 65,480 read a longword outside the table and `jsr` through it. That is actor_dispatch_behavior's wrapped offset at a smaller table. Every handler but slot 0's ends by posting a text_request and text_lifetime_request := 50, exactly like effect_handler_table's -- and THREE of them post id 0, which is not "no message" but a CANCEL of whatever text_post_bonus_points_a4be posted a few instructions earlier. RECONSTRUCTED end to end in batch 38 (recreate/src/effects.c and the dispatch in recreate/src/behavior.c).
fn 0x105e4 pickup_effect_none
cmt 0x105e4 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/effects.c, batch 38). A bare `rts`, slot 0 -- and the byte that bounds pickup_effect_table above. It is a reconstruction rather than a hole because the dispatch has to be able to answer "entry 0 ran" separately from "the index left the table".
fn 0x105e6 pickup_effect_grant_bbc4
cmt 0x105e6 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/effects.c, batch 38). hud_slot_bbc4 := $01ff, text_request 0. THE ONE GRANT THAT NAMES NOTHING: every other handler here is named for the message it posts and this one posts none, so hud_slot_bbc4's meaning is still open. What it does close is that slot's own plate, which used to credit the address to code no batch had recovered: $105e6 is recovered.
fn 0x10600 pickup_effect_grant_wing_boots
cmt 0x10600 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/effects.c, batch 38), renamed off the slot-address suffix it used to carry. hud_slot_bbc2 := $feff, then text_request $52 -- message id 82, "    Wing Boots." -- so this handler IDENTIFIES hud_slot_bbc2, by the method batch 17 used for the helmet and the gauntlet (the message a slot's own writer posts). The slot's wonderboy.h name is not changed with it; ../recreate/STATUS.md queues that rename.

fn 0x1064e pickup_effect_grant_revival

fn 0x10668 pickup_effect_grant_fire_balls
cmt 0x10668 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/effects.c, batch 38), renamed off the record code it used to be numbered for. `addq.l #2,effect_record_write_ptr / movea.l it,a1 / move.w #$0605,(a1)` then text_request $5e -- message id 94, "   Fire Balls." -- appending one 16-bit code to effect_record_list. The write_ptr is bumped BEFORE the store, so the list's first slot is never written; that slot is $b444, whose first byte $10746 uses as the attack level, and the two facts fit. THE FOUR APPENDS HERE PUSH THE SAME FOUR WORDS effect_push_record_0605/0508/0705/0803 push from effect_handler_table, so the two dispatch tables grant the same four items and the list is an inventory of them. Each word's HIGH byte is distinct across the four ($06/$05/$07/$08) and its LOW byte is not ($05/$08/$05/$03), which is as far as the evidence for "{item, count}" goes.

fn 0x106f0 pickup_effect_refill_meter
cmt 0x106f0 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/effects.c, batch 38). panel_frame_delay := $0500 and hud_meter_value := hud_meter_max -- a full refill, no clamp needed, so it fills the meter even from ABOVE the maximum. text_request 0, which CANCELS the bonus-points box the score arm may have posted.
fn 0x10714 pickup_effect_add4_meter
cmt 0x10714 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/effects.c, batch 38). panel_frame_delay := $0500, then hud_meter_value += 4 -- but NOT the same as effect_add4_clamped_b6fa ($10296). Both compute `hud_meter_value + 4` and `bgt hud_meter_max`; $10296 then CLAMPS (`move.w hud_meter_max,hud_meter_value`), this one just SKIPS the store, so a meter within 3 of the maximum stays where it is instead of topping up. The compare is the SIGNED 16-bit one of its sibling and the add wraps in sixteen bits, so a meter near $7fff comes out negative and stores. text_request 0.
fn 0x10746 pickup_effect_bump_attack_level
cmt 0x10746 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/effects.c, batch 38). `cmpi.b #3,$b444 / bgt $10768` then `addq.b #1,$b444` and text_request $63 (message id 99, " Offensive Power \n    Increased.   "), and UNCONDITIONALLY scene_exit_request := $ffff at $10768 -- the writer the scene_exit_request plate already names, and it runs on the REFUSED arm too because it sits below the join. The compare is SIGNED, so the $ff the new-game reset leaves in this byte (the high half of effect_record_list's $ffff "empty" word) is negative and bumps -- turning that word into $00ff, which $b39c's `tst.w / bpl` no longer reads as empty. The $b444 tension the batch-17 addendum records is therefore not a naming error but the shipped overlap of two fields at one address, and this port reproduces it.
fn 0x10772 pickup_effect_vanish_followed
cmt 0x10772 RECONSTRUCTED (recreate/src/effects.c, batch 38), renamed off the bare "sets a flag" reading it used to carry -- the write set names the effect. `jsr followed_actor_record` (encoded `4eb8 67e0`, abs.SHORT -- the operand-hiding form batch 31's hidden caller used), then on the returned record 21(a1) := $ff, bit 6 of 8(a1) and bit 4 of 9(a1) set; text_request $64, message id 100, "    Vanished !   ". Those three writes are $69fe's own damage-flicker state at its maximum: actor_flicker_countdown full, the flicker bit that makes the projection publish no sprite, and the invulnerable bit that makes $69fe return without writing anything at all. So the pickup makes the player invisible and untouchable for 255 frames, which is what the message says. AND IT ESTABLISHES A READER FOR OFFSET 21: $f14 ticks the byte down under `btst #6,8(a0)`, and this is the one writer that raises that bit beside it -- behaviour slot 23's $64 into the same offset does not, so whether ITS write is ever ticked depends on the record's flicker bit having been raised elsewhere. The last handler, and the byte past its `rts` is scene_exit_request.
